## Recurring failure modes (instances of §3–§5, kept for pattern-matching)

The citation-extraction boundary named below is bounded at mechanism grain, not unknown: code inspection finds six non-fixture helpers that mint `DeclarationRef` from caller-supplied module and declaration strings (`wet_surface_ref`, `roster_decl`, `target_decl`, `principle_ref`, `family_subject`, `coverage_subject`). Their calls are not members of the parser-visible 31 and are not silently counted as citation targets: an argument may be computed, so a textual call count is not an unchecked-citation population. Whether the cited-symbol wall reaches those wrappers through another path is unmeasured here; establishing that separate question requires a planted wrapper call and execution, not an inference from this extractor read.

The landing measurement partitions the 31 parser-visible identities into **2 cited-and-syntactically-called** (`gunbc.auth.credentials`, `gunbc.auth.optional_impersonation`) and **29 cited-not-syntactically-called**. It independently partitions them by compile disposition: **25 live under `dag/`, the first and therefore entry-producing root of the existing `--source-root dag --source-root src/v2` corpus compile; 6 live under `src/v2/`, which that invocation indexes only as a dependency pool.** Because this population has no inbound import edges, those six pool-only identities are structurally unreachable by that operation. The call split is evidence, not semantic resolution: indirection may hide a call and an equal bare callee spelling may bind elsewhere. It separates an under-declaration candidate present only in the called arm from the compile-coverage deficit present in both arms. PR #9453 is the worked construction-side repair for one called specimen: the missing import was a real dependency and incidentally makes that citee enter a consumer closure. It does not repair the shared coverage class. The existing compile mechanism covers the 25 `dag/` identities by collecting every `.dag` file under its first source root as an entry regardless of inbound edges; coverage for the six `src/v2/` pool-only identities remains open. The current whole compile-route cost is UNMEASURED: the available 7 GiB figure is a rounded-up, stale lower-bound proxy from the former compile-clean floor route, not a demand measurement, and differencing it from a budget would fabricate a shortfall. The existing carrier names the re-measure trigger: a dated uncensored whole-tree peak on the compile route itself. No citation-derived edge is proposed for the not-called arm: it would manufacture reachability from the claim being checked and make the check green by construction. **THE RED EVIDENCE NO LONGER DEPENDS ON THE LIVE CREDENTIALS DEFECT.** The live 87.712s refusal remains a dated mechanism measurement, but `orphan_entry_compile_has_a_controlled_red_and_clean_control` now compiles two in-memory, caller-authored orphan entries: one undefined value must produce `CompileRefused`, and the clean peer must produce `CompileCompleted`. Repairing or importing any live retained module cannot green that discriminating pair.

**THE THIRD EMIT-STAGE ESCAPE MODE NOW HAS A MEASURED POPULATION (2026-08-27), AND THE NUMBER IS 31, NOT THE 39 A TEXT SCAN SUGGESTED.** `v1_compiler.declaration_index` already derives each module's imports and typed-literal `DeclarationRef` citations from the parse sweep. Its one ingestion-derived join now reports every distinct non-fixture module cited by a different non-fixture module and reached by no import edge, with all 31 RETAINED IDENTITIES beside the count on the required parse phase. Fixture citers and targets are evidence carriers, not production authority demand; self-citation creates no consumer; both are excluded by construction. The earlier regex figure 39 was an instrument result, never an oracle: on the tree measured here its comparable scan returned 38, including six `module_path` fields that are not `DeclarationRef`s at all, while one real citation routed through the local `wet_surface_ref` wrapper lies outside the index's declared extraction boundary. Neither discrepancy is smoothed away. This bounds mode three at the exact grain the parser-backed instrument can establish; it does not bound mode one, all emit-stage diagnostics, or every possible citation spelling. **THE DISCRIMINATING EXECUTION EXISTS AND THE REQUIRED PHASE DOES NOT.** Compiling the retained `gunbc.auth.credentials` entry refused on its four known hard diagnostics in 87.712s; compiling the clean orphan control `extdeps.vendor.arm` passed in 85.119s. That pair proves entry compilation detects the class without making every legitimate orphan red. It also refuses enrollment of a serial 31-entry phase: these subjects cost about 85s each, with 21–24s (about 27%) in reconcile, not the neighbouring emit-compile row's 40–50s and ~85% reconcile over a different roster. Both measurements stand; neither is generalized across the other population. At this cost shape a whole-population serial wall is outside the admitted runner envelope, so mode three remains unprevented while its exact parser-visible remainder is now bounded and named. The construction-side candidate is citation-derived dependency edges, which would make a cited authority enter its citer's closure instead of adding a second compile; it is NOT claimed here because qualified references already prove import closure under-approximates dependency, the index deliberately does not resolve every wrapper-produced `DeclarationRef`, and the closure-growth and historical/quarantined-citation semantics have not been bounded. Its next decision requires those measurements, not a second edge authority improvised in the census.

- **A CARRIER DECLARED AS AN ALGEBRAIC STRUCTURE THAT LACKS AN OPERATION, WHILE THE OPERATION REMAINS WRITABLE ON IT AND ANSWERS OUTSIDE THE STRUCTURE'S CARRIER SET** (INVALID STATE: std.nat declares `Nat = CommutativeSemiring<Magnitude>`. A semiring is precisely a ring WITHOUT additive inverses -- the absence of subtraction is not an omission from the declaration, it IS the declaration. Yet `a - b` for `b > a` is writable on two Nats, typechecks, and evaluates to a NEGATIVE value which is then carried in Nat-typed positions. HARM: the type name is read by every author as the guarantee it names, so nonnegativity is assumed at exactly the sites that never check it. The value does not trap, does not saturate, and does not refuse -- it flows onward as an ordinary number, so the first observable consequence is arbitrarily far from the subtraction, and a comparison against a lower bound silently answers for a quantity that cannot exist.RECEIPT, EXECUTED, WITH A CONTROL THAT STAYS FALSE. A probe module declaring `fn probe_underflow() -> Nat { 0 - 96 }` alongside `fn probe_control() -> Nat { 196 - 96 }` was run through the seed: `probe_underflow() < 0` returned `true` and `probe_control() < 0` returned `false`. The pair is what makes it evidence rather than a reading -- the same expression shape, the same return type, the same comparison, differing only in whether the subtraction has an answer in the carrier set.THE MECHANISM, TRACED THROUGH THE RESOLVER RATHER THAN INFERRED FROM A NEIGHBOURING MODULE'S PROSE. v1_compiler_infer_types.binop_algebra_fields maps each operator to the algebra field it resolves against, and the row for subtraction is `BinOp::Sub => AlgAdd` -- THE SAME FIELD ADDITION RESOLVES AGAINST. infer_binop_type_node then finds that field on the carrier and adopts its RETURN TYPE as the result type, so `Nat.add : (Nat, Nat) -> Nat` is precisely what makes `a - b` type as Nat. Subtraction is admitted BY THE PRESENCE OF ADDITION, and the semiring's missing additive inverse is never consulted because nothing on this path looks for one. AlgebraFieldKind has no AlgSub and no AlgNegate at all.THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS ROW ATTRIBUTED THE CAUSE TO THE COERCION REGISTRY -- that std.coercion registers Int -> Nat as an identity, so minus dispatches as Int subtraction -- AND THAT WAS WRONG. It is retained here because the error is the more instructive half. The coercion identity is real and was quoted accurately from std.checked_arithmetic; it simply is not on this path, and the operator admission already has enough to make the wrong decision before that explanation is needed. The attribution was taken from an ADJACENT MODULE'S PROSE ABOUT A CAST TABLE while the executed evidence established only the EFFECT, and a mechanism read off a nearby comment is not a traced mechanism. The consequence was not cosmetic: it made the next-rung trigger name a repair to the coercion registry, which would have changed nothing here, so the row would have been RETIRED BY WORK THAT LEFT THE CLASS FULLY ALIVE. A wrong cause produces a wrong trigger, and a wrong trigger is worse than an absent one because it will eventually be satisfied.THE CLASS IS THEREFORE MUCH WIDER THAN Nat, which the traced mechanism makes plain and the coercion story concealed. Because addition and subtraction share ONE resolution key, NO CARRIER ANYWHERE CAN ADMIT ADDITION WHILE REFUSING SUBTRACTION. Every additive structure without inverses is affected identically -- every semiring, every monoid, every quantity carrier whose declared algebra stops short of a group. Nat is the instance that happened to be probed, not the extent of the defect, and a repair scoped to Nat would leave the shape intact everywhere else.THE NEIGHBOURING ASYMMETRY IS STILL EVIDENCE, NOW FOR A DIFFERENT POINT. std.checked_arithmetic carries CheckedNat with checked_int_magnitude, guarding the INT-TO-NAT crossing so that no input maps to a fabricated zero. That direction was modeled because its crossing is VISIBLE AT A COERCION, where an author has to write something down. Nat-minus-Nat has no such site: there is no nat_sub, no checked difference, no typed carrier for a difference that may not exist -- because the operator resolves silently and presents no place to put the guard. The modeled direction is the one that made an author stop; the open one is the one that never did.RECOGNITION RULE, RESTATED STRUCTURALLY BECAUSE THE FIRST VERSION NEEDED VALUE REASONING AND THIS ONE DOES NOT. The original rule asked whether a subtraction's left operand was provably at least its right -- a question about VALUES, decidable only per site and frequently not at all. The traced mechanism supplies a rule about DECLARATIONS instead: AN OPERATOR IS ADMITTED ON A CARRIER THROUGH AN ALGEBRA CAPABILITY THAT DOES NOT ENTAIL THAT OPERATOR, allowing the operation to produce a value outside the carrier's declared structure. The specimen instantiates it exactly -- operator Sub, admitted via AlgAdd, carrier declared CommutativeSemiring, the capability actually missing being the additive inverse, the observed result a negative value inhabiting a Nat position. Both operands of the rule are declarations already in hand, so it finds the CLASS rather than one instance of it.HOW IT WAS FOUND, RECORDED BECAUSE THE ROUTE MATTERS MORE THAN THE INSTANCE: it was NOT found by an audit of arithmetic. A reviewer asked, of ONE test fixture, whether a false verdict arrived by saturation or by refusal -- and the answer was neither. The fixture, `mark_feature_above_the_body`, held `center_y: 0, radius: 96`, so its containment witness computed `0 - 96` and reported `not contained` because a negative is below the body's lower bound. The verdict was correct and its intermediate was meaningless: the witness would have kept passing had the containment rule been rewritten, and would have flipped had the subtraction ever begun to refuse. A green test resting on an underflow is the class's characteristic disguise, because the underflow pushes the value AWAY from the bound the assertion tests.TWO NEARBY ROWS THAT ARE NOT THIS ONE. This is not the absorbing fallback of section 5: nothing here widens or substitutes a superset on failure -- the operation does not know it failed. Nor is it plain integer overflow, which std.checked_arithmetic models at the realization bound with a typed outcome; that class is a FINITE-MACHINE fact about i64, whereas this one holds in the mathematical model too, since the difference genuinely has no answer among the naturals. Conflating them would send the repair to the bound rather than to the carrier.RUNG FOUND AT: below the ladder. This is not a weak wall, it is silent wrongness -- section 4b places that outside the ladder and forbids it outright, and the reported rung must be the one executed evidence establishes rather than the one the type name suggests. CEILING: structurally impossible. Which algebra capability an operator requires is decidable from the operator alone, and a carrier that does not declare that capability has no way to express the operation. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, A CAPABILITY, STATED AGAINST THE TRACED MECHANISM RATHER THAN THE ONE THIS ROW FIRST GUESSED: operator admission able to DISTINGUISH subtraction from addition -- an additive-inverse AlgebraFieldKind that Ring and OrderedRing declare and CommutativeSemiring does not -- SUFFICIENT FOR a subtraction over a semiring-declared carrier to be REFUSED AT INFERENCE with a located diagnostic rather than typed from the add field's return type. That row in binop_algebra_fields is the repair surface, and it closes the class for every additive carrier at once rather than for Nat alone. A CHECKED DIFFERENCE IS NECESSARY BUT IS NOT THE WALL, and the ordering matters: adding a typed nat difference -- DifferenceReady { value } | DifferenceUnderflow { lhs, rhs } -- while bare Nat minus Nat stays legal creates an OPTIONAL SAFER ROUTE rather than a wall, so the refusal must land with or before it and the legitimate existing sites migrate onto it. WHAT THIS TRIGGER DELIBERATELY DOES NOT REQUIRE is any change to the Int -> Nat coercion identity: that seam is sound and load-bearing for construction-side grounding, and explicitly widening two Nats to subtract them AS INTEGERS must stay expressible, its result being an Int where -96 is a perfectly good answer. The wall is narrower than the first draft implied -- Nat minus Nat returning Nat must cease to be a TOTAL operation.
- **censored estimator drops its own tail** (an estimate of a variable is computed over the observations that SURVIVED a threshold on that same variable, so the sample structurally excludes its own extreme and the estimate is biased low with no bound. It is the neighbour of `instrument_output_read_as_subject_content` moved one step earlier: there a REPORT is complete for the reporter and read as complete for the consumer; here the truncation is in the ESTIMATOR'S OWN DOMAIN, and no amount of reading the instrument's output correctly recovers what the filter removed. SPECIMEN, both directions, 2026-09-01: the required floor's per-run cost inflation was estimated by pairing every claim that reported a cost in BOTH attempts of one tree -- median 1.053, p90 1.107. A claim that exceeds the cpu ceiling goes INTERRUPTED-BEFORE-VERDICT AND REPORTS NO COST, so the pairing dropped exactly the two most inflated observations; recovered from the ceiling itself, their inflation was at least 500/418 = 1.196, censored from above. THE SECOND HALF IS WHY THE ROW EXISTS: a peer lane, correcting a DIFFERENT defect in the same measurement, took the biased p90 by relay and derived an at-risk population of ONE -- an understatement arriving with the credibility of a retraction, which is the artifact nobody re-audits. Both counts were lower bounds and neither was labelled as one. RECOGNITION RULE: whenever an estimate is computed over rows that COMPLETED, RETURNED, PASSED, or were OBSERVED, ask what the incomplete rows would have contributed -- and if the incompleteness is caused by the very variable being estimated, the statistic is a floor and must be reported as one. The tell is a filter and an estimand naming the same quantity: cost estimated over rows that finished, latency over requests that did not time out, size over responses that were not truncated. REMEDY: report the censored bound rather than the sample statistic, and recover the excluded observations from the THRESHOLD they crossed, which is a real datum -- a row killed at 500ms is not missing, it is known to be above 500.)
- **a selection view read as the population** (a decidable predicate is written over MEASURED values -- cost at or above N, latency under N, rows a report printed -- and its output is then treated as the population of the class under study. Membership in that set is a property of THE MEASUREMENT, not of the subject: it moves when the instrument moves, when load moves, when the reporter truncates, while the subject is unchanged. The set and the population are two objects joined by an assumption nobody states, and the assumption is false exactly when the measured quantity carries an unbounded term the threshold cannot see beneath. **WHAT MAKES THIS DIFFERENT FROM ITS NEIGHBOURS IS THE ROUTE IT ARRIVES BY, AND THAT IS ALSO THE WHOLE DIFFICULTY: IT ARRIVES AS THE FIX FOR A REAL OBJECTION.** `instrument_output_read_as_subject_content` is a report read past its own promise, and its closest unnamed relative here is a statistic computed over the observations that SURVIVED a threshold on the very variable being estimated -- filed separately as `censored_estimator_drops_its_own_tail`, and cited here by identity because that row now resolves in this tree; it was carried descriptively while it was unlanded, since a boundary drawn against a symbol that does not resolve is worse than a boundary drawn descriptively. Both of those are errors in the READING. This one is produced BY diligence. The predicate is decidable, it is honestly derived, and it is very often the correct answer to a legitimate refusal -- which is precisely why the next question does not get asked. **BEING CAREFUL IS WHAT MAKES IT INVISIBLE: the diligence is spent on the repair, and the repair's correctness is what stops anyone asking whether it answers the question that was posed.** A defect arriving inside a correct fix is not caught by the diligence that produced the fix. SPECIMEN, WITH BOTH ENDS BELONGING TO THE SAME AUTHOR THREE HOURS APART, which is why it is filed rather than blamed. (i) A peer lane computed a median and p95 over the rows a required-floor run's `[over-cost]` listing PRINTED -- an instrument that ranks by cost and truncates to a fixed head, and whose own closing line says so and names the per-claim cost artifact the run uploads as the complete population. This author correctly told them an order statistic of a truncated tail describes nothing, because the truncation is a property of the REPORTER. THE TWO PRODUCERS ARE NAMED RATHER THAN THEIR OUTPUTS TRANSCRIBED, per DESIGN section 6: the truncated view is that `[over-cost]` listing, and the population is `required_floor_claim_cost.tsv` as uploaded by the same run -- either is re-derivable from any floor run, and no count here needs to be trusted. (ii) The same author then had a declared drop refused for defining its population as `every required row whose headroom is smaller than the observed inflation` while calling that inflation censored with no upper bound -- an undecidable membership predicate, correctly refused under DESIGN section 4b(3). The fix was a decidable admission rule over each run's own per-claim cost artifact, which was the right repair for the refusal, and its output was then written into the row AS THE POPULATION. With an unbounded contention term, no lower threshold can prove the rows beneath it unaffected, so the set was a view the whole time. REPAIR: name the closed subject universe from the SUBJECT rather than from any measurement -- for the specimen, every required identity for which the cpu deadline is armed while it executes under the uncontrolled shared-runner envelope -- and keep the threshold set, which is genuinely useful, under an honest name: an EXPOSED ATTENTION SUBSET for prioritising work. A COROLLARY WORTH CARRYING SEPARATELY, because conflating the two hides the seam: the evidence floor behind such a threshold may be MONOTONE while the membership set is NOT. An observed floor only rises, so a constant derived from it only falls; individual identities still enter and leave the subset as their measured values vary. Monotonicity of the bound says nothing about stability of the set. **RECOGNITION RULE: when a set is produced by comparing a MEASURED value against a threshold, ask what the set would contain if the measurement were taken again under different conditions -- and ask it hardest when the predicate was just written to satisfy a reviewer, because that is the moment the answer feels settled.**)THE INHERITED-VIEW FORM: when a consumer takes its population from a structure someone else produced, ask whether that structure is the POPULATION or a SELECTION over it -- and treat a name like `references` or `declarations` beside an `entries` as the tell, since a filtered view and its source are usually siblings in one carrier.** THE REPAIR IS NOT TO SWITCH VIEWS: a consumer must declare its OWN total eligibility disposition over the source population, every member either in the subject or excluded by a NAMED cause, because silence from a view someone else built is exactly how a family disappears with nobody deciding it should -- and that holds even where the two agree today. RUNG FOUND AT: below the ladder, since the census reported a complete-looking population. EXECUTED EVIDENCE: the calibration witness's perturbation control -- deleting every appearance of one variant moves the observed count by zero, with both counts nonzero -- which is what makes the absence a control rather than an observation.THE CAPABILITY-PROBE FORM, which is the same family reached from acquisition and execution rather than from a measured threshold: A PROBE ANSWERS ABOUT THE CONTEXT IT WAS RUN IN, AND ITS ANSWER GETS PROMOTED TO THE CAPABILITY. Two receipts, one author, hours apart, each costing an operator a request for privileged access that was never needed. (i) `apt-get install` needs root, which no session container has; that was read as `this environment cannot acquire software`, an escalation was filed asking which host should receive an installation, and the missing dependency was meanwhile sideloaded out of .deb archives. conda-forge resolved the same libraries into a user-owned prefix with no privilege at all. (ii) `docker version` reported no reachable daemon; that was read as `there is no container execution route`, and a base-image change plus a privileged package install were escalated. `docker context ls` listed a reachable remote context the whole time, and the workload ran there unchanged. THE STRUCTURE IS THE SAME BOTH TIMES: the probe names ONE package manager, or ONE docker context, and the conclusion names the ENVIRONMENT. What makes it hard to see is that the probe's answer is TRUE -- apt really does need root, that daemon really is unreachable -- so there is nothing wrong to notice at the point of reading; the defect is that the question asked about a member and the answer was recorded about the set. **RECOGNITION RULE: before writing that a capability is unavailable HERE, name which selector you actually queried, and ask what the sibling selectors are -- other package managers, other contexts, other hosts, other transports. A capability is a relation (client x selector x target), never a boolean about the current shell, and an escalation asking a human for privilege is the moment to spend one command enumerating the selectors instead.** The two forms are worth carrying under one identity because the repair is identical: state the population from the SUBJECT -- every acquisition route, every execution context -- rather than from whichever member was probed first.
- **a citation to a symbol that has not landed** (a canonical row cites an identity that exists only in an OPEN PR, so on main -- the tree the row lands in -- the citation resolves to nothing. **THE MECHANISM IS THAT THE TWO STATES ARE INDISTINGUISHABLE AT THE CITING END.** A name that resolves and a name that will resolve are the same text in the same position; nothing at the point of authorship separates them, so this is not carelessness and reading more carefully does not find it. The check that discriminates is a LOOKUP AGAINST MAIN; the check an author naturally performs is a lookup against their own context, in which the symbol is vividly present because they or a peer just wrote it. Both receipts below were caught by CI and neither by review, which is what that asymmetry predicts. **A VOCABULARY DEFECT CARRIES IT BETWEEN LANES, AND THAT HALF IS WHAT MAKES THE CLASS PREVENTABLE RATHER THAN MERELY DETECTABLE.** One receipt arrived through a MESSAGE saying a class was `filed`. That word is true the moment a row is authored in a branch and is heard as `the tree carries it`; the two states collapse into one utterance. So the rule has a speech half: never say FILED without saying filed WHERE -- `authored in gunbc#NNNN, open` or `landed on main` -- because a reader cannot recover the distinction the word destroyed. TWO INDEPENDENT RECEIPTS, ONE HOUR APART, DIFFERENT LANES AND DIFFERENT CARRIERS, EACH CAUGHT BY CI. (i) A recurring-failure-mode row drew its load-bearing boundary against a sibling class that lived in the author's own other open PR. (ii) A proof field in a separate lane cited a class living in a third session's open PR, reached through the `filed` message above. Both authors repaired it the same way without knowing of each other: DESCRIBE THE SIBLING BY SHAPE RATHER THAN NAMING IT. THE RULE, and it is stronger than it first sounds: A CANONICAL ROW MAY CITE ONLY WHAT RESOLVES ON MAIN AT THE MOMENT THE ROW LANDS. Not `resolves eventually`. **That formulation FORBIDS STACKING the citing PR behind the cited one**, which is the tempting repair precisely because it feels like it fixes the citation -- it satisfies resolves-eventually and fails resolves-at-landing, so it defers the defect rather than removing it. THREE ADMISSIBLE FORMS, AND THE RANKING BETWEEN THE FIRST TWO IS CONDITIONAL ON WHETHER THE CITED IDENTITY IS STABLE -- an unconditional ranking was proposed, and withdrawn by its own author on the argument below. WHERE THE CITED IDENTITY IS STABLE, name the PR AND ITS OPENNESS -- `authored as <identity> in gunbc#NNNN, which is open at this writing, so this points at the PR carrying the row rather than asserting an identity that does not yet resolve`. It stays TRUE after the cited PR lands and TIGHTENS to the bare identity rather than needing correction. **WHERE THE CITED IDENTITY IS NOT STABLE, BOUNDARY BY DESCRIPTION RANKS FIRST INSTEAD, AND AN IDENTITY IN AN OPEN PR UNDER ACTIVE REVIEW IS PRECISELY THE UNSTABLE CASE.** If the cited PR RENAMES its row before landing -- under review pressure, or because two lanes converge and merge their classes -- the PR-naming form points at a real PR carrying a row that no longer has that name, AND IT FAILS WHILE STILL LOOKING RESOLVABLE: the PR number resolves, the identity does not, and the sentence reads as correct. A description survives that, because it does not name the thing that changed. So: PR-plus-openness when the identity is settled, description when it is in motion. BOUNDARY BY DESCRIPTION -- name the sibling's shape and not its symbol -- is otherwise second: correct, but it must be REWRITTEN once the sibling lands, and a rewrite is where a citation quietly stops matching the row it describes. Never: a bare identity that does not resolve on main today, which is worse than no citation at all because a reader takes the name as established and stops checking. RUNG, CEILING AND TRIGGER, per DESIGN section 4b. RUNG FOUND AT: MITIGATABLE. The repository already runs a required cited-symbol resolution check -- its rung drop `cited_symbol_census` is RETIRED, so the capability is live -- and it did not fire on either receipt, because BOTH CITATIONS WERE PROSE INSIDE AN `authored: String`, not typed references. A prose name is not reachable from the namespace tree the check walks, so the check was not weak here; it was not applicable. What caught both was a review bot reading the text, which is containment after the fact and not prevention. ATTAINABLE CEILING, AND IT IS NOT STRUCTURAL WHILE THE CITATION STAYS PROSE: deciding whether an identity-shaped token inside a paragraph is a CITATION or merely a mention is not decidable, so a scanner over prose would be a heuristic, and DESIGN section 5 forbids dressing one as a wall. This class therefore sits in the *outside the modeled guarantee* column for as long as row-to-row citation is prose -- observed and repaired by reading, never gated -- which is the same standing `unbacked_execution_claim` records for the same reason. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, A CAPABILITY AND NOT AN ARTIFACT: A TYPED ROW-TO-ROW CITATION CARRIER -- the ability to express `this row's boundary is drawn against THAT row` as a reference the namespace authority resolves, rather than as a name inside a paragraph. The moment such a citation is typed, the EXISTING required check covers it and the class climbs to structurally guaranteed for every typed citation in one step, with no new gate to build. `RecurringFailureMode` carries an `evidence: List<DeclarationRef>` field, which is the nearest existing carrier and is NOT that capability: it records declarations that witness the class, not the class's boundary against a sibling, and pressing it into service would be a second meaning for one field. WHY THIS ROW'S OWN `evidence` IS EMPTY, stated rather than left to look like an omission: both receipts are PULL REQUESTS AND REVIEW ARTIFACTS, not declarations, so there is no `DeclarationRef` to carry them and the field is honestly empty rather than padded. THE RECEIPTS AT CHECKABLE GRAIN: (i) gunbc#9946, review 58237 -- the boundary clause of `selection_view_read_as_population` named `censored_estimator_drops_its_own_tail`, which at that moment lived only in the same author's other open PR, gunbc#9932. BOTH IDENTITIES RESOLVE IN THIS TREE NOW, which is why they are named here rather than described: the receipt is that they did NOT resolve when the citation was written, and the repair the citing row carries is the descriptive-then-tightened form this row ranks second; (ii) gunbc#9949, review 58260 -- a proof field naming a class living in a third session's open PR, gunbc#9939, reached through the `filed` message. Both reviews are codex, both on the first pushed head of their PR, and each names the unresolvable identity explicitly. **THE TWO RECEIPTS ARE CARRIED IN TWO DIFFERENT ADMISSIBLE FORMS, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE: the first names identities because its two subjects have since landed, the second names a PR and its openness because gunbc#9939 has not. A reader who sees the ranking stated learns the conclusion; a reader who sees one paragraph use both forms because its two cited PRs are in different states learns the DISCRIMINATOR.** A COROLLARY THAT THIS ROW ITSELF DEMONSTRATES, and it is the reason the first receipt does not read as two bare names: WHEN A CITATION TIGHTENS FROM DESCRIPTION TO IDENTITY, THE RECEIPT USUALLY LIVES IN THE STATE THAT JUST ENDED, so tightening SILENTLY DESTROYS IT. This row's first receipt is the historical NON-RESOLUTION of those two names, not the names; tightened to identities alone it would show a reader two symbols that resolve and no evidence the class ever occurred -- a row about unresolvable citations, evidenced by resolvable ones. So a tightening must carry the ended state as a stated fact beside the new name, never merely replace it. THE CAUSE IS A SEPARATE SHAPE AND IS DELIBERATELY NOT FOLDED IN HERE: a correct decomposition -- splitting two classes into two PRs because a rider in a large PR gets skimmed rather than read -- is often what SEVERS the citation path between the pieces. That is a cause held at one receipt and not yet filed; this row is about why the severance is invisible from the citing end, which is a different question with a different repair. If both are ever filed, the causal link is named rather than merged.)
- hollow alias (minimality ≠ grounding)
- state-space conflation (an `Option`/`None` meaning >2 things — split into named variants; its most-repeated form here is **not-applicable rendered as malformed** — one reason symbol over "the input is wrong" and "this strategy had nothing to say about it", which have opposite owners and opposite repairs. Found three times in three stages by one lane (#8801 and #8828 in body lowering, `parse_g0_tokens_remain` in parse), always by reading the producer and never from the message. Recognition rule: if the arm sits downstream of a search, lookup or alternative that returned `Absent`, it is not-applicable and needs its own reason. A SECOND FORM, which does not match that shape and is the same class: **a dichotomy stated over a domain with three states.** Specimen (gunbc#9324): the `floor_resource_sample` comment documented `pswpin` rising with `pgmajfault` as swap and `pgmajfault` rising with `pswpin` flat as mapping churn — two arms over three states, since BOTH FLAT is quiet. Churn is a defect this lane owns and quiet is the absence of one, so the missing arm inverts the verdict. The mechanism of the misread is what the recognition rule keys on: `pgmajfault rises` and `pswpin flat` are two conditions and only their CONJUNCTION is churn, so a reader matching on the cheaper condition alone selects churn for a state that satisfies `pswpin flat` and nothing else — which zero-and-zero does. **Recognition rule for this form: for every arm of a stated dichotomy, enumerate the domain and check that each arm's conditions are required jointly.** What made it invisible rather than merely wrong is that BOTH readers landed on the same arm — 26 intervals so classified by one and 6 by another, neither having compared notes — and agreement reads as confirmation. THAT IS A PROMPT TO RE-DERIVE, NOT A DIAGNOSTIC, and the distinction is load-bearing: convergent readings are equally produced by shared assumptions, a common heuristic, ambiguity in the subject, or one reader having anchored on the other, and n=2 on one specimen cannot separate those from a defect in the rule. What survives is only the weaker direction — agreement between readers of one rule is not independent evidence about that rule, because the shared input is a shared potential defect, so it licenses re-deriving from the domain and never a conclusion about which cause produced it.) **A THIRD FORM, and the one that is hardest to rank because nothing is wrong today: A STATE THAT IS ALREADY LOAD-BEARING AND CARRIED AS AN AD-HOC SPELLING RATHER THAN A CONSTRUCTOR.** Specimen (2026-09-01): the identity key threaded through v1.compiler.coercion has THREE inhabitants -- a real declaring module path, the synthetic <kernel:NAME> that v1.std.core kernel_span mints for kernel nodes, and the empty string meaning unknown -- while every consumer discriminates only empty from non-empty. lookup_checkpoint and type_realization_decision therefore read <kernel:String> as a KNOWN declaration and proceed to the spelling-keyed arm; decl_file_declares_structurally compares that synthetic identity against a roster of real paths with contains, which no <kernel:NAME> can ever match, so the structural gate is unreachable for a kernel-resolved reference BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by decision. The key reaches those gates with exactly that value through type_reference_decl_file, from v1.compiler.emit coerce_primitive_type and v1.compiler.emit_rust rust_named_type_base and rust_applied_type_base. **What makes this the third form rather than an instance of the first two is the evidence that the state is REAL: the tree already handles it, three times, by string prefix.** numeric_realization_declaring_modules carries the literal "<kernel:" as a row in a roster of MODULE PATHS; is_kernel_minted_file is a substring predicate over the same spelling; and resolved_node_is_kernel_identity_for_name owns the same fact by EXACT EQUALITY, its own note recording that a substring or prefix test is a second, weaker authority for it. THE CITED MODULE WAS WRONG AND IS NOW ALSO MOVED: no module v1.compiler.env exists; the declaration lived in v1.compiler.infer_env and was relocated (XL-0B, 2026-09-04) into v1.std.core, beside the kernel_span whose output it compares against, because v1.std.core declaration_provenance_of could not import it without closing a cycle and so re-derived the same comparison in place -- a fourth recogniser this row had not counted. Cite it at v1.std.core. The relocated body asks kernel_span; the one it replaced inlined the format literally. **Recognition rule for this form: when a value's domain is carried in a type that cannot express one of its inhabitants, look for the inhabitant being recognised by SPELLING somewhere -- a prefix test, a magic string in a roster of real values, a sentinel. Each such site is a consumer that already knows the state exists, and their number is the count of authorities the missing constructor has spawned.** NO WRONG ANSWER IS CLAIMED FOR THE SPECIMEN AND NONE WAS MEASURED: for the kernel names reaching those gates today the spelling-keyed answer is the correct one, which is exactly why it never surfaced -- the class is filed on the state space, not on an output, and a form that produces no incident is the one that will be found last. **CLIMB RECEIPT (2026-09-02), appended rather than replacing the filing above, because DESIGN section 4b(4) keeps a class's discriminating evidence enrolled AFTER the climb rather than dissolving it with the production machinery.** The missing constructor now exists: std.coercion TypeDeclarationProvenance is CorpusDeclared | KernelMinted | DeclarationIdentityAbsent, minted by v1.std.core declaration_provenance_of, which decides the kernel arm by EXACT EQUALITY against kernel_span -- the minter itself -- rather than by a prefix test over its output. All four spelling authorities this row counted are gone: the literal "<kernel:" row is deleted from numeric_realization_declaring_modules, is_kernel_minted_file is deleted, and the two gates that substring-compared a synthetic identity against real paths now take the coproduct, so the structural gate's unreachability for a kernel-resolved reference stops being BY CONSTRUCTION and becomes BY DECISION -- a named arm answering false. **WHAT IS NOT CLAIMED, because this row's own honesty is the thing being climbed:** no wrong answer was found before and none is fixed now; every answer is preserved bit for bit, which is why the climb is in the STATE SPACE and not in an output. The empty-string inhabitant is likewise gone from the channel, and the bare-table answer it used to reach silently is now the named ground GroundedByUnidentifiedBareRow, so a declared rung drop that was previously indistinguishable from a properly-keyed answer is countable and refusable. **THE RECOGNITION RULE IS CORROBORATED AND THE CORROBORATION IS INDEPENDENT.** gunbc.type_reference_decl_file_occurrence_census reached this same four-authority set from the opposite direction -- that census asked what each of thirty-nine occurrences NEEDS, this row asked what the identity key can HOLD -- and the counts agreed. **WHAT SURVIVES UNCLIMBED, stated so the row is not read as closed:** CorpusDeclared still carries a file path matched by contains, so the surviving arm answers WHAT A DECLARATION REALIZES AS positionally. v1.compiler.infer_env global_bare_fallback_invariant already states the terminal key for it -- filepaths are irrelevant, the declared module path is the containment tree -- and re-keying that arm needs a declaration reference at each gate, which is a separate change.

**A FOURTH FORM, MEASURED 2026-09-02 (quick-crab-396), AND IT SITS IN THE INTERPRETER RATHER THAN IN ANY MODEL: DECLARED OPTIONAL AND CONSTRUCTED ABSENT ARE TWO STATES WEARING ONE SPELLING.** A record field declared `T?` is read by authors -- and by this document's own §2 reasoning about optional carriers -- as optional-with-a-none-default. It is not. A literal that OMITS the field typechecks, and a later read of that field REFUSES at evaluation with `NoSuchField { type_name, field }`. So `T?` means mandatory-at-construction-and-typed-optional, and the two states the spelling conflates are `the field is absent because the author wrote none` and `the field is absent because the literal predates it`. EVIDENCE, a two-row probe with a positive control, which is what makes it a finding rather than a conjecture about one type: `type Widget { name: String  tag: Int? }` with `Widget { name: "w" }` refuses on `.tag` with `NoSuchField { type_name: "Widget", field: "tag" }`, while `Widget { name: "w", tag: Present { value: 3 } }.tag` reads back correctly -- so the mechanism is neither `reads fail` nor `this type is broken`, it is omission-with-no-default, and it is GENERAL to any record type. RUNG: this is FAIL-CLOSED and explicitly NOT silent wrongness -- the interpreter refuses rather than answering the false that a reader would expect -- which is the whole of the good news and it is what sets the rung at mitigatable rather than below the ladder. WHAT THE CORPUS CURRENTLY BELIEVES INSTEAD, and this is the reason the row exists: two carriers filed this on 2026-08-27 as `a nested compile is not evaluable in the interpreter, refusing with NoSuchField Node.ident` -- v1.tests.claim.reference_derived_disposition_census_witness_test and a sibling paragraph in v1.compiler.emit_rust. Nested compiles are one POPULATION that happens to construct Nodes through helpers omitting v1.std.core Node's `ident` and then read it; they are not the class, and the repair that description suggests (route around the nested pipeline with a host builtin) does not touch the cause. **POPULATION: UNENUMERATED, and stated as such rather than guessed.** It is every record type in the corpus carrying an optional field together with a literal that omits it; `v1.std.core Node.ident` is one specimen and the probe above is another. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, a capability and not an artifact: construction that supplies every declared field, so that a literal omitting an optional field is either unwritable or carries the absent value -- at which point the distinction between the two absences is made at construction and no read can encounter a field that was never written. NOT REPAIRED HERE BY RULING: a defaulting change alters evaluation semantics corpus-wide and needs its own subject, population and review. **A FIFTH FORM, MEASURED 2026-09-03, AND IT IS THE FIRST ONE WHERE THE DECLARATION ALREADY EXISTED: ONE SENTINEL CARRYING TWO FACTS WHILE A TYPED DECLARATION SAYS THEY ARE DISTINCT.** std.algebra free_monoid_collection_templates declares first and last as [ReceiverSelf] -> OptionalOf { inner: ReceiverElement }, and positional get as [ReceiverSelf, Int] -> the same. So the result is an optional OF THE ELEMENT: where the element type is itself optional the two absences are distinct levels. The v1 interpreter's arm bodies returned items.front().cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null), collapsing 'the collection had no first element' and 'the first element is itself absent' into one Value::Null, because in the interpreter Null is both the absent-marker and a legal element value. DISCRIMINATING RED, executed on both arms, not asserted: over [none, Present { value: 2 }] -- a list of length TWO whose first element is absent -- the interpreter took the OUTER Absent arm (answering 'the list is empty') while emitted Rust answered Present { Absent }. Positive control [5,6].first() returned 5 in both. WHAT MAKES THIS FORM DIFFERENT FROM THE PRECEDING FOUR, and why it is filed as a receipt on this row rather than as its own class: THE MISSING CONSTRUCTOR WAS NOT MISSING. The declaration existed, was mirrored into the seed as std_algebra.rs, and was READ BY THE TYPE CHECKER -- v1.compiler.infer_method walks algebra_templates_for_profile matching on name, which is why std.target_representation representation_spelling_for typechecks with `-> RepresentationSpelling<R>?` returning `|> first` directly. The EVALUATOR in the same binary referenced std_algebra zero times. One binary, one declaration, the type checker honouring it and the evaluator contradicting it. THE RUNG THIS SAT AT WAS NOT A RUNG: the interpreter did not refuse, it answered confidently and wrongly, which DESIGN section 4b places OUTSIDE the ladder. A REPAIR EXISTS AND IS DELIBERATELY UNLANDED (gunbc#10187, DRAFT, not merged): the two arms WOULD build optional_present/optional_absent, mirroring map_lookup_as_optional, which already performs exactly this for maps -- and the reason it is withheld is the PRIOR FILING recorded below, not an unfinished implementation. NO CLIMB IS CLAIMED BY THIS ROW. The class stands where it stood -- 'decided by call site, not value shape, so a stored V = Optional<T> payload is never mistaken for an already-wrapped result' -- prior art in the same file rather than a new mechanism. THE REALIZATION PICTURE IS THE PART FUTURE READERS SHOULD NOT FLATTEN, AND IT HAS THREE COLUMNS RATHER THAN ONE VERDICT. VERIFIED BY EXECUTION: rust emission nests faithfully; and the python and typescript TYPE CONSTRUCTORS cannot carry the nesting at all -- typing.Optional[typing.Optional[int]] EVALUATES TO typing.Optional[int] and compares equal, and in the typescript realization `{0} | undefined` the absent element and the absent selection are the same value (undefined === undefined). No method template can repair that, because the type it would have to return does not exist in those targets; a boxed encoding would be a different realization of Optional, not a better template. REFUTED BY EXECUTION: the inference that go is faithful BECAUSE its optional row is `*{0}` and **T is a legal Go type. The row permits the nesting and the emitter did not do it -- go emitted *int64 for a declared Optional<Optional<Int>>. THE ROW PREDICTS CAPABILITY, NOT BEHAVIOUR, and that is the transferable lesson here: the python collapse and the go faithfulness were derived the same way, from the optional row, and only one survived execution. UNOBSERVED: what python and go emission actually ANSWER end-to-end, because for this input neither artifact runs -- go returns two values from single-return signatures and references an undefined Present, python uses Optional and Present with neither imported nor defined. Those sit in accepted_source_emits_uncompilable_target and are UPSTREAM of this class; the denotation question is not reachable there until the artifacts compile. NO REFUSAL IS FILED FOR THOSE PATHS AND THAT IS DELIBERATE. A refusal on unrepresentable nesting would be a wall in front of a hole, and for typescript it would be unreachable in any case: main.rs parses only rust | python | go | dag and RenderTarget has no TypeScript variant, so no fixture can drive that path and any check over it would be permanently green by construction. Recorded here as an OBSERVED BOUNDARY with its evidence rather than as a check that establishes nothing. PRIOR FILING, FOUND AFTER THE REPAIR WAS WRITTEN AND BEFORE IT LANDED, WHICH IS WHY THE REPAIR IS DEFERRED RATHER THAN SHIPPED: this seam is ALREADY FILED as audit-queue item 0 of docs/plans/compiler-guarantee-recovery-gap-analysis.md (2026-08-27, from gunbc#9491), and extdeps.bmc.pid_control_decode carries the minimal reproduction inline plus an admitted workaround -- it renames its own field to magnitude on exactly this ground and claims no compiler guarantee. (1) MEMBER-SELECTION POLICY: v1.compiler.infer_lookup field_summary_for_type opens `if field == "value" && normed_opt` and unwraps otherwise, so FIELD ACCESS THROUGH AN OPTIONAL AUTO-UNWRAPS FOR EVERY SPELLING EXCEPT `value` -- which item 0 calls a language-semantics decision, not obviously a bug, with three candidate answers. (2) TYPE CONFORMANCE, below floor independently of (1): a record-valued expression accepted where an Int is required. THAT PRIOR FILING RECLASSIFIES THIS ROW'S OWN REPAIR AND THE RECLASSIFICATION IS THE POINT. The type path ALREADY treats first() as optional and silently unwraps field access through it; the evaluator returned a bare element, so that unwrap was a no-op. Making the evaluator construct a real Present{...} therefore does not merely expose consumers that depended on a flattening -- it makes the EVALUATOR INCONSISTENT WITH AN AUTO-UNWRAP THE TYPE CHECKER STILL PERFORMS, in the opposite direction from where the two halves started, which is not a climb. Honouring the declaration at the evaluator requires the matching evaluator-side unwrap, and that IS item 0(1)'s open policy question. SO THE INTERPRETER/EMITTED DIVERGENCE AND ITEM 0(1) ARE THE SAME SEAM APPROACHED FROM TWO SIDES, AND WHOEVER SETTLES THE MEMBER-SELECTION POLICY OWNS THE EVALUATOR REPAIR TOO. That is corroboration of a class filed five days ago and is NOT a second finding; a second row for a filed class is the nicknaming refusal at the ledger layer. What such a check must additionally do is adjudicate any surviving duplicate against main's BYTES and REFUSE when neither copy matches, rather than resolve by position or first-wins, which is silent. **A SIXTH FORM, whose consequence is not a bad message but a SILENT SAFETY HOLE: a `Bool` return carrying a three-valued question, where the collapsed value is the ADMITTING one.** Specimen (gunbc#10337, 2026-09-04): `where_refinement_brand_nominal_mismatch` returned `Bool` over three outcomes -- these are distinct declarations, these are the same declaration, and I CANNOT TELL which declarations these are. HARM, AND THE REASON THIS CLASS HIDES: the number that lands is REAL -- a true measurement of something, charged to a row that does not own it -- SO NOTHING LOOKS WRONG. The row's reported cost becomes a function of DISCOVERY ORDER rather than of the row, and because the required floor's safety limit is a FAIL-STOP, an order-dependent number decides whether the line stops. DISTINGUISHING FACT, AND IT IS THE FORCING CLASS RATHER THAN ANY OBSERVED FIELD: an artifact every identity of which is knowable before the fold is PREPARATION-FORCEABLE -- the floor builds it ahead and bills preparation, no claim window ever contains that build, and it therefore carries NO fill bracket CORRECTLY. ABSENCE OF A BRACKET IS NOT EVIDENCE OF A DEFECT, and reasoning from that absence is the specific misread this class invites. An artifact only a claim can force must record its fill, at which point the claim is charged the marginal half. The third state is the conjunction: claim-forced AND unbracketed. RECOGNITION RULE, AND IT IS NOT AVAILABLE FROM THE COST LOG: a cost log reports what a run OBSERVED, while regime membership is a property of the CONSTRUCTION -- is the artifact memoized, is it shared, is every identity it can be asked for knowable before the fold. Those are decidable by reading the producer and are not recoverable from a measurement, so a row's absence from shared-fill accounting is equally consistent with preparation-forceable, with not-shared-at-all, and with this class -- and the floor output cannot separate the three. The difference is the consequence. THE REPAIR IS A REFUSAL ARM, NOT A REPOINT, and this is the transferable half.
