Executed and enforcing are different properties, and no amount of coverage over the calling path distinguishes them. **It is bounded against three neighbours and none of their recognition rules will find it.** `reachability_read_as_occupancy` asks whether to DELETE a guard that is quiet, and warns that mechanism-yes / join-yes / occupancy-zero is a healthy guard; this class is not about deletion and not about occupancy -- the conjunct here should stay, and the defect is that it was believed to be doing work it was not. Section 6's inert-lens tell is machinery that nothing GATES on; this is gated on. Section 4b's decoration case is a check whose RED cannot be authored ANYWHERE; this RED can be authored -- it simply never was, which is the whole difference, and it is the difference between a permanent ceiling and one missing fixture. **THE REMEDY HAS TWO HALVES AND THE SECOND IS WHAT MAKES THE FIRST SOUND.** First: for each wall, run the DELETE arm and confirm EXACTLY ONE witness reddens -- not that a witness reddens. Second: the subject that isolates the conjunct must be chosen with care, because a red for the wrong reason is how this class survives its own test. **A THIRD HALF, AND IT IS THE MIRROR OF THE SECOND (amended 2026-09-03, tidy-koi-619 on gunbc#10257, found by warm-seal-35): CONFIRM THE DELETE ARM ACTUALLY DELETED.** The second half guards a RED for the wrong reason; this guards a GREEN for the wrong reason, and the remedy as written above is satisfiable by a no-op. A probe that FAILED TO MUTATE and a probe whose mutation the check TOLERATED print the identical passing value, so 'run the delete arm and read the verdict' cannot distinguish a wall that holds from a wall that was never tested. THE PROBE MUST ASSERT THAT IT MUTATED -- count the substitutions, or diff the tree -- as a claim SEPARATE from what the check then says, and then restore and re-run to confirm the green returns, because a one-sided probe does not establish that the verdict tracks the mutation at all. SPECIMEN, and it is worth recording because both failure paths are real rather than imagined: proving the discriminating red for every_restored_stall_is_still_rostered took THREE attempts and the first two mutated nothing. Attempt one ran the edit through `python3 -c` under DOUBLE QUOTES, so the shell consumed the escape sequences and the replacement matched no text. Attempt two chose an anchor that matched TWICE -- the check's own pin list had just been added to the same module as the roster and repeated the same member spellings, which is a general hazard: ANY CHECK THAT ENUMERATES ROSTER MEMBERS CREATES A SECOND POPULATION WITH THE SAME SPELLINGS, so every later sweep over that module must distinguish them. Both attempts printed the passing value and would have been read as the wall holding. Only the third, anchored on roster-only context, dropped the row and produced the red, with the green returning after restore. THE CATCH WAS INCIDENTAL, WHICH IS THE ARGUMENT FOR WRITING IT DOWN: the no-ops were noticed only because the edit script happened to carry an occurrence-count assertion written for an unrelated reason. Nothing in the remedy required it. **A FOURTH HALF, WHICH MOVES THE ROW FROM PROBE DESIGN TO READ DESIGN (2026-09-05, vivid-ibex-751, gunbc#10345): THE VERIFICATION MUTATED NOTHING, SO THERE WAS NO MUTATION TO CONFIRM.** The third half tells an author to assert that their delete arm actually deleted, and it is scoped to a probe the author CONSTRUCTS. A read-only verification has no such arm: the artifact is not modified, nothing can fail to apply, and the third half's question -- did my edit land -- has no referent. The class survives the move intact because the invalid state does not depend on there being an edit at all: **TWO HYPOTHESES, ONE OUTPUT SHAPE, AND A REGION READ THAT IS BYTE-IDENTICAL UNDER BOTH.** **SPECIMEN OF THE READ FORM.** The claim under test was that a generated projection renders each row's FIRST receipt string. The check run was a grep for the row's bullet, confirming it BEGAN with `receipts[0]`. It did, and the claim was false: the projection concatenates ALL receipts into one bullet. A prefix match is satisfied identically by first-receipt-only and by all-receipts-concatenated, because a concatenation STARTS WITH ITS FIRST ELEMENT -- so the region inspected could not differ between the hypothesis and its negation, and the check could not have come out any other way under either. The tail was the only discriminating region and it was never read. The disconfirming bytes were present in the same file, one line further along. **THE RECOGNITION RULE FOR THE READ FORM IS A QUESTION ABOUT THE REGION, NOT ABOUT THE PREDICATE: NAME THE BYTES THAT WOULD DIFFER IF THE CLAIM WERE FALSE, AND CHECK THAT YOUR READ COVERS THEM.** `Did I verify it` is the wrong question and it is why this passes review: the verification is real, it executes, it reads the true artifact, and it is honest. The failure is that the WINDOW was chosen where the two hypotheses agree. A structural over-approximation of the window -- read the whole record, diff the whole file -- is the cheap discharge and is usually correct; where the window must be narrow, the narrowing must be justified by naming what distinguishes it, in the same way the second half of this row demands a red be shown to fail for the intended reason. **THE TELL IS THAT THE READER CANNOT STATE WHAT THEIR CHECK WOULD PRINT IF THE CLAIM WERE FALSE**, which is the same tell this ledger records for a change detector, arriving here with no literal and no test to inspect. **SPECIMEN, with both halves executed** (gunbc#9838, `gunbc.self_host_compile_phase_frontier`): `census_population_is_duplicate_free` was added as a conjunct of `receipt_population_coherent`, evaluated on every coherence check and reachable from the live path; deleting it left the whole witness module green. The fixture that isolates it is a receipt whose `refused_files` REPEATS A PATH -- enrolled as `a_receipt_whose_refused_population_repeats_a_path_is_refused_by_coherence` in `test.claim.self_host_compile_phase_frontier_witness`, with `the_same_receipt_without_the_repeat_is_coherent` as its positive control. The subject that looks equally good and is not: a repeated ERROR DIAGNOSTIC IDENTITY also reddens the module, but it breaks a count invariant one line over rather than this conjunct, so it would have scored the DEAD conjunct as LIVE and closed the investigation with the wall still inert. **RUNG: mechanically preventable, and the honest ceiling is the same rung rather than higher.** The unenforcing conjunct stays writable; what exists is an executed falsifier per wall, not a construction that forbids authoring one. The next-rung trigger is a CAPABILITY and not an artifact: a mechanism that derives, from the check itself, the population its removal would change -- so that a conjunct with an empty such population fails to compile rather than waiting for an author to think of the delete arm. Enumerating delete arms by hand does not retire it; it only executes this row's recognition rule once per wall. **Its near twin is `instrument_output_read_as_subject_content`, and they meet from opposite sides**: there a report is complete for the REPORTER and read as complete for the SUBJECT; here a conjunct is genuinely executed and read as genuinely discriminating. Both are the shape where the health signal the reader actually checks -- completeness there, execution here -- is fully satisfied while the property they believe it establishes does not hold. **Neither is found by checking harder along the axis that already reads green.** **SECOND SPECIMEN, 2026-09-02, the same shape one layer down and found by a different tell.** `v1.stage0.required_regen_host` `admit_stage_execution_from_model` built ONE identity list from the stage plan and passed it as BOTH the `planned` and the `executed` field of `RegenStageExecutionObservation`, so `v2.workflow.regen_convergence_transaction` `regen_identity_population_eq` evaluated x == x.clone() on every install of the self-host convergence transaction. The conjunct ran, the install boundary really blocked, and `StagePlannedExecutedMismatch` stayed authorable in `v2.test.claim.regen_convergence_transaction_witness_test` while being unreachable from the real acceptance path -- the exact split section 4b's authorable-RED rule exists to catch. **THE DELETE ARM IS NOT WHAT FOUND IT; THE PRODUCER COUNT IS**, and that is this specimen's addition to the row: reading the two sides of one population equality back to their PRODUCERS and finding a single producer is cheaper than removing the conjunct and re-running, and it needs no experiment -- any equality whose two arguments trace to one value is this class already. **THE REPAIR IS A SECOND PRODUCER, NEVER A SECOND READ OF THE FIRST**: a digest delta over the journalled generated roster, taken before the copy loop and again after the build, carried in its own Rust newtype `ObservedEffectPopulation` with no conversion from the plan, so a later refactor cannot re-collapse the two sides without deleting a type. Its discriminating red `stage_execution_joins_the_plan_to_independently_observed_effects` carries TWO arms because one-directional identity checking is how the loss survived: an effect on a path OUTSIDE the plan, which was green before because `allowed_after` blanket-permits every already-dirty stage0 path for the rest of the transaction; and a planned surface the stage left byte-identical, which did refuse before but as `InstalledDigestMismatch` -- a CONTENT verdict standing in for a POPULATION one, so the arm asserts the cause and not that it refused.)
- **unbacked execution claim** (prose asserts a live executing relation — this runs on X, X reads these fields, Y already resolves Z — that no authority backs, and a reader forms the premise and plans against it. §4b's CI rung drop names the harm PREMISE CONTAMINATION in its own words; this row is that harm as a general class, since the drop is about one paragraph in the canonical authority and the class is about every carrier. **THE ORIGINS ARE A FIELD OF THIS ONE ROW RATHER THAN SEPARATE ROWS, because the recognition rule is identical across them** — resolve the claim against the authority that owns the relation — **but the REMEDY BRANCHES ON THE ORIGIN, and a remedy applied without reading the origin manufactures new false statements.** Three origins are ATTESTED here and a fourth is conceivable but was NOT found. ORIGIN 1, EXECUTOR DELETED: the claim was true and its executor was removed. Specimen: `gunbc.floor_component_receipt_document` recited that an alert downloads an artifact named `floor-component-receipt` from a run id it is given and reads four named fields; that alert was `falsifier-alert.yml`, deleted 2026-08-15, and a lane read the module, believed the transport live, planned against it, and was corrected by its reviewing authority — the cost is measured, not hypothetical. Second specimen, same origin: `gunbc.ci_failure_class` stated its dissolution trigger as calling `classify_failure_reason` *the way claim_executor already resolves* `gunbc.floor_component_receipt`; `write_floor_component_receipt_at` did exactly that from 2026-07-30 (gunbc#7467) until gunbc#9228 deleted it on 2026-08-25. ORIGIN 2, THE CITED SYMBOL DIED WHILE ITS CONTENT SURVIVED AS AN ANNOTATION, and this one has a corpus-wide producer: `v1_compiler.cli_run` twice cited `floor_component_resource_checkpoint_note` and `floor_component_phase_journal_scaffold_note`, which were real `data …: String` declarations in `gunbc.floor_component_receipt` until the 2026-08-30 prose-bankruptcy sweep (gunbc#9752) converted module-scope commentary rows into §4c annotations corpus-wide. §4c makes an annotation uncitable by construction — `v1_compiler.cli_run` `annotation_erased_scan_text` feeds the semantic passes, so annotation text is not present in the tree the citation harvester walks. **THE CONSEQUENCE IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE ONE THIS ROW FIRST DREW, AND THE CORRECTION IS THE INSTRUCTIVE PART.** The first revision reasoned that the sweep therefore broke every citation of a converted row, making the population the sweep's own diff. Measured by a separate lane (gunbc XL-0-CITE, 2026-09-01) against `v1_compiler.declaration_index`, whose only citation producers are `citation_from_record_literal` and `citation_from_constructor_call`: the typed-citation breakage is **ZERO**, computed both today and as of the sweep commit, on a control that discriminates in both directions. A plain name in annotation prose was never a citation, so the sweep could not break one. What that leaves is a REAL class with an honest ceiling rather than a defect population: these citations bind READERS and no mechanism, so they sit in §4b's *outside the modeled guarantee* column — observed and repaired by reading, never gated — and calling them a dangling-citation population would have been rung inflation about a check that by construction cannot see them. The two `cli_run` sites are genuine instances of the harm and there is no mechanical census that would have found them. ORIGIN 3, WRONG AUTHORITY FOR SOMETHING THAT STILL EXECUTES: `dag/test/claim/instrument_sandbox_witness_test` says its live half `runs on the falsifier lane`; that half sits on `FalsifierSubstrateLongLane`, which `std.witness_admission` `witness_cadence_has_scheduled_route` reports routeless — but it also carries a local recipe, and `OfflineLocalRecipe` HAS a route, so the relation is live and only the named authority is wrong. THE FOURTH, NEVER BACKED AT ALL, IS NOT ATTESTED IN THIS CORPUS and is recorded as unmeasured rather than as a population. **THIS ROW IS NOT THE AUTHORITY ON ANY POPULATION, and saying so is the point:** `gunbc.deleted_cadence_reference_census` already owns the falsifier-cadence instance of origin 1 — twelve sites, a `DeletedCadenceReferenceStanding` vocabulary whose `PremiseInvalidated` arm is exactly this class, and one `GuaranteeRungDrop` filed once for the shared executor rather than per site. A pattern row that restated its population would be a second authority for a fact that already has one, which is the §3 violation this row's own remedy is meant to prevent; so the census is cited here and nothing about its contents is copied. What belongs HERE is only the recognition rule and the remedy arms, which generalize past the falsifier. **THE MECHANICAL TEST for finding one is sharper than asking whether the verb is runs or is enrolled: resolve the claim against the authority that owns the relation, and ask whether the prose implies a live route for something that authority says has none.** The naive runs/enrolled discriminator loses one arm and it is the common one — `Enrolled on falsifier_rehomed_bin_wet_entries (nightly batch 5)` is nominally an enrollment claim, which is TRUE, but `nightly` asserts a cadence that executes and `FalsifierRehomedBinWet` is routeless, so the reader forms the false premise anyway. Three outcomes per line: asserts execution (contaminating), asserts enrollment only (true, leave it), asserts enrollment while implying a live cadence (contaminating). **THE REMEDY HAS THREE ARMS, KEYED TO WHAT THE AUTHORITY SAYS NOW, and picking the wrong one is itself an instance of this class.** Origin 1 → PAST TENSE WITH THE DATE, keeping the reasoning, because the reasoning usually outlives its executor and a reader who finds a dangling claim silently deleted learns nothing. Origin 2 → name the module and the FACT rather than the dead symbol, since §4c left no symbol to cite; past-tensing here would wrongly report the content as gone when only its citability is. Origin 3 → CORRECT IT to the authority that does back it, NOT past tense: past-tensing a live relation records it as dead and is a new contamination in the opposite direction, produced by the remedy itself. Origin 4 → say it was never backed, or delete the assertion while keeping any surviving reasoning; NEVER past tense, because there is no past to record, and `X USED TO resolve Y` asserts an execution that also never happened. **The default against deletion is a default and not an absolute: a sentence whose entire content is a false execution claim has no reasoning to preserve.** **THE SELF-RECEIPT, and it is the most useful thing in this row (2026-09-01):** the first revision of this entry asserted the `ci_failure_class` clause and the two `cli_run` notes as never-true, on the strength of a grep showing they resolve nowhere TODAY. **Both were true when written**, and the row about unbacked claims therefore contained two of them. It was caught by a reviewing authority asking for verification rather than inheritance, not by any check. **RECOGNITION RULE EARNED: `resolves nowhere now` is not `never resolved`, the distinction is decided by history and not by the working tree, and the instrument is `git log --all -S` over the DECLARATION FORM** — which also separates origin 1 from origin 2 by showing WHAT deleted the referent. The neighbouring rule is `positional_citation`'s: a citation left naming a deleted symbol fakes a grep hit, so every arm above records the change rather than quietly removing the name.)
- **a source-ingested module has no judged standing** (the required gates adjudicate entry-derived closures, while source-root ingest admits a larger identity population; a module outside every required closure can carry a structurally checkable defect while every gate stays green. This is NOT an import-edge class: globally unique names can resolve without a direct import, so authored inbound imports neither prove nor disprove judgment. It is also NOT a claim that every identity outside the closures is defective or should be gated. The severity is unknown, and that unknowability is the finding. **SPECIMEN AND DISCRIMINATING CONTROL** (node://adhoc-2d3c27b7-660, PR #9899): gunbc.annotation_parse_instrument_agreement constructed Terminal { reason, authority } although Terminal declares only reason; a clean required run left both build and witnesses green and both in-run judgment receipts omitted the module. The matched visible control placed the same undeclared-field construction in ordinary production module gunbc.clock_read: both required lanes refused with `field 'authority' not found in type 'LaneVisibilityControl'`; removing only the plant returned both lanes to green. Thus visibility, with defect class held constant, discriminates the arms. The witnesses prediction was weaker than its result: only submitted-closure membership was known beforehand, yet execution established strict judgment by refusing. **MEASUREMENT, EXPLICITLY NOT COVERAGE OR ENFORCEMENT:** the producer joins every first-party identity admitted by the exact source-root ingest against the union of module identities for which build or witnesses produced a strict typecheck verdict. On clean run 33484666539 it derived 4,419 admitted, 947 build-judged, 2,158 witnesses-judged, 2,319 in the judgment union, and 2,100 with no required-lane judgment. Build contributes only 161 identities beyond witnesses, so the two required closures are far from independent and their union remains close to one lane's reach. Exactly one of the 2,100 is confirmed orphaned by the executed control; the other 2,099 are Undecided because no typed authority currently says whether each is an executing standalone entry, fixture/generated/excluded, retired, or orphaned. Those identities are deliberately re-derived and NOT committed as a baseline or roster: a hand-carried 2,099-row exemption set would be a ratchet wearing a wall's name. The instrument reports; it does not refuse, and citing it as a gate is rung inflation. **PROVENANCE LIMIT:** in-run strict judgments and cross-process content-addressed cache judgments are separate columns. Required CI does not arm GUNBC_RESOLVED_GRAPH_CACHE_DIR, so the cross-process column was empty beside nonempty in-run columns in both planted and reverted runs. That empty column is a control for the unarmed premise, NOT evidence that the provenance split is exercised by required CI. **CEILING: decidable but waiting on a single standing authority.** A wall now would either false-red legitimate importer-free subjects or grandfather opaque debt. The next rung requires four capabilities together: (1) one typed standing registry exhaustively binds every first-party ingest identity to required judgment, a verified executing consumer, typed fixture/generated/exclusion, retirement, or orphan standing; (2) each executing-consumer binding is checked against the named consumer's actual roster and execution receipt, so a hollow alias cannot admit it; (3) a changed retained identity resolves only through its named consumer from materialized facts within the required floor's 8-second wall and 500-millisecond CPU envelopes, never by whole-corpus compilation; and (4) the two-direction matched undeclared-field control remains executable. At that rung admission makes the invalid state unwritable; until then this remains measurement and an Undecided population, not a defect backlog.)
- refusal deferred to the emitted runtime (a compiler refusal expressed as a construct IN THE EMITTED PROGRAM rather than as a diagnostic in the COMPILING one, so the compile reports success and the line does not stop). INVALID STATE: an emission path that cannot answer a declared fact writes a panic, an error expression or a stub into the artifact and returns normally, so the compiler emits N files and reports zero diagnostics beside a body that cannot run. HARM: fabricated plausible output at the strongest grain -- an artifact that looks like a compiled program and is not one. The refusal message may be perfectly honest and still fail DESIGN section 5, because nobody reads it until production; the reviewer's three questions are does the line stop, is the stop typed and located, does analysis precede restart, and this arm fails the first. DISTINGUISHING FACTS: the tell is a SUCCESS REPORT beside a refusal construct in the same run -- 'compiled: 7 files emitted, 0 diagnostics' printed by a compile whose emitted body was a panic naming its own refusal. It is NOT the same as a legitimate runtime arm at an honest boundary: the discriminator is whether the compiler KNEW at emit time, from modeled structure, that the artifact could not answer. If it knew and wrote the knowledge into the output instead of the diagnostic stream, the class applies. SPECIMEN AND HOW IT WAS FOUND: gunbc#9886's shell service-emission climb refused an unrealizable output channel by emitting panic!() from emit_shell_channel_expr while emit_artifact returned its files normally. Found by a DIFFERENTIAL READ against an independent implementation of the same repair (calm-boar-314's superseded WIP), then by execution on a probe; five consecutive reviews APPROVED the arm, two of them praising the refusal by name, so review did not catch it and is not the mechanism that will. WHY REVIEW MISSES IT: the refusal is present, typed, located and honestly worded at the point a reader's eye lands, and the missing half is an ABSENCE elsewhere -- no diagnostic in a stream the diff does not show. RECOGNITION RULE: for every arm that emits a refusal construct into a target artifact, ask what the COMPILE reports on the same input. If the answer is success, or any file count above zero, the arm belongs to this class. REPAIR, and it is usually already written next door: pair the refusal expression with a blocking emit-stage diagnostic so the artifact producer returns no files. In the specimen the FILE transport already had that pair and shell had the expression half only, so the repair was building the twin rather than inventing a path -- which is the general shape, since a codebase that emits refusals at all usually has one arm that does it correctly. KEEP THE EXPRESSION as the unreachable second half rather than deleting it, so the two readers cannot disagree if the wall is ever weakened. RUNG FOUND AT: 1, mitigatable -- a runtime panic in generated code, established by executing the emitter and reading its own success line, NOT by reading the type. CEILING: structurally guaranteed, and reached in the specimen -- a located compile-time refusal per declared field, derived from modeled structure, with zero files emitted. NEXT TRIGGER for the class as a whole: an emission-stage census that pairs every refusal-construct emitter with its diagnostic twin, since nothing today makes the missing half visible and the specimen was found by accident. POPULATION NOTE THE SPECIMEN CARRIES: the wall converted a SILENT corpus defect into a loud one -- gunbc.WitnessBin.Run declares stderr_truncated, stderr_total_bytes and stderr_retained_bytes, three channels the Rust realization cannot answer because the emitted Command body implements no capture policy, so every entry closure reaching that operation now refuses at field grain. That was always true and the old arm said nothing. Measured OUTSIDE REQUIRED COVERAGE, which is the honest phrasing and not 'affects nothing': claim_executor --required-emit-compile is green on all 8 rostered entries, each with its own mutation-established red, those 8 reaching 47 modules, with ZERO shell-channel diagnostics.)
- **mitigation injected where the judgment declined** (a downstream stage injects a RUNTIME mitigation at exactly the seam where an upstream judgment returned Undecidable, so a 4b rung-1 fallback comes to occupy the place the wall belongs and the declined seam stops looking empty. **THE LOAD-BEARING HALF IS WHAT THE COINCIDENCE PROVES ABOUT THE DECLINE**: an Undecidable verdict is normally read as benign conservatism — the facts did not settle it, nothing is claimed, no harm asserted — and a mitigation landing on the SAME LINE is the first evidence that a real defect was sitting under that silence. The decline is therefore load-bearing AT THIS SITE, which refutes reading an Undecidable verdict as evidence of ABSENCE. **IT DOES NOT MAKE THE DECLINE CENSUS PREDICTIVE, and this row carries that limit because the author first wrote the stronger claim and then measured it FALSE** on the same board that produced the specimen: 259 of 272 declines sit in modules with ZERO blocking errors, only 6 of 28 decline-bearing modules carry any error at all, and the module holding 42 of the 63 errors carries ZERO declines and 105 ACCEPTS. Declines and defects are ANTI-CORRELATED at module grain, so this specimen is ONE co-location out of 272 and a walk of the decline census would be walking sites that are ~95% empty. The class is about a mitigation landing on a declined seam; it is NOT a claim that declines predict defects. Distinct from `absorbing_fallback`, whose arm WIDENS to a superset and destroys the precise mechanism s signal; here the arm NARROWS to a panic and destroys nothing except the evidence that a judgment was owed. Distinct from `reflection_evidence_structural_proof`, which mistakes an observation for a proof; here nothing is proven or observed — a decision was declined and then papered. Specimen with a receipt, measured 2026-09-01 on gunbc 0e8c49d and unrepaired at authoring: `v2.std.diagnostic outcome_accepted` is `fn outcome_accepted<T>(value: T) -> Outcome<T>` with T FREE; `v2.compiler.07_target_carriers target_fidelity_quotient_optional` declares `-> Outcome<Optional<Node>>` and calls `outcome_accepted(Present { value: child })`, so T instantiates to `Optional<Node>` and THE SOURCE IS CORRECT. The Rust emitter nonetheless rendered `Some(child).expect("fail-closed: an optional value flowed into non-optional parameter 0 of outcome_accepted (empty Optional at runtime)")`. FOUR DEFECTS IN ONE LINE: it fires on a GENERIC formal where `Optional<Node>` is a legitimate instantiation rather than a cardinality escape, so the check does not know T is free; it substitutes a runtime panic for a compile-time judgment; the emitted code does NOT typecheck anyway (rustc E0308 expected `Option<Rc<Node>>` found `Rc<Node>`), so the mitigation buys nothing while standing where the wall belongs; and its own panic string calls itself `fail-closed`, which is 4b rung inflation inside a string literal — a diagnostic naming the discipline it violates is worse than a silent one because it will be cited as evidence the discipline holds. The coincidence that makes this a class and not a bug: `v1.compiler.infer declared_type_inhabitance` returned InhabitanceUndecidable{UndecidableOptionalCarrier} at that module s ONLY optional-carrier decline, 07_target_carriers.dag:128:24 parameter `value` — the same line the emitter mitigated. RUNG FOUND AT: 1 (mitigatable) on a seam whose CEILING is 3 (structurally guaranteed), since generic-formal instantiation is decidable from modeled structure. CONSEQUENCE WITH ITS OWN TRIGGER, deliberately NOT folded into this row: wiring the optional-carrier judgment is argued by this specimen but is a separate change, and its trigger is a producer at that seam that can distinguish a FREE type variable from a declared non-optional formal — until that exists the judgment would decline for the same reason it declines today.)
- **a transport close is read as protocol completion** (a consumer of a streamed response decides the response is FINISHED because the connection ENDED, rather than because the protocol's own terminator arrived. Every partial answer then presents as a complete one, and the consumer reports success while holding a fragment. This is state-space conflation on the completion axis specifically -- `the bytes stopped` and `the sender said it was done` are two facts, and a stream has a third state, SEVERED, that a two-state reading cannot express. **SPECIMEN, measured 2026-09-01 by a peer session on its own routing layer.** A streaming SSE client accumulated `message_start`, then a long run of `content_block_delta`, and registered completion in `res.on('end')` UNCONDITIONALLY -- never checking that `content_block_stop`, `message_delta` carrying `stop_reason`, and `message_stop` had arrived. A stream severed after 364 thinking deltas therefore produced a well-formed response object containing an intro line and a heading, which the transcript recorded with a tidy `stop_reason` and the UI rendered faithfully as the model's answer. **EVERY LAYER DOWNSTREAM OF THE READING LAYER BEHAVED CORRECTLY, AND THAT IS WHY THE TRUNCATION PROPAGATED SILENTLY.** The reading layer did NOT behave correctly -- it is the defect, converting a transport termination into a protocol completion and minting a `stop_reason` no sender had emitted. What every layer BELOW it then did was faithful: the transcript recorded the object it was handed, and the UI rendered that object exactly. The distinction is the whole diagnostic value of the row -- a defect that hands its consumers a WELL-FORMED artifact recruits every correct layer beneath it into carrying the wrong answer, so correctness downstream is not evidence of correctness upstream. It is also why the class survived a full evening of investigation that cleared the UI, the parser, the token budget, and the serving stack in turn: each of those really was innocent, and each exoneration made the true location look less likely rather than more. **THE DIAGNOSIS WAS INVERTED FOR HOURS BY THE ARTIFACT THE DEFECT ITSELF PRODUCED.** The fabricated `stop_reason: end_turn` was read at face value as `the model chose to stop`, so the investigation was pointed at the model and at long-context serving -- a lead reinforced by a genuinely large KV allocation -- while the defect sat in the layer doing the reading. **RECOGNITION RULE: find the completion predicate and ask whether it names a PROTOCOL EVENT or a TRANSPORT EVENT.** `on('end')`, `on('close')`, process exit, EOF, a read returning zero, and `the socket closed` are transport; a terminator frame, a `done` member, a declared length satisfied, and a trailer are protocol. A consumer whose completion predicate is transport-shaped cannot distinguish a finished answer from a severed one, no matter what it does downstream. **THE SECOND HALF OF THE RULE IS ABOUT RECOVERY, AND IT IS WHY THE CLASS IS NOT MERELY A MISSING CHECK.** Neither the Messages API nor Ollama's compatibility layer carries a resumption protocol -- no sequence numbers, no cursor, no reattachable session -- so a severed stream cannot be resumed and the only recovery is re-issuing the whole request. A client that cannot DETECT truncation therefore cannot retry either, and the missing predicate silently removes the entire recovery arm rather than one diagnostic. **THE REMEDY IS ORDERED AND THE ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING**: make truncation a typed refusal FIRST, because until it is detectable a retry cannot be triggered and a fix cannot be verified; only then add re-issue. Adding retry first produces a client that retries the errors it can already see and silently accepts the ones it cannot. **GUNBC'S OWN EQUIVALENT PATH IS STRUCTURALLY IMMUNE, and the contrast is the useful part rather than a boast.** `gunbc.spark.serving_readback_parse` `spark_serving_parse_generate_done` reads completion from the response's OWN `done` member, and `spark_serving_parse_version_json` refuses a body it cannot parse with `SparkServingReadbackRefused` carrying a typed cause instead of collapsing to an absent reading. A severed body there produces a refusal, not a short answer -- the protocol terminator is the predicate, which is exactly the repair the specimen needs. **BOUNDED AGAINST NEIGHBOURS.** Against `empty_observation_narrow`: there an observation that could not express what changed renders as `nothing is affected`; here a PARTIAL observation renders as a COMPLETE one, so the count is plausible rather than empty and no zero draws attention. Against `absorbing_fallback`: that arm WIDENS on ignorance and is merely expensive; this arm reports a fragment as an answer and is silently wrong, which is below the ladder rather than on it. Against `unbacked_execution_claim`: there prose asserts a relation no authority backs; here a machine-generated artifact asserts a completion no protocol event backs, and it is more dangerous precisely because a `stop_reason` field reads as evidence.)**THE SAME MECHANISM ON THE SESSION-LIFECYCLE AXIS, filed here rather than as a new class because only the axis differs** (calm-eagle-759, 2026-09-05). Substitute a work session for the stream: THE SESSION ENDED and THE OBLIGATION WAS DISCHARGED are two facts, and archiving a session was read as the second. RECEIPT: `dashboard-ops archive` on a lane closed its work item, and that item had been filed specifically to OWN a DESIGN section 4b next-rung trigger. The capability was never delivered; the item closed because the session did. A trigger is retired by its trigger and by nothing else, never by the item tracking it being closed. WHAT MAKES THIS AXIS WORSE THAN THE TRANSPORT ONE IS THAT NOTHING IS WRONG AT THE MOMENT IT HAPPENS. A severed stream at least leaves a fragment that a terminator check could contradict. The archive is CORRECT about the session and asserts nothing whatever about the obligation, so there is no field to check and no consumer to disagree -- the loss is of TRACKING, not of data. It is therefore silent everywhere the closed item is not cited. In the receipt it surfaced only because the item id had been written into a pull request body about to merge, where the sentence claiming the trigger had an owner had quietly become false. The same archive call had a second effect on a third subject: it orphaned a pull request the lane had published five seconds earlier. The practice repair is to archive on THE LANE'S OWN STATEMENT that it has published nothing, never on a poll taken before the archive call -- a lane that is finishing up is precisely a lane about to publish. Recognition rule for the general axis: when a lifecycle event closes a container, enumerate what the container OWNED that the event says nothing about.)
- **merge region excludes the shared tail** (a roster carrier whose rows are multi-line blocks ending in an identical suffix makes every two-lane append resolve WRONG BY DEFAULT. The three-way merge factors the shared suffix out of the conflict region, so the region holds two half-blocks above the markers and one tail below them, and the purely additive resolution -- delete the markers, keep both sides, which is the CORRECT resolution when the two appended rows are independent -- leaves the first row's closing lines belonging to the second. Specimen: this carrier, which conflicted on every integration of main across four merge bases in one session (2026-09-01) with a shared tail of `evidence: [],` and `}`; `gunbc.rung_drop` had the same shape with `}` alone. **THE CLASS IS LOUD, AND THAT IS THE HALF A REPORT OF IT WILL GET WRONG.** The first reading was that the severed row survives as a structurally broken declaration under which the natural check -- declared names against rostered names -- still passes, because both lists stay complete. Measured against a gunbc built at d0009ca531 on the resolved shape: it is a PARSE REFUSAL at the module index, `expected expression, found Eq`, because a `data` keyword cannot stand in record-field position. Nothing reaches the checks that reading worried about, so the cost is toil and not silent wrongness -- a load-bearing authority whose every integration lands a conflict whose natural resolution does not compile, hand-repaired each time. **RECOGNITION RULE: for a carrier two lanes append to, ask what suffix the appended units SHARE, because a conflict region never contains it -- whatever semantics live in that suffix are exactly what a resolution can drop.** THE REPAIR IS THE UNIT AND NOT A CHECK: make the appended unit one line, and the shared suffix is a blank separator carrying nothing.  **SECOND, INDEPENDENT INSTANCE, AND IT RE-RAN THE MISREPORT THIS ROW WARNS ABOUT** (vivid-boar-481, 2026-09-01, integrating main on a later base than the one above): the severed identity was `sealing_property_erases_structure` and the recipient of its donated tail was `accepted_source_emits_uncompilable_target`, over the same `evidence: [],` and `}` suffix. The finder produced the naive both-sides resolution, spotted the fusion by AUDITING THE RESULTING DECLARATIONS rather than by any marker, repaired it -- and then reported that the fused file STILL PARSED and that nothing went red. That report was an inference from reading the text, never an execution, and compiling the reconstructed fused shape refused at `expected expression, found Eq`, the same diagnostic recorded above. So the row's corrected reading survived contact with a second lane, and the reading it corrects -- silent survival -- is what the second reporter independently reinvented before measuring. THE TRANSFERABLE PART IS THAT THE MISREPORT IS THE DEFAULT AND NOT A LAPSE: a reader who resolves the conflict, sees both rows present and no markers, and does not compile, will conclude 'parses fine' every time, because every visible signal available WITHOUT executing agrees with that conclusion. The audit that finds the fusion and the execution that establishes its cost are two different steps, and doing only the first is what produces a confident wrong report about severity. **THE ONE-LINE REPAIR IS HALF A REPAIR, AND THE OTHER HALF ARRIVED AS ITS OWN NIGHT OF CONFLICTS** (2026-09-03, `gunbc.rung_drop`, three lanes). Collapsing a row to one line removes the shared SUFFIX and with it the append hazard, and it converts the row into a multi-kilobyte single line -- so two lanes making LOGICALLY DISJOINT edits to ONE row, a `standing` flip and an `authored` extension, now collide on that line with certainty and with a diff no reviewer can read. Neither shape is safer than the other. They are the two halves of ONE deficit: the arbiter of a structured roster is text-level three-way merge, which sees lines and not declarations, so every line a carrier makes shared is a hazard and the only question is whether the sharing falls on appends or on edits. **CORRECTED RECOGNITION RULE: ask what LINE two independent authoring acts are forced to share, for BOTH acts a growth surface admits -- adding a unit and amending one.** THE REPAIR IS STILL THE UNIT AND STILL NOT A CHECK, but the unit is the FILE: one row per file makes both acts share nothing, and reduces the shared text to a roster of bare identifiers, one line each, where an append has no tail to lose and an amendment touches no roster line at all. **AND ONE FIELD PER LINE IS NOT ENOUGH, WHICH IS THE PART ONLY MEASUREMENT FOUND.** A three-way merge needs an UNCHANGED line between two changed regions to keep them apart, so two lanes editing ADJACENT fields conflict exactly as they did on one line. Measured with `git merge-file` on `floor_cut_heal`, a `standing` flip against an `authored` extension: one-line row, 1 conflict; one field per line with `standing` and `authored` adjacent, 1 conflict; the same fields separated by a blank line, 0 conflicts with both edits present. So the carrier unit is a line AND ITS SEPARATION, and a representation that merges is one where every independently amendable field is separated from its neighbours by a line no amendment touches. The roster still conflicts on a two-lane append -- measured, 2 regions -- but each region is a whole line on both sides with no shared suffix, so the additive resolution is correct and total, which is the property the one-line row bought and this shape keeps. Worked repair: `gunbc.rung_drop`, whose 30 rows are 30 files under `dag/gunbc/rung_drop/` enumerated by `gunbc.rung_drop.roster`. THE SHARED TEXT IS NOT ZERO AND THE HONEST COUNT IS THE ENUMERATIONS: a new drop still touches the roster line, and a new PROSE drop touches three further lists that exist only to keep the prose arm closed. Each is a whole line with no shared suffix, so each resolves additively -- which is the claim, and it is weaker than `disjoint edits no longer conflict`. What this does NOT establish is enforcement -- nothing refuses a second row added to one of those files, so the default is safe and the shape is not walled.)
- **a construction-restricting property read as a structure-erasing one** (a declaration is annotated to RESTRICT how its inhabitants may be built, and a consumer that asks about the declaration's SHAPE treats the annotation as a reason to stop looking -- so the deliberate climb on the construction axis silently drops a rung on every axis decided by shape, with no declaration, because the two axes are read by different consumers. **THE INVERSION IS THE CLASS**: the annotation exists to make the carrier MORE distinct from its payload, and the consumer's response is to make it INDISTINGUISHABLE from everything. Recognition rule, keyed on the SHAPE and not on any one keyword: whenever a declaration-level marker is stored in a general side-channel -- a properties list, an attribute bag, a modifier set -- find every consumer that BRANCHES ON THAT CHANNEL BEING NON-EMPTY rather than on the specific marker, and ask what each of them would have answered had the marker been absent; a consumer that answers 'opaque', 'unknown', or 'skip' for the whole channel has conflated 'this declaration carries a marker I do not model' with 'this declaration has no structure', and every future marker inherits the same silence on arrival. **SPECIMEN, with the discriminating pair** (gunbc XL-0-FLOOR, 2026-09-01): `v2.compiler.normalized_tree` `NormalizedTree` was sealed by BL-1 from an alias for `Node` into a `sole_constructor` record so that a wrapper-retention drop would be unwritable. `sole_constructor` is carried as a PROPERTY on the declaration node (`v1.compiler.parse` `parsed_sole_constructor_properties`), and `v1.compiler.infer` `exposure_view_for_node` answered `OpaqueTypeHead` for any node whose properties list was non-empty -- so `nominal_product_head_name` answered the empty string for every sealed carrier, `nominal_product_inhabitance_refusal` short-circuited on the empty head, and `declared_type_inhabitance` fell through to `Inhabits`. `v2.compiler.source_authority` `normalized_source_ast_equal_witness` then bound a `NormalizedTree` at a formal declared `Node` and the compiler ACCEPTED it: the ordinary-floor violation DESIGN section 4b names as exact application bijection, below baseline. The discriminating pair is two record declarations identical to the token except for the word `sole_constructor` -- the sealed one compiled with zero diagnostics where the plain one refused. **WHY A CENSUS MISSED IT, which is the part that generalizes**: gunbc#8886 measured 285 sites behind the `v2.*` direct-call argument-type exemption, of which 148 were `Node` against `ResolvedTree`/`ParseTree` and siblings -- every one a transparent-ALIAS false positive. This shape never appears in that roster and could not have, because it is a TRUE positive the comparison relation cannot reach: the census enumerates what the relation REFUSES, so a defect the relation is blind to is invisible to the census by construction, and a large census reads as thorough coverage of exactly the region it cannot see. **THE MISDIAGNOSIS THIS CLASS INVITES, recorded because this lane was dispatched with it**: the silence sat inside a module the exemption covers, so the exemption was the obvious cause; refuting it took running the identical program in an ordinary module and on a tree with the exemption deleted, both of which stayed silent, while the same run showed the exemption's effect on a kernel red so the arms were provably live. A plausible nearby mechanism that is genuinely broken is the most expensive wrong answer available, because repairing it changes nothing and the repair reads as coverage. **Rung: mechanically preventable, and the ceiling for this class as a class is the same rung.** The repair makes one marker structure-preserving by positive establishment -- the parser builds the identical declaration node and only the properties list differs -- and every OTHER marker in that channel stays opaque and unmeasured, which is a named residue rather than a covered class. The next-rung trigger is a CAPABILITY: markers carried in the declaration's own type rather than in an untyped side-channel, so that a consumer branching on the channel cannot compile.)
- **a two-reader disagreement census is blind to the case where BOTH readers are wrong together** (an instrument whose subject is DISAGREEMENT between two producers of one answer reports AGREEMENT as healthy, so the population where both answer the same WRONG thing is scored green and is invisible to it by construction. The trap is that such an instrument is commissioned precisely to adjudicate a known-defective population, and it can be structurally incapable of seeing that population while looking perfectly well-formed and producing rows. **SPECIMEN, measured 2026-09-01.** `v1.tests.claim.carrier_realization_census` records, per type-reference occurrence, the legacy short-circuit key and the strict authority answer, and reports where they diverge. Its calibration control -- stated in docs/plans/carrier-realization-arbiter-repair-design.md -- requires the diagnostic-producing `DivergesWithExactIdentity` subset to reproduce arm A 25 sites, joined by source declaration and enclosing declaration, never by line. Run over the transitive import closure of `src/v2/compiler/01_tokenize.dag`, the module that emits the file where every arm-A site lives contributed 76 rows: 74 Agrees, 2 IdentityUnavailable, ZERO divergences, and all 26 of its `String`-named rows Agree. Every divergence row in the closure sat in `v2.std.text`, the DECLARING module, in the opposite direction. Empty intersection with the population the control exists to find. **THE SHAPE OF THE ABSENCE IS THE DIAGNOSIS, and it is what distinguishes this class from an over-broad walk.** `gunbc#9900` predicted the walk might visit occurrences the emitter never renders; that defect shows the target population PLUS extras. This showed the target population NOT AT ALL. Same instrument, same rows, opposite conclusions -- and only the shape separates them. **WHY BOTH READERS AGREE THERE**, read off `v1.compiler.emit_rust` `checkpoint_table_bypasses_identity_note` REACHABILITY CORRECTION rather than executed here: the short-circuit returns the host spelling before consulting any authority, and the authority answers from `type_reference_decl_file`, whose fallback returns the file containing the REFERENCE. Two readers, one wrong answer, perfect agreement. **RECOGNITION RULE: any two-reader comparison cited as CORRECTNESS coverage.** A differential oracle, a twin fixture, a cross-check, a self-hosted-versus-seed comparison, an A/B over two emitters -- each answers `do these two concur`, and none answers `is the answer right`. Ask what a SHARED error would look like in its output; if the answer is `indistinguishable from health`, the instrument cannot be the correctness evidence, whatever else it is good for. **THE REMEDY IS A DIFFERENT ORACLE, NOT A REPAIR OF THIS ONE.** The adjudicating instrument has to reach OUTSIDE the pair of readers for its ground truth -- for this specimen, a census of agreement-at-a-wrong-answer joined against emitted-crate diagnostics. Widening the occurrence set, fixing the walk, or adding a third reader that shares the same defective input do not help, and the first two are what a reader who diagnosed this as an over-broad walk would have spent. **Bounded against `executed_conjunct_discriminates_nothing`**: there a live gated conjunct never meets a population that would falsify it, and one authored fixture retires it; here the instrument meets its population on every run and reports it as healthy, so no fixture over this instrument can retire it. **Bounded against `instrument_output_read_as_subject_content`**: there the instrument answers its own question faithfully and a consumer substitutes the subject; here the instrument answers ITS OWN question faithfully too, and the defect is that its question -- do the readers concur -- was never the question that was asked.)
- **a census with an unresolvable fraction of its population reports a denominator it does not have** (an occurrence census that emits a third disposition for `could not determine` is honest at the row grain and misleading at the total grain: rows scored `identity unavailable` are neither agreements nor divergences, so any ratio taken over the reported total silently treats them as decided. **SPECIMEN, unowned and unexplained at filing (2026-09-01).** The `v1.tests.claim.carrier_realization_census` run over the `src/v2/compiler/01_tokenize.dag` closure produced 121 `IdentityUnavailable` rows -- roughly a tenth of its population. The earlier run recorded in docs/plans/carrier-realization-arbiter-repair-design.md, over the same subject module at the FRONT-END phase on 2026-08-21, reported `1142 rows: 1134 Agrees, 8 DivergesWithExactIdentity, 0 IdentityUnavailable`. Zero to a tenth, across a phase change nobody predicted it for. **BOTH RUNS ARE NAMED RATHER THAN THEIR NUMBERS INHERITED**: the earlier is the front-end-phase run the design itself later withdraws as measuring the wrong phase, and the later is the typed-graph census landed by `gunbc#9900`. They are not the same instrument and the pair is recorded to locate the question, not to compute a delta from. **RECOGNITION RULE: an instrument with a `cannot answer` disposition, whose consumers aggregate over the reported total.** Ask what fraction is undecided before reading any ratio, and treat the undecided fraction as a separate finding rather than as noise. **WHY IT IS FILED WITHOUT INVESTIGATION**: identity being unavailable is a different defect from the readers disagreeing, it has no owner, and it was found while running a control for an unrelated question. A row that records it with both runs named is what stops the next reader taking the census denominator at face value.)
- **a restoration promise names a route that does not exist** (a mechanism withholds, defers or dormant-marks a population and tells the reader it becomes observable again under some named condition — and that condition names a RUN, LANE, CADENCE OR SWEEP the tree does not contain. Nothing refuses, because the promise is a string in a diagnostic rather than a citation anything resolves; the population is not silently dropped, which is what makes the class survive review — it is dropped WITH A RECEIPT, and the receipt is what stops anyone looking. **THE BOUNDARY AGAINST `unbacked_execution_claim` IS THE TENSE AND IT DECIDES THE REMEDY.** That class is prose asserting a relation that RUNS NOW; this is prose asserting a relation that WILL run — a future condition, so `git log --all -S` over the declaration form finds nothing to past-tense and the origin arms there do not apply. It is also not `absorbing_fallback`: nothing widens, the withhold is precise and correctly counted. It is 4b(3)'s trigger trap with the polarity inverted — there a trigger names LESS than the capability it restores and gets satisfied while the capability stays dead; here the trigger names a capability whose PRECONDITION IS ALREADY FALSE, so it can never be satisfied at all and the row waits forever in a state that reads as temporary. **SPECIMEN WITH A RECEIPT, measured 2026-09-01 on head 4c6c509e and unrepaired at authoring.** `v1_compiler.cli_run.required_floor_runner` `suppress_withheld` removes enrolled expected-red identities whose module sits outside the required gate, printing that their enrolment `becomes observable again when the gate roster admits the module or in the whole-corpus receipts run`. THAT RUN DOES NOT EXIST: the phrase occurs exactly once in the tree, inside the message that promises it, and the repository carries three workflows of which none is it. 39 identities across 23 modules sit under that promise. Each is enrolled on `v2.workflow.floor_expected_red`, whose own header states what an enrolment asserts — that the identity REACHES ITS SUBJECT AND ANSWERS, and that a row belongs there only while someone is fixing it — so every one of the 39 asserts `runs, fails, someone is fixing it` about a row no run reaches. The only surviving route by which one executes is the changed-witness override, i.e. somebody editing it. **THE POPULATION IS DERIVABLE AND IS DELIBERATELY NOT TRANSCRIBED HERE**: it is `v2.workflow.floor_expected_red` `floor_expected_red_roster` minus the identities whose module matches `v2.workflow.required_floor` `required_gate_prefixes` — two authorities and a set difference, so it re-derives instead of rotting. **THE SAME ROSTER'S HEADER ALREADY RECORDS THE ANCESTOR OF THIS MISTAKE**, which is why it is a class: 101 rows were held there as agreement while never reaching their subject, and were reclassified into `v2.workflow.floor_route_gap` on 2026-08-20 once `ExpectedRedArm` was taught to refuse `HostEffectRefused`, `HostToolUnresolved` and an interrupted budget. That repair closed the arm where a NON-VERDICT was read as agreement; this class is the same harm one step earlier, where a row never reaches an arm at all and a sentence promises it will. **RECOGNITION RULE: whenever a diagnostic says a withheld thing becomes observable again `in`/`under`/`by` some named run, grep the tree for that name and require an executing consumer — a workflow job, a scheduled entry point, an actuator argv. If the only occurrence is the promise itself, the population is dormant forever and the honest states are two: admit the row cannot be observed on any cadence, or delete the enrolment. **RUNG: 1 (mitigatable) — the withhold is counted and located, which is the whole of what holds. CEILING: 3, since `whether a named route exists` is decidable from the workflow and entry-point authorities the tree already carries. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, a CAPABILITY and not an artifact: restoration conditions expressed as a resolvable citation to an executing consumer rather than as prose, so a promise naming no route fails to compile — writing this particular sentence better retires nothing.)**SECOND SPECIMEN, AND IT MOVES THE CLASS FROM A WITHHOLD TO A GREEN-BY-EXECUTION RECEIPT, measured 2026-09-05 at head 4da9c1a3fdd.** The subject here is not a suppressed expected-red but the repository's WEAK SELF-HOST BEHAVIORAL RECEIPT: `test.claim.self_host_logic_behavioral_witness` `self_host_logic_behavioral_receipt_holds`, whose transport `tools.self_host_logic_behavioral_transport` emits `dag/std/logic.dag` with the real `gunbc` binary, compiles the emitted Rust against the seed crate, and runs the resulting binary plain and `--inject-fault` — DESIGN section 7 equivalence-by-execution with its own discriminating RED, landed as Wave 1 Gate 4. IT IS CLASSIFIED ONTO A CADENCE THAT EXECUTES NOTHING: `FalsifierSelfHostWet`, whose sole scheduled executor `falsifier.yml` was deleted at 611fd02770 (#8283, 2026-08-15); `std.witness_admission` `witness_cadence_has_scheduled_route` answers false for it, and the tree is CORRECT about this at every carrier — the witness file says so in its own header, `v2.compiler.self_host.wet_receipt_enrollment` records the cadence flag's deletion, and `gunbc.deleted_cadence_reference_census` censuses it as PremiseInvalidated. WHAT MAKES IT THIS CLASS RATHER THAN AN HONEST DARK LANE: the classification is not a promise of restoration in prose, it is a LIVE ROUTING DECISION that names a cadence the tree does not contain, and the receipt it routes is CITED AS COVERAGE ELSEWHERE — `docs/plans/v2-self-hosting.md` lists Wave 1 gate 4 as RECEIPT LANDED. A landed receipt with no executor is the inert-lens state DESIGN section 4b names worse than absent, and the citation is what stops anyone looking, exactly as this row's first specimen describes. THE BOUNDARY THAT KEPT IT UNFILED: every carrier is individually honest — nobody claims the cadence runs — so a reader auditing any ONE of them finds a correct statement, and only the JOIN of `this receipt is cited as gate coverage` with `this cadence has no route` is false. Restoring a scheduled route for the cadence is reserved to operator agreement by `std.witness_admission` `witness_cadence_has_scheduled_route_note`, so the repair is not a lane's to take unilaterally, which is why the standing must be visible here rather than resolved quietly.)
- **an instrument resolves an alias, lands on a name that COLLIDES with a kernel spelling, and attributes the result to the kernel** (the reference under measurement is authored QUALIFIED and points at a type alias; the reader resolves the alias, arrives at the alias declaration's own name, and that name is also a kernel name -- so the row is attributed to the kernel identity, naming THE EXACT CARRIER THE QUALIFICATION WAS WRITTEN TO MOVE AWAY FROM. **IT DOES NOT FAIL TO ANSWER; IT ANSWERS CONFIDENTLY AND WRONGLY**, and that is the whole severity: a missing answer is visible to its consumer and an inverted one is not, so the two rank completely differently and only the second can be cited as evidence for the opposite of what holds. **Distinct from `instrument_output_read_as_subject_content`, and the boundary is worth stating because they were found in the same instrument in the same hour**: there a report is COMPLETE for the reporter and read as complete for the consumer, so the defect is in the READING; here the report is wrong on a specific row it does examine, so the defect is in the INSTRUMENT and no amount of careful reading recovers it. Also distinct from `hollow_alias`, which is about a second name minted for one concept: the alias here is legitimate modelling, and what fails is a reader of it. **SPECIMEN, with a discriminating probe executed on covered ground (2026-09-01, gunbc#9907 follow-up).** `v1.tests.claim.carrier_realization_census` reports, for two functions appended to `v2.compiler.tokenize` on adjacent lines with identical position kind, differing only in alias-versus-concrete: a param authored `std.determinism.Determinism` keeps its dotted name, while a param authored `v2.std.text.String` comes back as bare `String` attributed to `<kernel:String>`. `v2.std.text` declares `type String = FreeMonoid<Char>`, so the alias name collides with the kernel spelling. Both probes produced rows (17661 -> 17663), which is what makes the negative half evidence rather than absence -- the positive control is the whole discriminator, and the same census had already produced a null result on an uncovered fixture whose emptiness was indistinguishable from confirmation. **CONSEQUENCE MEASURED, NOT IMAGINED**: read naively, the census contradicted the namespace wall about nine function declarations and would have been read as nine admission rows asserting a binding the carrier lacks, on a merged PR. The wall was right; the contradiction was this bug. **RUNG: MECHANICALLY PREVENTABLE AT BEST TODAY AND CURRENTLY UNENROLLED** -- the wrong answer is produced silently and nothing executes over it. The next-rung trigger is that two-line probe being ENROLLED as a discriminating RED in any covered module, which is cheap; a class whose RED is two lines and still does not execute has no excuse, and naming the cheapness is what makes it rank rather than sit. **Recognition rule**: wherever a reader resolves through an alias, ask whether the resolved name could collide with a name in a DIFFERENT namespace the reader also knows -- kernel, prelude, or ambient -- because at that point the reader cannot tell them apart and will pick one silently.)
- **restored bytes reviewed as authorship** (a clobber repair returns content a prior PR already merged and a prior reviewer already accepted, but a diff has no way to say so: the restored lines are ADDED LINES, so a reviewer attributes them to the repairing PR and reviews them as new work. Specimen: gunbc#9937 restored two rows that gunbc#9868 had reverted off a stale base, and review 58203 filed REQUEST_CHANGES against a DESIGN section 6 transcribed-count defect in bytes that are character-for-character gunbc#9888's merged row -- a finding correct on its merits and misaddressed by one PR. **THE SECOND-ORDER EFFECT IS WHY THIS IS A CLASS AND NOT AN ANECDOTE: it makes clobber repairs EXPENSIVE TO LAND, which biases the fleet toward leaving clobbers standing.** The repairing lane inherits review debt for content it did not write, and the only way to discharge it inside the repair is to edit another lane's cells from a base lacking their context -- which is precisely the failure the repair exists to undo, committed a second time with better manners. RECOGNITION RULE: a diff whose added lines are byte-identical to a prior merged blob. It is DECIDABLE rather than a judgement call -- `cmp` against `git show <sha>:<path>` answers it, and provenance for the whole region comes from `git log -S` naming the commit that added the text and the commit that removed it. THE REMEDY IS THE AUTHOR'S AND IT IS CHEAP: state the restoration ahead of the diff and carry the byte-identity receipt, so a reviewer meets provenance before content; without it the same finding is re-filed against the same bytes on every re-review, and the loop is paid for twice. A finding against restored bytes is well-formed and BINDS THE DESTINATION: provenance decides who answers it and in which diff, and never whether it is answered. **THE WRONG READING OF THIS ROW WAS AUTHORED AND REFUSED IN ITS OWN FILING** (review 58220): an earlier revision said such a finding 'belongs in its own diff', which reads as licence to merge a known defect behind an authorship argument -- DESIGN section 5's hard reject, and inverted, since a restoration is exactly the motion that puts the bytes back on main. What actually happened on the specimen is the rule: the reviewer's section 6 finding was correct, the repairing lane argued for routing it elsewhere, the owning manager OVERRULED that, and the defect was repaired inside the restoration under a ruling -- which is what made the edit a decision rather than one lane rewriting another's cells. Routing is a question about AUTHORITY and context, decided by whoever owns both lanes; the bytes are reviewed at their destination either way. **Rung: mitigatable** -- the author states the restoration and carries a byte-identity receipt, and a reviewer may still miss it. **Ceiling: mechanically preventable**, because the recognition rule is decidable: a tool that joins each added line against the merged blobs of the commits git log -S names can annotate a diff as restored before a human reads it. It is not structural -- nothing can make a restoration unwritable, nor should it. **Next-rung trigger, at capability grain: the review surface distinguishes restored bytes from authored bytes without the author asserting it**, since an author-supplied notice is exactly the evidence a tired or adversarial diff will omit. **THE RECEIPT MUST BE TAKEN AT THE DESTINATION AND NOT AT THE SOURCE, and that is the transferable half.** `cmp` decides the git case because it reads the bytes where they LANDED, not the bytes an author meant to send; the general shape is ANY step between assertion and delivery altering content while the delivery reports success -- and the step is not always the one a reader would blame first. Measured the same day in three places, and the THIRD one is a caution about naming the culprit too early: git's merge region dropped a row outside the conflict markers; a review read merged bytes as authored ones; and two sessions' messages arrived with a backticked identifier deleted and a dollar-variable replaced by a plausible shell path, which BOTH of them first attributed to the message transport. That attribution was wrong, and it was settled by measurement rather than by reasoning: a third session kept the local source of a sent message, read the delivered body back out of the store, and diffed them -- identical but for a trailing newline, with backticks, a backslash continuation and a dollar-brace token all intact. The corruption was in the SENDER'S OWN SHELL, expanding an inline double-quoted argument before the tool ever saw it, and the proposed remedy of banning backticks would have cost a real notation to avoid a hazard that lives in a construction nobody has to use. A deletion leaves a gap a reader might notice; a substitution leaves a syntactically fine claim that is simply false, which is why source-side confidence is not evidence at all.)
- **a stale-buffer write reverts another author's landed change, and the reversion is invisible in the reverting diff** (a whole-file write derived from a buffer that no longer matches the ref it is committed against silently drops whatever landed in between). **THE HARM IS THAT THE REVERSION IS UNREVIEWABLE BY CONSTRUCTION, not merely unnoticed:** a diff is computed against the state already lost, and the clobber is not part of the change, so the diff a careful author reviews looks EXACTLY like the change they intended. Specimen, and it ran in BOTH directions on one file inside one hour: on gunbc#10114, eae0ccae carried a new truncation section and zero of three corrections landed at b561d7c4; the repairing 9bdd91c3 carried all three corrections and zero of the truncation section. No commit on that branch carried both until afb2dd37. Two reviewers each read their own diff carefully and each missed the other's loss. **TWO DIFFERENT MECHANISMS, ONE CLASS**, which is why the row is about the buffer and not about scripting: one side was a script reading a stale working tree; the other was a hand edit from a REUSED worktree whose `git log` line printed the correct SHA while the file on disk did not match it -- so a correct-looking provenance check passed while the bytes were wrong. **RECOGNITION RULE, and it is the observable tell rather than a judgement:** a later diff shows a THIRD party's earlier hunk as a NEW change, which is impossible if that hunk survived. It is found by reading a diff for what it implies about the OTHER side, not for what it says about yours. **THE FLEET CONDITION THAT MAKES THIS LIKELIER HERE:** shared worktrees plus a shared git identity, so neither authorship nor a log line tells a reader whether the bytes in front of them are current -- `gunbc-ci-auto-heal` authors commits for many sessions, and a surprise commit on your own branch is not evidence of a stranger. Adjacent but NOT the same class as `restored_bytes_reviewed_as_authorship`: that row is about a clobber REPAIR being misread as authorship at REVIEW time; this is about the clobber being invisible in the clobberer's own diff at AUTHORING time -- different mechanism, different detection point, different repair. **Rung: mitigatable** -- caught after the fact, by inference, and only because someone read a diff against the wrong expectation. **Ceiling: mechanically preventable, and no higher, with the reason:** the invalid state stays writable because nothing structurally prevents a process from holding a stale buffer, and a write tool cannot know which content a caller MEANT to preserve. **Next-rung trigger, at capability grain: a file write asserts on the RESULT before committing it** -- every claim the artifact must carry is present in the buffer, so a missing marker fails closed rather than reverting. Deliberately NOT phrased as any lane adding such an assertion by hand: both lanes on the specimen built one AFTER the fact, and a trigger satisfied by one session's discipline retires the row while every other session stays exposed. The capability is the assertion being the DEFAULT in whatever writes files on this fleet.)
- **a review summary that inverts real roles and then affirms the join** (a review names files that all exist, assigns each the wrong role, and closes by asserting the one property it was uniquely positioned to test. Specimen: review 58215 approved gunbc#9937 at `fafd2adc7e1` with *'restoration of two ledger rows to recurring_failure_mode.dag ... plus an accompanying prose update in the gap-analysis doc ... Diff is narrow and matches the PR title.'* The carrier held ONE added row, not two; that row was a NEW class row, not a restoration of anything; the actual restoration was the docs file it demoted to 'accompanying'; and the diff did NOT match the title, because the `.dag` file had been swept in by a staged-change leak the PR body never declared. **DISTINGUISH IT FROM A HALLUCINATED SUBJECT, which is the neighbouring failure and a much easier one:** review 58070 described a `match` arm that appeared nowhere in its diff, and an invention is refutable by grep. Here EVERY NOUN IS PRESENT IN THE TREE and only the relations between them are wrong, so the usual defence -- check that the cited things exist -- returns all-clear. **THE AFFIRMATION IS THE HARM AND NOT THE MISDESCRIPTION.** A lane cannot audit its own title-versus-diff agreement with fresh eyes; a reviewer can, and this one asserted that agreement in the exact PR where it failed. A review that stays silent on a property leaves the check undone; a review that affirms it wrongly marks the check DONE, which is worse and is why this is not merely a low-quality summary. RECOGNITION RULE, and it is decidable rather than a matter of reading care: join the changed-FILE set against what the title and body claim to change, at file grain, and refuse any file the body does not account for. That join is mechanical, needs no understanding of the diff, and would have fired here. **THE BACKSTOP QUESTION IS THE SECOND HALF OF THE ROW, and its answer is honest rather than comforting:** the leaked roster row carried no regenerated `DESIGN.md` or `docs/design-ledgers.md`, and by construction the required build lane's generated-artifact phase compares every `CommitRequired` projection against its authority, so it WOULD have refused. It did not get the chance -- that lane was CANCELLED on the leaked sha when the next push superseded it, and the leak was caught by the author re-reading the diff. So the backstop is real and was not what caught it, and a row claiming the gate held here would be asserting an execution that never ran. **Rung: mitigatable** -- the harm is contained only by a second reader noticing, and on the specimen the second reader was the author. **Ceiling: mechanically preventable**, and no higher: whether a summary DESCRIBES a diff correctly is not decidable, so no construction forbids the bad summary -- but the one clause that did the damage is decidable, because a changed-file set and a body are both machine-readable. **Next-rung trigger, at capability grain: no review verdict is admissible unless a changed-FILE-set join against the declared scope has executed and passed** -- not a reviewer instructed to check it, which is the same assertion that failed here, and not a lint on summary wording, which would ratchet the prose while leaving the join undone.)
- **fabricated debt** (a declaration is authored for an exposure that does not exist, or that is already declared elsewhere, so the ledger grows a row whose trigger CANNOT FIRE because there is nothing to restore. It is the exact inverse of the unfiled gap and it is the more comfortable mistake: filing reads as diligence, the row survives review because every reviewer checks whether a declared drop is HONEST about its loss and none checks whether the loss is REAL, and the cost lands on whoever later tries to retire it. **THE HARM IS NOT MERELY CLUTTER, WHICH IS WHY IT IS A CLASS.** A drop is retired BY ITS TRIGGER AND BY NOTHING ELSE, so a row for a non-existent exposure is permanent by construction; it inflates the standing-drops list that DESIGN projects on every turn of every session, and the next reader plans against an exposure nobody has. A row DUPLICATING an existing declaration is worse still: it forks the single authority for one safety fact, so the two can be retired independently and the tree ends up asserting both that the class is dropped and that it is restored. **THE ORIGIN IS ALWAYS THE SAME, and naming it is the recognition rule: the population was read from a PARAGRAPH rather than measured from the tree.** A loss clause enumerates what was unguarded on the day it was written; capabilities return, get re-homed, or acquire their own declarations, and the sentence does not move. So the rule is: before authoring a drop, resolve every named item against the tree -- is this exposure live NOW, and does it already have a row? **TWO RECEIPTS, both caught before authoring, 2026-09-01, while splitting `gunbc.rung_drop` `floor_cut`'s six-capability loss into capability-grain rows.** (1) GENERATED-ARTIFACT DRIFT was on the loss clause's list and is RESTORED -- gunbc#9415 enrolled a required `generated-artifact` phase adjudicating every member of `committed_generated_artifacts`; the same paragraph elsewhere admits the phase took it off the list, so the row contradicted itself and a reader following the enumeration would have declared a drop for a live gate. It is now stated as a FRACTION, 1 of 7 discharged and 6 outstanding, because a population that shrinks silently cannot be checked. (2) THE PER-WITNESS EVAL DEADLINE was on the same list and ALREADY CARRIES ITS OWN DROP at `gunbc.witness_row_cost` `gunbc_ci_fast_lane_rule_note`; a second row would have been the section 3 fork, and it is cited from the sibling rows rather than re-declared. Six items on the paragraph, five real rows. **NEIGHBOURS, and the boundaries are what keep this from absorbing them.** Distinct from `unbacked_execution_claim`: there prose asserts a live relation that no authority backs, and the subject EXISTS while the claim about it is false; here the subject itself is absent and the claim of loss is the fabrication. Distinct from `rung inflation`, which reports a class as SAFER than its evidence supports -- this reports the tree as MORE EXPOSED than it is, and both mislead the same reader in opposite directions. Distinct from `restoration_promise_names_a_route_that_does_not_exist`, which is a REAL exposure with an unsatisfiable trigger; this is an unreal exposure, and the two are found by the same question asked at different ends -- does the trigger name something that exists, and does the LOSS name something that exists. **RUNG: 1 (mitigatable) -- caught by a reader resolving the population, which is exactly what happened here and is not a mechanism. CEILING: 3, since `does this exposure exist` is decidable for any loss expressed as a citation to a mechanism rather than as prose. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, a CAPABILITY: a drop row whose lost capability is carried as a resolvable citation to the mechanism that provided it, so a row declaring the loss of something the tree still has -- or something another row already declares -- fails to compile. Enumerating populations more carefully by hand does not retire it; it only executes this row's recognition rule once more.)
- **a YAML inline comment is ingested as scalar content** (a bounded YAML reader that does not model comment separation accepts `key: value # comment` and stores `value # comment` as the value, so a syntactically valid document is silently assigned the wrong tree. Measured 2026-09-01 against `extdeps.languages.yaml.ingest` `ingest_yaml_source`: a column-zero full-line comment and an indented full-line comment both refused loudly as malformed mapping entries; an inline trailing comment was accepted and reserialized as a quoted scalar containing the comment; a hash-leading line inside a block literal was accepted and preserved as content. The last arm is the discriminating control because generated workflow run blocks contain shell comments and issue references such as `#5828`; a line-wide hash filter would reject or delete real scalar content. Repair: full-line comments are skipped only at parser block boundaries, never in literal collection, while recognizable unquoted inline comments now produce `YamlIngestRejected` until lexical inline separation is modeled. **APPROXIMATION DIRECTION:** the temporary predicate intentionally over-refuses richer unquoted scalar forms containing separation-whitespace (space or tab) followed by hash that this bounded parser does not model, excludes double-quoted scalars, and never examines block-literal content; it does not claim full YAML comment lexing. Rung: mitigatable -- the silent wrong tree becomes a typed refusal. Ceiling: structurally guaranteed once comment separation is a lexical production in the YAML grammar, so comment bytes cannot enter scalar content except through quoted or block scalar productions. Next-rung trigger: inline YAML 1.2.2 comment separation modeled lexically rather than recognized by the temporary value-text refusal.
- **accepted source emits uncompilable target** (INVALID STATE: a .dag construction the front end accepts with zero blocking diagnostics, whose emission is a target program the target compiler refuses. The instance filed here is a coproduct's UNIT VARIANT standing in a NON-APPLIED TYPE POSITION -- a field, parameter or return type spelled with a constructor rather than a type. HARM: section 5 silent wrongness on the source-to-target path. gunbc says Accepted and hands over a program that cannot build, so the only wall that fires belongs to the target's compiler; where a target has no such wall, or where the emitted artifact is never compiled, nothing fires at all. Section 7 makes this the seed's floor and not the target's problem: the .dag graph is the authority and Rust is one realization, so 'rustc catches it' is exactly the outsourcing this project exists to end. THE SPECIMEN IS CARRIED IN THIS ROW RATHER THAN CITED, because the fixture that produced it was never committed to this repository (neat-otter-332, 2026-09-01).
