Its emitted artifacts are held by that lane's own receipts; no path is given here, since a path outside this repository is not a citation a later reader can resolve. Authored source, two modules: `scope.provider` declares `type Quantity = Time | Memory`; `scope.consumer` does `import scope.provider { Quantity, Time }`, `type NonApplied { value: Time }`, `fn field_as_quantity(subject: NonApplied) -> Quantity { subject.value }`. gunbc accepts it. DISTINGUISHING FACT, and the reason this class must not be read off the target's error code: WHAT rustc says is decided by what else the emitter minted, not by the source defect. Under the NARROW emitter classifier the consumer emits `pub use crate::scope_provider::{Quantity};` then `use crate::scope_provider::Quantity::{Time};` with no marker binding, and `pub struct NonApplied { pub value: Time }` refuses `error[E0573]: expected type, found variant Time` at `src/scope_consumer.rs:15:16` on `pub value: Time,`, label `not a type`, help `consider importing this struct instead`. Under the BROAD classifier the consumer instead emits `pub use crate::scope_provider::{Time};` beside `{Quantity}` -- the provider having emitted `pub enum Quantity { Time, Memory }` AND `pub struct Time;` -- so the field declaration COMPILES, and the refusal moves to the function: `error[E0308]: mismatched types` at `src/scope_consumer.rs:20:5` on `subject.value.clone()`, `expected Quantity, found Time`, against `expected Quantity because of return type`. INDEPENDENTLY REPRODUCED AT PARAMETER POSITION on this branch, 2026-09-01 on gunbc baeabbbf80, by a single-file source handed to the compiler: `type StampMode = StampClass | StampOther` with `fn take(stamp: StampClass) -> StampMode { stamp }`. `gunbc compile --target rust` exits 0 with 0 blocking errors, emits `pub fn take(stamp: StampClass) -> StampMode` beside `pub struct StampClass;`, and `cargo check` on the emitted crate refuses `E0308 expected StampMode, found StampClass`. One source defect; E0573, E0308-at-the-parent, and E0308-at-the-body depending on emission. THE MASK IS THE SHARP HALF, and it is fabricated plausible output rather than a lucky green: the broad classifier's marker import made the FIELD-ONLY source compile by substituting a distinct struct for a variant. It never preserved the modelled meaning, and extending the SAME accepted source across its declared parent boundary -- the function returning `Quantity` -- is what exposes it. So the narrow classifier's E0573 is this class becoming VISIBLE, not a regression the narrowing introduced. GENERAL FORM: a green obtained because the emitter manufactured a target-only entity for a source name is not evidence the source is well-typed, and the discriminator is to extend the source past the boundary the manufactured entity does not model. THE ONE .dag-SIDE SIGNAL IS ABOUT THE WRONG QUESTION: on the parameter reproduction the compile printed the ADVISORY `unlisted import use 'StampClass' (referenced but not in any import's name list)`. It fires because the name was not found among types -- the front end reached the exact fact that decides this case and reported it as import hygiene. It is not a partial wall: it is advisory, it names listing rather than type position, and the field specimen above IMPORTS `Time` explicitly, so it does not fire there at all (see `diagnostic_name_mechanism_silent`). RUNG FOUND AT: below the ladder, established by execution at the emission boundary -- the compile accepts and the emitted crate refuses. Section 4b's rung-1 mitigations are absent: nothing at the .dag boundary is typed, located or countable about this construction. CEILING: 4, structurally impossible, and the reason is that constructor identity and type identity are two distinct modelled facts whose membership is decidable from the coproduct declaration -- a variant name is reachable from that declaration as an ARM and never as a type, so the type-position slot has no constructor that admits it. No undecidable predicate is involved, so this is a wall now and not a ratchet. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, PHRASED AT CLASS GRAIN, phrased as the capability that retires the row rather than an artifact that would contribute to one: FRONT-END TYPE AGREEMENT for every construction whose emission carries a type obligation, sufficient that NO Accepted program emits a target program its compiler refuses on a type ground. That capability has one component per mechanism this row carries, and the row retires only when ALL of them hold: (i) type-position name resolution that consults the TYPE namespace alone and refuses a name resolving to a constructor with a located diagnostic, sufficient that no Accepted program contains a variant name in any non-applied type position -- field, parameter or return; (ii) arm-type agreement across a match, sufficient that no Accepted program mixes a coproduct constructor with a kernel literal across the arms of one match; (iii) agreement between the two renderings of an arrow at a call argument, sufficient that no Accepted program passes a function VALUE where the emitted parameter's rendering cannot accept it; (iv) agreement between the carrier a declared type names and the carrier the primitive families over that type are defined on, across BOTH the accepting and the emitting path, sufficient that no Accepted program emits a primitive call whose argument carrier its own runtime cannot accept; (v) exhaustiveness that reads a pattern to its LEAVES, sufficient that no Accepted program marks a variant covered on the strength of an arm restricting one of its fields to a single inner constructor; (vi) a constructor roster that distinguishes a genuine open scalar, a closed single-constructor product and an unresolved lookup, sufficient BOTH that no Accepted program has an unresolved type adjudicated as open AND that a closed single-constructor product is adjudicated closed, its total destructure accepted rather than refused -- the two halves of that specimen's own restoration condition, which are separate failures in opposite directions and are not discharged by either alone; (vii) a COMPLETE per-target realization of a declared optional -- its type spelling, its constructors, its return convention, and its representation as a collection element -- sufficient that no Accepted program emits, for any enrolled target, a module that target's compiler refuses. The four parts are enumerated rather than summarised as one carrier decision because the specimen measured them as four separate seams and NO source receipt establishes that one producer owns all four: the optional TYPE SPELLING is undefined on the Python arm and is `[]*int64` on the Go arm; the CONSTRUCTOR `Present` is undefined on both; the RETURN CONVENTION returns two values from single-return Go signatures; and the COLLECTION-ELEMENT representation declares `[]*int64` while returning `[]interface{}`. Naming them as one mechanism would assert a shared root the measurement does not carry, and would let a repair to any one of them read as discharging the component. (viii) use-line derivation that reads the EMITTED reference set rather than the module's source references, sufficient that no emitted module carries a `use` line for a provider its own emitted bytes never path into and that no provider path is spelled by the emitter rather than observed in what the emitter produced. THE PYTHON ARM OF THAT SPECIMEN DOES NOT INHABIT THIS ROW, AND COMPONENT (vii) NO LONGER PRETENDS IT DOES. This row's invalid state, stated in its first line, requires the target compiler to REFUSE. The Python measurement is that `py_compile` PASSES and `import` SUCCEEDS, the failure arriving only when annotations are evaluated or the function is called -- so on the class's own identity that arm is a NONMEMBER, and my earlier wording of (vii) ('or, in the Python arm, one it silently ACCEPTS') was a trigger component widened to admit it. A trigger component cannot make a nonmember fit a class: the retirement condition would then range over a state the identity excludes, and the class would read as covering a failure it does not define. (vii) is therefore scoped to the compiler-refusal identity, which the GO arm of the same specimen does satisfy. WHAT REMAINS OPEN, AND WHY IT IS NOT DECIDED HERE: the Python paragraph was appended by an earlier lane (still-swift-363, cool-ferret-679) and its membership defect is PRE-EXISTING -- my change did not create it, it made it legible by trying to cover it. Its two honest dispositions both exceed a specimen append: broaden and rename this class, its opening invariant, its projection and its class-grain trigger to cover an accepted source emitting a target realization that cannot uphold the source type contract even where compilation and import succeed; or home that specimen in a class whose identity already includes that state. Either touches this row's identity and every citation of it, so it is recorded as an open question against the row rather than performed inside a change that was appending a different specimen. The paragraph is deliberately left standing and unedited: deleting another lane's measured receipt to make this row internally consistent would destroy the evidence the disposition needs. THE ROSTER IS MAINTAINED BY A RULE, NOT BY A COUNT, and that rule exists because this roster has now been WRONG TWICE in the same direction. It first read as component (i) alone while the population had grown to three. Then, appending the carrier mechanism, I extended it to (iv) and left the sentence claiming one component per mechanism the row carries -- while nested patterns, the constructor-roster conflation and the per-target optional emission were all already documented here and none was covered. Both errors retire the row early, and both are the same shape: a completeness claim asserted at a moment, about a population that grows underneath it. THE RULE: appending a specimen whose mechanism is not already a component OBLIGES adding one, in the same change. The roster is the row's retirement condition, so a specimen documented without a component is a failure the row would retire while leaving authorable -- and the appending lane is the only one that knows the mechanism is new. Reviewers can check this mechanically: every specimen paragraph names a mechanism, and every mechanism must appear in the list above.Repairing any specimen, narrowing the emitter classifier, or adding a fixture satisfies less than any one component and retires no part of this row. WHY THE TRIGGER IS STATED THIS WAY, recorded because the row previously said otherwise and a reviewer was right to refuse it: the trigger read as (i) ALONE while the row's population had grown to three mechanisms, so satisfying it would have RETIRED THE ROW WHILE TWO OF ITS OWN DOCUMENTED FAILURES REMAINED AUTHORABLE -- DESIGN section 4b(3)'s grain mismatch exactly, a plural loss under a singular trigger. Noting the mismatch inside the row, which is what the function-value paragraph originally did, DOCUMENTS the defect rather than repairing it: a trigger that a row's own prose admits is too narrow is still the trigger the row will be retired by, since a drop is retired BY ITS TRIGGER AND BY NOTHING ELSE. RECOGNITION RULE: when the target compiler names a symbol at a type position, check whether that symbol is declared as an ARM of a coproduct in the source; if it is, the defect is in accepted .dag and the target compiler is the only wall that fired. SCOPE STATED RATHER THAN GENERALISED: executed for a unit arm at a field type and at a parameter type, Rust target only. Record-shaped arms, applied positions such as `List<Time>`, and other targets were not measured and are not claimed.) **SECOND SPECIMEN, AND ITS SUBJECT IS THE CHANGE THAT WAS CONSTRUCTING THE DISTINCTION IT VIOLATED** (quick-crab-396, 2026-09-02, the type_reference_decl_file state-space split). v1.tests.claim.carrier_realization_census identity_query_provenance declares a return type of std.coercion TypeDeclarationProvenance and answers it in five arms: three return the bound provenance and two -- IdentitiesDisagree and IdentityUnavailableBoth -- returned the empty string. The front end ACCEPTED it with zero blocking diagnostics, emit produced the mirror, and rustc refused the mirror at E0308 `match arms have incompatible types`, expected Rc<TypeDeclarationProvenance>, found String. THE SPECIMEN IS WORTH MORE THAN THE FIX because of what the two bad arms were: this change exists to replace `decl_file: String` with a coproduct precisely so that ABSENT declaration identity is a constructor (DeclarationIdentityAbsent) instead of an empty string -- so the uncompilable arms were the exact conflation the change was built to make unwritable, surviving inside the change that makes it unwritable. RECOGNITION RULE, and it is narrower than 'run the emitter': a match whose arms MIX a coproduct constructor with a kernel literal is the shape, and it is invisible to a reader who checks that every arm is present, because exhaustiveness holds and only the arm TYPES disagree. WHAT THE ROW ESTABLISHES ABOUT THE RUNG: the class is still mechanically preventable and not structural -- the refusal fired in the TARGET compiler, one phase and one language too late, and the .dag front end that had every fact needed to refuse it did not. The next-rung trigger is unchanged and this instance sharpens rather than moves it. (AMENDED WHEN THE FUNCTION-VALUE INSTANCE LANDED, and the amendment is to the trigger's GRAIN rather than to this specimen's reading: the trigger has since been restated at class grain with one component per mechanism, and THIS specimen is component (ii). The sentence above stays because it was right about what the specimen does -- it sharpens rather than moves -- and what changed is that the row stopped stating one mechanism's trigger as though it were the class's.) **A FURTHER INSTANCE, AND IT MASKS BY A DIFFERENT MECHANISM FROM THE FIELD AND PARAMETER SPECIMENS -- CANDIDATE EVIDENCE, NOT A RUNG.** (Deliberately not numbered: this row gained a specimen from another lane while this one was in flight, and an ordinal written at authoring time is wrong the moment a second lane appends. The specimens are named by their shape.) Executed by snappy-koi-286, 2026-09-02, and carried in gunbc#9989 (`The function-value adapter, judged by rustc`), which is OPEN at this writing -- so this points at the PR carrying the fixtures rather than asserting paths that resolve on main today. SUBJECT: the function-value adapter at the call position in `v1.compiler.emit_rust` `rust_call_arg_function_value_adapt`. An arrow type has TWO Rust renderings -- a function VALUE renders through `callable_type_template` as a reference-counted dyn Fn, a function-typed PARAMETER renders through `emit_rust_param_type` as `impl Fn(..) -> .. + Clone` -- and the reference-counted form carries no blanket Fn impl the way a boxed one does. The adapter closes that seam, and it fires ONLY when the argument is spelled as a CALL EXPRESSION. SOURCE HANDED TO THE COMPILER, three declarations in one import-free module: `fn let_add(k: Int) -> fn(Int) -> Int` returning a closure over `k`; `fn let_apply(f: fn(Int) -> Int, x: Int) -> Int` applying it; and a probe that binds `let_add(k: 1)` TO A LET AND THEN PASSES THE BINDING to `let_apply`. GUNBC'S VERDICT: ACCEPTED, zero blocking diagnostics -- and that is established BY THE ROUTE REFUSING TO REPORT IT OTHERWISE, not by reading a success line: `v1_compiler.emitted_closure_compile_host` `FixtureClosureOutcome` separates `SourceRefused` from `Measured`, a `SourceRefused` fails the pair rather than producing a rustc verdict, and this arm reports Measured. RUSTC'S VERDICT ON THE EMITTED CRATE: cargo completes non-zero with error[E0277], expected a `Fn(i64)` closure, found `Rc<dyn Fn(i64) -> i64>`, attributed to the FIXTURE'S OWN emitted module at the call rather than to some other member of the closure, and the note reads required by a bound in `let_apply` -- THE BOUND IS ON THE CONSUMER, `pub fn let_apply(f: impl Fn(i64) -> i64 + Clone, x: i64)`, which is the half that makes this the two-renderings SEAM and not a random type error. THE POSITIVE CONTROL IS WHAT MAKES THIS AN INSTANCE OF THE CLASS RATHER THAN A BROKEN FIXTURE, and it is the sharp half: a sibling fixture is THE SAME PROGRAM WITH ONE AUTHORED DIFFERENCE -- the producer's result passed directly as a call expression instead of through the let binding -- and it emits and compiles clean. Same producer, same consumer, same call; the only variable is whether the adapter fired. INSTRUMENT NAMED RATHER THAN TRANSCRIBED, per DESIGN section 6: the opt-in pair authored in that same OPEN PR as an ignored `v1-compiler` lib test naming the function-value adapter fixture-closure discrimination -- described rather than spelled, because an identity under review may be renamed before it lands, and a bare name that no longer resolves reads as correct; three runs on three bases agree, the latest on that PR's integrated head. WHAT IT ADDS TO THE CLASS, and it is why it is filed rather than folded into the field and parameter specimens: those mask through a MANUFACTURED TARGET ENTITY -- the emitter minting a target-only struct for a source name -- so their discriminator is to extend the source past the boundary that entity does not model. This one masks through a CALL-SITE SPELLING: one source meaning emits compilable or uncompilable target depending on whether an INTERMEDIATE BINDING WAS AUTHORED, with no manufactured entity anywhere. So the class has a second discriminator, and it is cheap: RE-SPELL AN ACCEPTED CALL THROUGH A LET AND RE-EMIT. WHAT THIS INSTANCE DOES NOT ESTABLISH, stated because the consumer's standing makes it easy to overclaim: the pair is `#[ignore]`d, so `cargo test --release -p v1-compiler --lib` does not select it and nothing executes over it on ANY path. It is CANDIDATE EVIDENCE. THE CONCLUSION RESTS ON THAT ONE FACT AND ON NOTHING ELSE, and it is written that way because an earlier revision rested it on two, the second being a claim that the `rust-unit-tests` job stood OUTSIDE the required aggregate. gunbc#10078 made that second clause FALSE, and it is REMOVED rather than softened -- a sentence reading as two independent protections is the dangerous shape, because deleting the `#[ignore]` would leave a reader believing the needs-clause still covered them. The lane IS required now, so un-ignoring this pair is BY ITSELF sufficient to put it on the acceptance path and change what it establishes. It establishes no rung and it discharges no component of the next-rung trigger above -- including component (iii), which is the component this instance's own mechanism names: exhibiting a failure is not building the wall against it. SCOPE THIS INSTANCE ADDS, stated locally so the row keeps ONE scope sentence rather than two that could drift: a function-typed argument passed through a let binding, Rust target only. It widens the scope sentence above by that one position and claims nothing beyond it. **A FURTHER INSTANCE REACHING THE SAME INVALID STATE THROUGH NESTED PATTERNS** (cool-ferret-679, 2026-09-02): the exhaustiveness checker is blind THROUGH NESTED PATTERNS. `check_match_exhaustiveness` folded arm coverage as `VariantPattern { name: n, parent_enum: _, field_bindings: _ }` and keyed a covered-set on the head name alone, so an arm restricting a field to ONE inner constructor marked its whole variant covered. The parser does not lose the nesting -- `parse_field_bindings` recurses into `parse_pattern` and a nested `VariantPattern` is fully recorded -- so the fact was DISCARDED, not missing. Authored source, one module: `type Inner = P { v: Bool } | Q`, `type Outer = A { i: Inner } | B`, `fn f(o: Outer) -> Bool { match o { A { i: P { v: v } } => v  B => false } }`. `gunbc compile --target rust` exits 0 reporting `compiled: 6 files emitted, 0 diagnostics`; the emitted `f` lowers the one authored arm to an INNER `match i.as_ref() { Inner::P { v: v, .. } => v.clone(), }` carrying no arm for Q, and `cargo check` on the emitted crate refuses `error[E0004]: non-exhaustive patterns: &Inner::Q not covered`. DISTINGUISHING FACT AGAINST THE UNIT-VARIANT INSTANCE ABOVE, and the reason both rows stand: nothing here turns on which entity the emitter minted. The emission is a FAITHFUL lowering of the accepted graph, and the target compiler is performing the analysis the front end declined -- so the two instances share the invalid state and share no mechanism, and repairing either leaves the other standing. THE TEMPTING REPAIR IS ITSELF THIS CLASS, in both directions. Treating any nested refutable binding as not covering its variant is sound but refuses live code, because `infer_bool_literal_pattern_classify` in `v2.compiler.infer` is exhaustive THROUGH its nesting; and computing coverage one FIELD at a time fails open exactly as the original did, since `A { x: P, y: Q }` beside `A { x: R, y: S }` reports both columns covered while `A { x: P, y: S }` matches nothing. A third near-miss is narrower and was caught only by a control: taking the default matrix whenever SOME row is irrefutable drops the constructor rows that also cover the wildcard's values, and refuses `A { x: P, y: _ }` beside `A { x: _, y: P }` and `A { x: Q, y: Q }`, which is exhaustive. The sound gate is signature COMPLETENESS -- the default matrix is admissible only when some declared constructor appears at no row head at all. RUNG FOUND AT: below the ladder, established by execution at the emission boundary -- the compile accepts and the emitted crate refuses. CEILING: 3, structurally guaranteed: coverage of a closed constructor roster by a finite set of authored patterns is decidable from the declaration, so a pattern-matrix recursion derives the refusal or the proof and no undecidable predicate is involved. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, phrased as the capability rather than an artifact: a closed constructor roster for the KERNEL SCALAR types, sufficient that a Bool, Int or String column covered only by literals is decided the way a Disj column's declared arms are decided. Until that lands, a nested column carrying no closed roster is not refused -- which is the pre-existing top-level behaviour (a non-coproduct scrutinee was never checked at all) carried to depth, and is the residual this instance does not remove. RECOGNITION RULE: when the target compiler reports non-exhaustive patterns on an INNER match the emitter generated from a SINGLE authored arm, the missing analysis belongs to the front end; and the question to ask of any exhaustiveness checker is whether its coverage key can represent DEPTH at all -- a key that is a variant name cannot. **THE SAME CLASS ON TWO FURTHER TARGETS, AND THE PYTHON ARM IS THE DANGEROUS ONE** (still-swift-363 produced the probe, cool-ferret-679 ran the toolchain, 2026-09-03). A three-line module -- `fn optionals() -> List<Int?> { [none, Present { value: 2 }] }` and `fn first_of_optionals() -> Int? { optionals().first() }` -- is accepted with ZERO blocking diagnostics on rust, python and go alike. Rust emits a compiling module. The other two do not, and the PYTHON FAILURE MODE IS WORSE THAN UNCOMPILABLE: `py_compile` PASSES and `import` SUCCEEDS, because `from __future__ import annotations` defers annotations to strings, so the undefined `Optional` never evaluates at import; `typing.get_type_hints` then raises NameError 'Optional', and CALLING the function raises NameError 'Present'. AN EMISSION DEFECT THAT SURVIVES IMPORT IS FAR MORE DANGEROUS THAN ONE THAT DIES AT IMPORT, BECAUSE EVERY CHEAP VERIFICATION ANYONE WOULD REACH FOR REPORTS SUCCESS -- a smoke test that imports the module passes, and the artifact fails only in the hands of whoever calls it. Stating the class as 'python emission is uncompilable' understates it into something a reader would expect a smoke test to catch. The GO arm is recorded as a SOURCE READING and not a toolchain receipt, because no go toolchain was available to the observer: the emission returns two values from single-return signatures in both functions, declares `[]*int64` while returning `[]interface{}`, and references an undefined `Present`. **A THIRD INSTANCE, FOUND BY REVIEW ON THE FIX FOR THE SECOND, AND THE REASON IT IS NOT REPAIRED IN THE SAME CHANGE (2026-09-03, gunbc#10028, review 59399).** The nested-pattern checker decides each column against `constructor_roster_for`, whose `ConstructorsOpen` answer is an ELSE BRANCH and therefore CONFLATES THREE STATES THAT HAVE THREE DIFFERENT CORRECT ANSWERS: a genuine open scalar (`Int`, `String`), where a finite set of refutable heads cannot cover the value space and a witness is owed; a SINGLE-CONSTRUCTOR PRODUCT (a record type, no `|`, hence no `Disj` connective), which is CLOSED with exactly one constructor and whose complete destructure is total; and a type whose lookup FAILED, where `resolve_scrutinee_type` returns the node unchanged, which is IGNORANCE AND NOT AN ANSWER. **MEASURED, NOT ARGUED, AND THE MEASUREMENT IS WHY THE OBVIOUS FIX WAS REVERTED.** The arm today answers `[]` -- no witness, i.e. exhaustive -- when no row head is irrefutable, which is a widening failure arm and is authored by this change rather than inherited (`origin/main` carries ZERO occurrences of `ConstructorsOpen`; its checker never examines these positions at all). Executed evidence for the defect: a two-module probe accepted at 0 diagnostics whose emitted crate refuses at `error[E0004]: non-exhaustive patterns` on both a bare `Int` scrutinee and a nested `A { v: 1 }`. Executed evidence AGAINST the one-line remedy of flipping that arm to refuse: with the guard deleted and the seed rebuilt, the corpus reports NINETEEN sites, sampled rather than counted, and they are FALSE POSITIVES of the second kind -- `v2.std.grammar` `derive_grammar_relation_token_edges_recursive_step` matches a `Witness` with both arms present and is reported for its nested `DeriveGrammarRelationTokensProgress` column, a three-field record whose total destructure is a `VariantPattern` and so never enters the default matrix. **THE GENERAL SHAPE IS WORTH MORE THAN THE INSTANCE: a widening arm and a false-refusal arm can be the SAME LINE read in two directions, and flipping it trades a fail-open for a fail-closed-on-valid-input without touching the modelling deficit underneath.** The deficit is that one coproduct arm answers for three states; §5's ⊤-as-answer-versus-⊤-as-ignorance is exactly the third row of that table. RUNG FOUND AT: 1, mitigatable -- the emitted target refuses at its own compiler, so the harm is a failed downstream build rather than a silent wrong answer, and nothing in the accepting path reports it. CEILING: structural. Membership is decidable -- whether a type node is a coproduct, a single-constructor product, or unresolved is a question the type environment already answers -- so the invalid state can be made unwritable by construction rather than checked. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER: split `constructor_roster_for` into the three states above, SUFFICIENT FOR a closed single-constructor product to be adjudicated as closed and an unresolved type to refuse as unresolved rather than either being answered as open; the population that trigger must clear is the nineteen sites named by the measurement above, which are its acceptance corpus and not its debt.

**AN INSTANCE WHERE A DECLARED TYPE'S CARRIER AND ITS OWN PRIMITIVES DISAGREE, 2026-09-04 (fierce-ant-136), REPRODUCIBLE FROM MAIN.** (Named by shape and NOT numbered, because this row already says ordinals go stale the moment another lane appends -- and mine would have been wrong on arrival: I wrote SECOND while the row already carried more.) `std.string_type` declares `type String = FreeMonoid<Char>` and invokes the string primitive families `string_length`, `char_at` and `substring` over it. The emitted realization represents that local `String` as a persistent vector while the runtime shims it calls accept kernel text, so the target refuses six times with `error[E0308]: expected '&str', found '&Rc<Vector<i64>>'`, the first at `std_string_type.rs:19` on `v1_rt::string_length(&left)`. HOW IT SURFACED, which is the part worth keeping: nothing changed in `std.string_type`. A public change imported it into `std.decl_ref`, which IS part of the emitted seed surface, so required regeneration correctly demanded a committed mirror -- `regen FAIL refusal: emitted surface has no committed mirror` -- and installing that mirror, exactly as the refusal instructs, is what first compiled this long-accepted module. The module was accepted for as long as nobody emitted it. WHICH AUTHORITY IS WRONG IS NOT ESTABLISHED, and this row deliberately does not guess. The evidence supports only: the front end accepts; emission produces a vector-backed local String; the runtime shim expects kernel text; rustc refuses. At least three mechanisms remain open -- the front end resolving a kernel-string intrinsic against the local `FreeMonoid<Char>` declaration, the emission choosing a carrier the primitives were never defined over, or the runtime shims being signed for kernel text where the modelled carrier is a code-point sequence. Naming a culprit here would be an attribution the measurement does not carry, and the repair differs per mechanism. WHY IT IS FILED AS A SPECIMEN AND NOT A NEW CLASS: the invalid state is this row's own -- accepted `.dag` producing target code the target compiler refuses, with the responsibility at the source-to-target floor rather than at rustc. It is a mechanism this row did not yet carry, so it is added as component (iv) of the ONE class-grain trigger above rather than given a trigger of its own. TWO ERRORS OF MINE ARE CORRECTED HERE RATHER THAN QUIETLY DROPPED, because this row already documents being burned by both. First: I appended a SECOND class-grain trigger saying the class 'now has two populations' and climbs when the constructor-roster split and the carrier capability both hold. That is a partial retirement condition standing beside the real one -- the row would read as restored while mechanisms it explicitly carries stayed authorable -- which is the exact 4b(3) grain mismatch the paragraph above records the row having already been refused for once. A class with one authoritative trigger does not acquire a second. Second: that clause attributed `constructor_roster_for` to the non-applied-unit-variant population. It belongs to the nested-pattern exhaustiveness instance, a different mechanism entirely, so the sentence joined one specimen's repair to another specimen's loss. The specimen-level trigger it implied is withdrawn; what this instance contributes is component (iv), and component (iv) is discharged by the capability named above and by nothing less.

**AN INSTANCE WHERE THE EMITTER MINTS AN OUTPUT PATH FROM A LITERAL RATHER THAN FROM WHAT IT EMITTED, 2026-09-05 (still-swift-125).** The mechanism: `05_emit_rust` derives a module's `use` lines from its SOURCE references and spells several provider paths as literals, so an emitted path is correct only while the corpus happens to contain a module of that name. It is a mechanism this row did not carry, so it is added as component (viii) of the ONE class-grain trigger above and is given NO trigger of its own -- this row records being refused once for acquiring a second class-grain trigger, and a component is the shape that does not repeat it. IT HAS ALREADY FIRED, WITH A MEASURED REFUSAL, which is why it is filed as an occurrence and not a worry: a derived use-line named a provider the emitted code never paths into, and the target refused `error[E0432]: unresolved import crate::v1_compiler_emit_core_support` in `v1-stage0-std-core` -- emitted for `v1.std.core`'s `to_string` calls, which `emit_typed_call` intercepts and renders as a host method, so the SOURCE reference is real while the EMITTED module never mentions that provider. Dead in a single-crate build, fatal under the seven-crate stage0 partition. That receipt is carried at `05_emit_rust` `host_realized_builtin_needs_no_import_note`, which is also where the remedy's ceiling is recorded. THE POPULATION IS THE SPELLINGS, NOT ONE SITE, and stating it at instance grain would understate it: the eleven-plus runtime paths the emitter spells unconditionally (`v1_rt::concat`, `length`, `lookup`, `map_get`, `member`, `trim`, `take_owned`, `step`, `symbol_lexeme`, `rc_empty_map`, `rc_empty_set`), the `v2_std_diagnostic::Outcome` path in the direct-ingest driver, and three `module_to_filename` literals in the retained-host arm -- `v1.std.core` at `emit_main_mod_uses` and at `emit_main_diagnostic_fns`, `v1.compiler.artifact` at `emit_main_pipeline_fns`. One mechanism, one dissolving capability, one component. A DISTINCTION THIS COMPONENT MUST KEEP, because erasing it would misfile the class and invite a repair that makes things worse: a literal naming a path in the OUTPUT program is not the same defect as a literal INTERROGATING THE CORPUS and branching emission on the answer. The first is wrong loudly, at the target compiler, and is what this component covers; the second emits a structurally different program silently, and is a source-to-target correctness defect of a different kind. Deriving an output path by searching the corpus for the module that declares some symbol converts the first into the second -- it needs a symbol-name literal, reads the corpus, and branches on what it finds -- so it removes the appearance of a hard-coded name while LOWERING the rung. That trade was proposed and declined on 2026-09-05; the component is discharged by the capability below and by no substitution of one literal for another. NEXT-RUNG COMPONENT (viii): DERIVE USE-LINES FROM THE EMITTED REFERENCE SET RATHER THAN FROM SOURCE REFERENCES, sufficient that no emitted module carries a `use` line for a provider its own emitted bytes never path into, and that no provider path is spelled by the emitter rather than observed in what the emitter produced. The wording is the repository's own, named from both ends at `05_emit_rust` `host_realized_builtin_needs_no_import_note` and `reference_derived_authored_source_gate_note`, which that file records as naming the same terminal shape from opposite ends. It is stated as the capability rather than as any artifact because it clears the whole population in one motion: a trigger naming a single site, or naming the arrival of a second compiler generation, would be satisfied while every other spelling stayed authorable.
- **incidental denominator as a wall** (a destructive operation is safe only because an upstream filter written to answer a DIFFERENT question happens never to hand it the dangerous input. Nothing is wrong today and nothing declares why, so the safety is a coincidence with no authority, no diagnostic and no test — and it dissolves silently the moment the upstream question changes, which is a change nobody reads as touching safety. Distinct from `authority_substitution`, where PROSE invents an A-to-B arrow: here the CODE leans on an arrow that genuinely holds and that neither carrier claims, so grepping the guarded operation finds no guard to have gotten wrong. Receipt (2026-09-01): `v1_compiler.required_regen_host` `install_convergence_stage_with_backend` copies `candidate_src/<basename>` over `stage0_src/<basename>` for whatever roster it is handed, asking nothing about the artifact. The emitter really produces a bare `Cargo.toml` (`v1.compiler.emit_rust`); `write_emitted_tree` writes it into the candidate tree verbatim because it is not rustfmt-normalizable; `produce_candidate_manifest` rows it as a `GeneratedSurface` declared by that emitter. Every stage between the emitter and the copy admits it. The sole reason it never became an install target was `is_compared_generated_basename`, whose job is to denominate the DRIFT COMPARISON. **THE HARM CLAIM IS THE PART THIS ROW GOT WRONG FIRST, and the correction is the instructive half.** The finding was briefed, amplified through a manager and written into a work-item title as a destructive overwrite of the 172-line hand-maintained `src/v1/stage0/Cargo.toml`. It is not: the installer joins destinations under `stage0_src = workspace/src/v1/stage0/src`, so a bare `Cargo.toml` basename resolves one directory BELOW the manifest, to a path that does not exist. Widening the population alone writes debris rather than destruction ONLY IF the join is the whole story, and THAT CLAIM WAS ITSELF AN UNDER-ENUMERATION, caught in review of the repair: `fs::copy` follows the DESTINATION link, git tracks symlinks, so a committed stage0 entry that is a symlink reaches any path in the tree with the join untouched. The lesson generalizes past this row: a harm correction that names ONE mechanism and treats it as the requirement is the same coincidence-as-wall shape turned on the analysis -- the honest form enumerates the ROUTES to the destination and says which are closed, since `resolves outside X` is a property of the filesystem and not of the path expression. Two readers reached the destructive reading independently and agreement read as confirmation, which is why the deciding observation is a JOIN EXPRESSION and not a semantic comparison of the two files — the artifacts genuinely differ, and that true fact was fused with a destination claim it never established. **The surviving harm is exemption, not destruction, and it is the one worth naming:** a declared `GeneratedSurface` that the comparison filter hides is invisible to the fixed point (`first_generation_equal`, `changed_paths`) and unreachable by the one authority that would force its gap to be counted — `v2.compiler.self_host.stage0_crate_layout` `emitter_produced_divergent_registrations`, which already enforces in three directions and holds `main.rs` for exactly this relation. So the filter did not accidentally PROTECT the artifact; it accidentally EXEMPTED it. Beside it sat a second, CALLER-LESS installer, `install_candidate_paths`, equally unguarded and invisible to review because a crate-wide dead-code allow kept it compiling: an unguarded operation with no consumer still reads as sanctioned precedent for the next author. **SECOND RECEIPT (2026-09-04), AND IT GENERALIZES THE ROW PAST MUTATING OPERATIONS TO IDENTITY KEYS.** gunbc#10337 keys brand declaration identity on `span.file` plus the authored name. Reviewed as a section 3 violation -- a String nickname beside `std.decl_ref.DeclarationRef` -- and offered as a bounded disposition on the ground that the key is FILE-keyed where the authority is MODULE-keyed. Measured before admitting the debt: across dag/ and src/v2, 4754 `.dag` files carry ZERO files declaring more than one module, with a control confirming the regex matches. File to module is therefore INJECTIVE and `span.file` IS identity-equivalent to a module path, so the offered gap does not exist and the drop row was refused rather than written. **But the equivalence is a CORPUS PROPERTY, not a structural guarantee, and that is this row's shape rather than a fact about modules.** Nothing makes a two-module file unwritable; no authority declares the one-module-per-file relation; no diagnostic fires if it is broken. So an identity key is correct today because of an invariant whose owner does not know it is load-bearing, and it would dissolve silently the first time a file declares two modules -- a change nobody would read as touching brand identity. The generalization: the row is not about DESTRUCTION, it is about CORRECTNESS RESTING ON A COINCIDENCE, and the mutating-bytes framing of the first receipt is one instance of it. Recognition rule extended to this form: when a key, join or identity comparison is sound, ask WHICH UNSTATED INVARIANT makes it sound, then name the authority that enforces that invariant. If the answer is 'nothing does, it just happens to hold', the soundness is a coincidence with no authority, no diagnostic and no test -- exactly the state this row names. The next-rung trigger for THIS instance is a wall refusing a second module declaration in one file, which would convert the coincidence into a structural guarantee. **Recognition rule: for every operation that mutates authoritative bytes, ask what it would do if handed the worst member of the population its input is DRAWN FROM — not the population it is called with today — then name the function that forbids that member and the question that function was written to answer. If those two questions differ, the wall is incidental and the operation has none.** The remedy is an admission at the mutation boundary itself, over the whole roster before the first byte moves and refusing rather than skipping, since a per-item skip re-exports the same silence one layer in; and it is honestly a mechanically-preventable rung, whose ceiling is structural impossibility once the plan projects a typed surface identity instead of a `List<String>`. **THE REMEDY HAS ITS OWN TRAP, AND THIS ROW WALKED INTO IT BEFORE CATCHING IT: restating the incidental denominator as the guard's own rule.** The first repair refused every non-`.rs` install target, which reads as a wall and is the SAME defect dualised — the accidental denominator promoted from coincidence to policy, in a second location, now refusing the correct end state by construction, since the emitted manifest is stage0's own and is supposed to converge. **Recognition rule for the repair: state the arm's reason, and check whether it forbids the state the system is trying to REACH.** If it does, the guard has ratified the coincidence instead of replacing it, and the honest arms are the ones the boundary can answer from its own facts — here a path that cannot address its own destination, and a hand-authored mirror — while the artifact-kind question goes back to the authority that owns dispositions. **SECOND RECEIPT, AND IT IS FILED HERE RATHER THAN AS A NEW CLASS BECAUSE THE MECHANISM IS IDENTICAL WHILE THE POLARITY IS INVERTED (measured 2026-09-02 at dc878756107b).** The receipt above is an upstream filter that accidentally KEEPS a destructive operation safe. This one is an upstream filter that accidentally KILLS a declared wall. gunbc#9824 moved the namespace transition-admission roster's cleanup bill off bystanders by making a provably consumed row an inert typed receipt, and put the deletion obligation in one arm: a consumed row refuses on the first change whose diff touches the roster's own source file. v1_compiler.cli_run.namespace_wave_admission.run_required_wave_admission computes that arm's predicate as roster_touched over the head side returned by diff_sides -- whose final act retains only paths satisfying in_sweep_scope, a predicate that opens by requiring a .dag suffix, while ADMISSION_ROSTER_REL_PATH names a .rs file. A .rs path cannot survive a .dag filter, so roster_touched is false on every production run and consumed_due in claim_executor.report_wave_admission_outcome can never be true. in_sweep_scope was written to answer WHICH FILES THE PARSER RE-READS to reconstruct the baseline; nothing joins it to the deletion obligation it silently decides, and that is this row's mechanism unchanged. **THE RECOGNITION RULE ABOVE CATCHES BOTH POLARITIES WITHOUT AMENDMENT and this receipt exists so the next holder of a dead arm recognises themselves in a row whose other specimen is accidentally safe:** naming the function that decides the operation and the question that function was written to answer is polarity-free, and the two questions differ here exactly as they differ there. **WHAT THE INVERTED POLARITY COSTS THAT THE SAFE ONE DOES NOT, and it is the reason the inversion is worth carrying:** an accidentally-safe operation still behaves correctly today, so the debt is latent. An accidentally-dead wall is cited as coverage while it is dead -- in a merged pull-request body, in the arm's own doc comment, and to every reader who greps for the arm and finds it present, gated on, and reached. Every reachability-based tell reports health, because the arm IS reached; what never happens is that its predicate is true. The declared window it guarded (roster-use bounded) is therefore unbounded in execution, which gunbc.rung_drop namespace_admission_consumed_row_deletion now declares.)
- **compensating errors cancel in the aggregate** (a completeness check compares a COUNT on each side instead of joining the two populations by IDENTITY, so a member missing from one side and a phantom member on the other cancel exactly, and the check reports the coverage it was written to refuse. The green is not a near miss: it is the check confirming a roster that covers nothing of the sort, and it is louder the longer both defects stand, because each new member added to either side keeps the totals in step. Distinct from `executed_conjunct_discriminates_nothing`, where the conjunct can never go red: this one goes red readily on a SINGLE defect and is blind only to defects in opposing directions -- which is precisely the pair a long-lived roster accumulates, since the same neglect that leaves a new member unrostered leaves a deleted member rostered. Receipt (2026-09-01): `test.claim.language_source_scaffold_index_test.compiler_tests_rust_blobs_are_all_rostered` asserted `declared_fn_count(ct_) == rostered_count_for(v1.compiler.compiler_tests_rust)`. The tree carried 44 `ct_` blobs and 40 roster rows, so it was RED and enrolled in `v2.workflow.floor_expected_red` -- but the residues were not 4 of one kind. FIVE live blobs were unrostered and ONE row, `ct_caret_parse_smoke_native_witness_tests`, had outlived the blob #8532 deleted from the carrier. Rostering four of the five would have balanced the counts at 44 and greened the witness with one blob still unmarked and one row still naming a declaration that does not exist. The count was never the claim; it was a NECESSARY CONDITION of the claim being read as the claim. **Recognition rule: for any check whose sentence contains "every", "all" or "covers", ask what it would report if one member were missing from one side AND one phantom stood on the other. If the answer is "green", the check measures a cardinality and reports a coverage, and the two are joined by an assumption nothing enforces.** The remedy is the join: name the two residues separately -- present-not-covered and covered-not-present -- and assert each is empty, so the two directions red with distinct meanings and neither can pay for the other. Cardinality then survives as a third conjunct answering the one question containment cannot, a DUPLICATE within one side, and it is no longer the assertion. The evidence must not stop at the repaired tree, since a repaired population makes both live arms permanently green and the join indistinguishable from the count it replaced: the discriminating REDs belong over authored fixtures, with the equal-counts-different-identities case enrolled explicitly, because that is the one case the previous form accepted. NO SWEEP FOR SIBLINGS WAS PERFORMED, and that is a declared boundary rather than coverage: the recognition rule above is cheap enough to apply to any check whose sentence contains every, all or covers, and this repository certainly holds others, but the receipt below establishes the class at ONE subject and says nothing about the population. Reading this row as a census of aggregate-cardinality checks would be the same substitution it names.
- **one refusal, two destinations, remedy misdirected** (a single refusal at a single source column emits two rows naming DIFFERENT expected types, because two producers resolve the same declared spelling in different environments. The reader repairs from the row that names the parameter -- and that is the row the checker did NOT refuse on. **THE SYMPTOM IS THE RECOGNITION RULE, NOT THE CAUSE**: what a debugging author encounters is one column, two rows, two expected types; the instruction that saves them is DO NOT REPAIR FROM THE INHABITANCE ROW -- the compat row is what refused. Specimen, measured 2026-09-01 on gunbc c0a378c07be by calm-boar-314 and bold-carp-449 independently, on two binaries and two phases (`gunbc compile` emit-stage refusal and `gunbc run` entry-resolve), unrepaired at authoring. A module declaring `type String = FreeMonoid<Char>` and `fn takes_bare(s: String) -> Int`, called from a foreign module that IMPORTS the function, with a deliberately wrong `Int` actual so the diagnostic must name its destination, refuses with: `type mismatch: expected 'Primitive(String)', got 'Primitive(Int)'` AND `value does not inhabit its declared type at the direct call argument for parameter 's': declared 'Node(String<Product(Char)>)', produced 'Primitive(Int)'`. THE TWO PRODUCERS, read from source rather than inferred from message shape: `v1.compiler.infer direct_call_arg_mismatch_diags` renders the compat row from the formal as the CALLER environment resolves it, and `v1.compiler.infer declared_type_obligation_diags` renders the inhabitance row from `obligation.declared` through `declared_type_inhabitance`. Both reach `type_mismatch_error` and `DeclaredTypeNotInhabited` in `v1.compiler.core`, so the rendering is shared and the DIVERGENCE IS UPSTREAM OF IT -- which is why the two rows look like one mechanism disagreeing with itself. **THE TRIGGER IS TWO COMPETING BINDINGS LIVE IN ONE RESOLVING SCOPE, NOT THE IMPORT AS SUCH**, and this row states the narrower claim deliberately: the with/without-import pair is measured, but the two AGREEING cells agree for OPPOSITE reasons -- an un-imported foreign caller agrees at `Primitive(String)` because only the kernel binding is available, and an in-module caller agrees at `Node(String<Product(Char)>)` because only the local declaration is. Phrasing the trigger as the import would make a reader conclude the class does not reproduce when some other construct puts a second binding in scope. An explicit `import <module> { <fn> }` is the ONLY construct measured to create the competition; whether any other does is UNMEASURED. Crossed arm, pre-registered before its log was read: removing the import moves ONLY the inhabitance row, from `Node(String<Product(Char)>)` to `Primitive(String)`, while the compat row is `Primitive(String)` under both -- so one producer follows the import binding and the other does not, rather than the whole reading shifting. RUNG FOUND AT: 1 (mitigatable) -- the line DOES stop, and stops located, so this is not silent wrongness; what fails is that the refusal MISDIRECTS the remedy, which is DESIGN section 5's requirement that a refusal be typed AND located read one layer deeper: located at the right column while naming the wrong destination. CEILING: 3 (structurally guaranteed) -- one resolution of a callee's declared type per call site, consumed by both producers, makes two destinations unconstructible. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, naming the capability rather than an artifact: a single resolved-formal authority at the call site that both the compat check and the inhabitance obligation READ, sufficient that no call-site refusal can render two different expected types for one parameter. Distinct from `meaning_fork`, which is one NAME carrying two meanings across scopes -- that is the underlying condition here and is separately rostered; this row is about two PRODUCERS disagreeing inside a single refusal, which is what makes the remedy wrong rather than merely the meaning ambiguous. Distinct from `diagnostic_name_mechanism_silent`: there the diagnostic names a mechanism that never ran; here both mechanisms ran and both reported faithfully about different environments. WHAT IS NOT CLAIMED, by either measurer: which producer is correct, and why they read the binding differently. No arm on either rig touches it, and the source reading above identifies the producers without settling which environment a call-argument formal SHOULD be resolved in.)
- **a join answers with ONE of its inputs, so the inputs it did not answer with are judged nowhere** (a reduction over several producers -- match arms, branch arms, any fold that collapses N types to one -- picks its answer by a tie-break and every downstream check then reads only that answer. An input the fold did not select is compared with NOTHING: not with the join's answer, not with the consumer's declared type. **THE TELL IS THAT THE VERDICT IS A FUNCTION OF INPUT ORDER**, and that is also the falsifier: permute the arms and re-run. SPECIMEN, measured 2026-09-02 on gunbc, two modules identical except for arm order: v1.compiler.infer folded match arm body types through v1.compiler.infer_types prefer_specific_type, whose tie-break for two disjoint coproducts is the LEFT operand, so `match fl { FYes => FResA  FNo => FOthA }` passed to a formal declared FRes was ACCEPTED, while the same module with those two arms swapped was REFUSED at the direct-call argument seam with `declared 'Coproduct(FRes)', produced 'Coproduct(FOth)'`. One of those is wrong and it is the ACCEPTED one -- values inhabit their declared types is the ordinary compiler floor, which DESIGN section 4b puts BELOW the ladder rather than on a rung of it. DISTINGUISHING FACT FOR THE KERNEL-SCALAR SPECIMEN: the identical Int-versus-String mismatch in a plain function body IS refused with a located `type mismatch: expected 'Primitive(Int)', got 'Primitive(String)'` diagnostic. The checker therefore exists and holds outside the join; the match fold walks around it by selecting the conforming Int arm, after which the declared-return seam compares Int with Int. WHY THE KERNEL-SCALAR SPECIMEN SURVIVED REVIEW AND CI: the malformed arm was unreachable from its only caller, so every executing control passed. Only the typechecker could have seen it, and a join wall that existed, ran on every arm, but recognized only coproduct disjointness was cited as though it covered the whole join. That is rung inflation rather than a missing check. **RECOGNITION RULE: wherever a reduction answers with one of its inputs rather than with a judgment ABOUT them, ask what judges the inputs it discarded -- and if the answer is 'the consumer, which sees only the winner', the discarded inputs are unjudged by construction.** The shape hides because the seam that WOULD refuse demonstrably works: it refuses the other order, so every probe that happens to order the arms unluckily reports the compiler healthy. BOUNDED AGAINST TWO NEIGHBOURS. It is not `absorbing_fallback`: nothing failed and nothing widened, the fold answered confidently and correctly for its own purpose -- selecting the more specific of two types is what it is for. It is not `selection_view_read_as_population` either, though it is that class's compiler-internal cousin: there a measured subset is read as the population, here a SELECTED REPRESENTATIVE is read as a judgment about the whole set. RUNG FOUND AT: below the ladder (silent wrongness). RUNG AFTER THE REPAIR: mechanically preventable -- v1.compiler.infer match_arm_join_diagnostics asks match_arm_types_are_proven_disjoint once per arm; that relation preserves the concrete-coproduct judgment and consumes the declared-conformance path's existing ground-type discipline for Int beside String and List<Int> beside List<String>. The order-sensitive coproduct RED, the conforming-first scalar and collection REDs, and their controls are enrolled at test.claim.match_arm_join_disjoint_coproduct_witness_test; the invalid state stays writable and safety depends on that check executing. CEILING: structurally guaranteed -- a join that produces a JUDGED type carrying every arm's verdict, rather than a representative selected from the arms, makes an unjudged arm unconstructible. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, a capability and not an artifact: one join relation that every N-ary join in the stage consumes, sufficient that no arm can reach a consumer without having been compared to the join's answer -- the ExprIf and ExprMatch joins in v1.compiler.infer are today two carriers asking two different questions of the same fold, which is what let one of them judge nothing at all.)

**TWO FURTHER SPECIMENS, 2026-09-02 (quick-crab-396), BOTH FOUND WHILE MEASURING SOMETHING ELSE.** (1) v1.compiler.emit_rust `module_name_from_filename` reads `map_get(module_index.by_filename, mod_filename)` and resolves the resulting `List<TypedModule>` with `|> first`. What makes it this class rather than an unlucky lookup is that the BUILDER KNOWS: `build_module_index` explicitly `list_push`es on collision, so the multiplicity is constructed deliberately and discarded silently by the reader, and the verdict is a function of fold order exactly as this row's recognition rule states. The map compounds it by being keyed on `module_to_filename(module_NAME)` -- a filename DERIVED from the module name rather than the source path a declaration sits in -- so it answers the inverse question to the one its name suggests. (2) v1.compiler.coercion `type_realization_decision` selects its checkpoint with `target_checkpoints(target) |> filter(cp => cp.dag_name == dag_name) |> first` -- a BARE-NAME filter resolved by first, standing inside the very function whose exact-identity rows (gunbc.rust_source_type_bindings) exist to remove that ambiguity. The second specimen is the more valuable of the pair because it sits in the mechanism under repair rather than beside it. **THE RUNG CLAIMED ABOVE IS INFLATED, AND THIS ROW IS WHERE THAT IS RECORDED (2026-09-04, fierce-ant-136).** The repair line says mechanically preventable because match_arm_join_diagnostics asks match_arm_types_are_disjoint_coproducts once per arm. It does -- and that relation is the WHOLE of what it refuses on, so kernel scalars and ground element collections never enter it. TWO REPROS, EXECUTED RATHER THAN REASONED. (1) `fn f(x: Shape) -> Int { match x { A { n } => n  B { s } => s } }` with `s: String` is ACCEPTED, and forcing the B arm RETURNS the string. (2) the immediate sibling one level up: the same shape declared `-> List<Int>` with a `List<String>` arm is ACCEPTED and RETURNS `[not an int]`. The second matters because the declared-return discipline's established domain is conformance_ground_type -- a required ground kernel scalar OR a required non-keyed element collection whose element is one -- so a repair scoped to scalars alone would leave the collection case writable. THE MECHANISM, AND IT IS THE JOIN AND NOT THE RETURN CHECK. Each arm is inferred with the surrounding expected type, but the generic expected-type wrapper adds only where-refinement diagnostics, so a String arm under an Int expectation stays diagnostic-free. The match then reduces all non-diverging arm body types through prefer_specific_type, whose final fallback is the LEFT operand, so two unrelated resolved scalars leave the first arm's type as the representative. The match node is constructed with that representative as result_type, and the declared-return conformance post-pass compares the declaration against it -- by which point the losing arm is no longer in the population it sees. THE FAILING ARM IS ERASED BY A REPRESENTATIVE-PRODUCING JOIN BEFORE THE DECLARED-RETURN WALL RUNS. That is this row's own recognition rule, applied to this row's own repair. WHAT THE INFLATION IS, STATED PRECISELY BECAUSE THE FIRST TRY GOT IT WRONG. match_arm_types_are_disjoint_coproducts is HONESTLY NAMED: it says exactly what it does, its diagnostic says disjoint declared coproducts, and the source note bounds the wall to that subject. The inflation is not a check scoped narrower than its name claims -- it is a narrow, honestly named per-arm check whose EXISTENCE was read as evidence that the join judged every arm-type relationship. The misleading authority was the coverage inference, not the predicate. REVISED RUNG: below the ladder for the conformance_ground_type population, mechanically preventable for the disjoint-coproduct population. The minimum across in-scope paths is the class's rung, so the class sits below the ladder. TRIGGER, AND THE SHAPE IS LOAD-BEARING: ONE broader match-arm join verdict under which the existing coproduct relation becomes one typed refusal reason and an established-ground-type relation becomes another -- one join authority with two reasons, not two joins, and NOT a widening of the coproduct predicate in place. Widening that function while keeping its identity would make its name false, which is the 3 meaning fork this roster exists to catch, committed while repairing a row about unjudged inputs. The join must also refuse incompatible arms where there is NO external expected type, so the declared-return pass must not walk match syntax to compensate: that would fix only matches observed through a declared-return boundary and leave an incompatible match in a local, a data initializer or a call argument decided by whichever representative won. And the join must not call the declared-versus-produced relation directly, because unified_arm_type is a SELECTED REPRESENTATIVE and not an authored type authority -- reusing the ground-domain relation is sound, reusing the directional framing at a peer join is not. FOUND FROM THE OTHER END, which is worth recording because it is how the population gets discovered: a review of gunbc-private #35 flagged a helper declaring -> Int and returning a String from one arm. The instance was unreachable from its only caller, so every executing control passed and only the typechecker could have seen it. An unreachable arm is exactly where this class hides -- reachable ones are caught by their values.
- **a liveness probe read as currency** (a probe answers whether a thing is RUNNING and its answer is consumed as whether it is running the CURRENT definition. The two questions differ only for a process that outlived the definition it was started from, which is the one state a converge is supposed to repair, so the conflation is invisible on every host that has never drifted and load-bearing on exactly the hosts that have. Its neighbour is `state_space_conflation`, and what separates it is that NOTHING HERE IS OVERLOADED: each probe answers its own subject correctly and the loss happens in the JOIN, where a reader picks the observation nearest the question instead of the one that answers it. SPECIMEN, 2026-09-02, gunbc.spark.serving_membership: serving convergence carries an activity member observed through `systemctl --user is-active`, whose four states are running, stopped, no-such-unit and unobserved, and a registration member whose digest is the unit content ON DISK. A host whose unit file was rewritten and whose manager was reloaded converges at BOTH addresses -- the disk holds the desired bytes, the manager has consumed them, the unit is active -- while the process still serves the definition it was started from. No member disagrees, so no remedy is planned, and the drift is reported as convergence. THE HARM IS NOT THE STALE PROCESS, IT IS THAT NOTHING CAN EVER SELECT ITS REMEDY: a restart has no observation to be conditioned on, which is why gunbc.spark.serving_realization carried its only restart as an unconditional third command welded into EnableSystemUnit, restarting every host on every converge because it could not tell which host needed it. RUNG FOUND AT: silent wrongness, which is not a rung -- a stale server and a converged one produced the same verdict with no diagnostic. THE STATE PREDATES THE CHANGE THAT FILED THIS ROW, stated because an honest declaration of a pre-existing gap is indistinguishable, to a reader scanning a diff for regressions, from a confession of a new one -- and it has now been read that way twice. The convergence path acquired this class when activity was modeled on `systemctl --user is-active` in gunbc.spark.serving_observed_members, whose four states name whether a unit runs and never which definition it runs. The PR that split the fused restart neither introduced this state nor closed it; it named it. IT IS STILL THERE ON THE CONVERGENCE PATH, and an earlier revision of this row said MITIGATABLE, which was rung inflation caught in review: `spark_serving_reactivation_remedy` refuses with a typed located cause, but it has NO PRODUCTION CALLER, so nothing routes a convergence through it and the operational path still reports convergence without the refusal. A rung is the minimum across a class's in-scope paths, and a selector nothing calls moves none of them. WHAT THIS CHANGE DID ESTABLISH is the vocabulary and a selector whose refusal is executed against FIXTURE inputs -- a declared boundary, not a rung, and named as one so it cannot be cited as coverage. THE ABSENCE OF THE CALLER IS A DECISION, NOT AN OMISSION: a production caller is a path that can emit a restart, and until an admission fence, a drain protocol and a lease population exist, a restart emitted on a live host is the destructive convergence the gate exists to prevent -- so wiring it early would trade an honest gap for an unguarded one. CEILING: structurally guaranteed. The question is decidable -- a running invocation either carries the definition identity it was started from or does not -- so this is DESIGN section 5's wall AFTER GROUNDING, waiting on its single authority rather than on a decidability argument. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, AND IT IS TWO THINGS RATHER THAN ONE, because either alone leaves the class exactly where it is: (i) an observation that joins a unit's RUNNING INVOCATION to the definition it was started from, sufficient to mint `RunningDefinitionObserved` for a live Spark -- nothing smaller counts, since a probe that reads the file, the manager's loaded definition, or liveness answers a different subject and would leave the capability dead while looking like its trigger; AND (ii) a guarded producer that routes `ReactivationUndecidable` into the convergence verdict, so a host whose running definition cannot be established stops instead of reporting converged. Naming only (i) would be the grain mismatch section 4b(3) warns about: the trigger would be satisfied by a probe landing while the convergence path stayed silent, which is the whole defect. THE OBSERVED-VERSUS-DESIRED DRIFT ON BOTH SPARKS IS A CANDIDATE CONSEQUENCE OF THIS CLASS AND NOT A FINDING: nobody has read a host back, and candidate is the correct word until someone does. RECOGNITION RULE: when a converge concludes from an observation, ask what the observation would say about a subject that is CORRECT BUT STALE -- if the probe answers the same for a current and an outdated instance, it is a liveness reading standing where a currency reading was needed. The tell is a remedy with no condition: an operation that is issued every time, or never, because no observation can distinguish the state it repairs.)
- **a non-verdict disposition surfaced as a refusal** (a gate COMPUTES the difference between THIS SUBJECT IS WRONG and I DID NOT FINISH LOOKING AT THIS SUBJECT, and then DISCARDS it at its aggregate boundary. THE NARROW CLAIM IS THE TRUE ONE AND AN EARLIER REVISION OF THIS ROW OVERSTATED IT: the floor does NOT conflate the two at the diagnostic grain, and saying so was falsifiable from the very log this row cites. It prints INTERRUPTED-BEFORE-VERDICT and COMPLETED-OVER-COST-REQUIREMENT as distinct typed diagnostics, and its summary carries `interrupted_before_verdict` and `completed_over_cost_requirement` as separate counters beside `failed`. A reader of the log can tell them apart perfectly. What cannot tell them apart is everything DOWNSTREAM of the fold -- the FloorRefused verdict, the red check, the dashboard cell, the merge gate -- each of which receives one bit and offers one affordance, which is to run it again. So the defect is not a missing distinction; it is a computed distinction erased on the way out, which is the worse shape of the two because the information exists and is thrown away. Its neighbour is `state_space_conflation` and its dual is `absorbing_fallback`: absorption widens an answer it could not compute, while this reports a computation it never completed AS an answer. The harm is specific and it is not the red itself: a refusal that names no wrong subject cannot be acted on, so the only available response is to run it again -- and a wall that is discharged by rerunning is not a wall. It teaches every consumer that its refusals are weather. SPECIMEN, 2026-09-02, gunbc.witness_floor_workflow required floor: the same commit, 9b00e24f592 on gunbc PR 9984, was run twice with no intervening edit -- ONE run id retried, Actions run 33604337589 attempts 1 and 2 -- and every counter below is addressed by ATTEMPT AND JOB rather than by the ordinals FIRST and RERUN, which name no instrument and do not distinguish this pair from run 33628404336, a different run on a later head. ATTEMPT 1, required-witnesses-floor job 100172868685, conclusion failure: planned=3486 executed=3486 passed=3404 failed=0 interrupted_before_verdict=4 completed_over_cost_requirement=3, verdict FloorRefused. ATTEMPT 2, required-witnesses-floor job 100189043027, conclusion success: planned=3486 executed=3486 passed=3411 failed=0 interrupted_before_verdict=0 completed_over_cost_requirement=0, verdict green. RE-DERIVE EACH SIDE FROM ITS OWN JOB with `gh api repos/gunb-ai/gunbc/actions/jobs/<job>/logs --allow-escape-sequences`, and NEVER with `gh run view --job <job> --log`: measured on this very pair, the latter ANSWERS THE ATTEMPT-1 JOB ID WITH ATTEMPT 2's CONTENT -- its runner banner reads 09:03 where that job's own log begins 08:05, and it reports interrupted_before_verdict=0 completed_over_cost_requirement=0, which are attempt 2's counters. A reader trusting it would record 0 and 0 for BOTH attempts, see no disagreement, and destroy the specimen this row is built on. It is the sibling of `empty_capture_read_as_clean_result` arriving through a WRONG-CONTENT channel rather than an empty one, and it is the more dangerous direction because the answer is ~600 kB of well-formed, plausible log for the wrong subject rather than a zero-byte file anyone would question. Identical planned and executed populations, zero failures in BOTH runs, and the cost-arm rows simply absent the second time. THE SUBJECT OF THIS CLASS IS THE FOUR INTERRUPTED ROWS AND NOT ALL SEVEN, corrected after review: `completed_over_cost_requirement` names claims that REACHED A VERDICT and were then reclassified on cost -- the floor's own text says "reached its verdict and then exceeded its budget ... cost=501ms EXACT ... This is a cost debt only -- it is not a defect", and the rows carry outcome=completed_over_budget, which is to say they PASSED. Those three are a cost-debt fact about a gate that did judge, and folding them into a class about gates that did NOT judge would inflate the specimen by more than half while contradicting the model's own deliberate distinction. They are retained above only as the second half of the nondeterminism observation, where they belong: both arms of the cost machinery vary run to run on fixed bytes. THE SAME-HEAD PAIR IS THE POINT: a cross-head comparison would have required arguing that the intervening commit could not have touched cost accounting, and an argument about what a diff cannot do is exactly what gets overturned; holding the bytes fixed by construction leaves nothing to argue. The disposition at issue is named non-verdict by the floor itself: INTERRUPTED-BEFORE-VERDICT says the deadline preempted the witness so whether it passes is UNKNOWN, and the row's cost is reported as above the ceiling with NO UPPER BOUND -- a bound, not a near miss. So on those four rows the gate is not reversing a judgment between runs; it is failing to REACH one and rendering that as a refusal. That is the whole class: FOUR rows in this specimen, and the arithmetic of the refusal is that four undecided rows were sufficient to refuse a run in which zero claims failed. THE COST HALF OF THIS STORY IS NOT THIS ROW'S AND IS NOT RE-DERIVED HERE. That a claim's measured cpu-ms is unstable on a shared runner -- and therefore that `completed_over_cost_requirement` varies run to run on fixed bytes -- is the declared standing drop `gunbc.rung_drop` `floor_cost_claim_qualification_unavailable`, whose restoration trigger is a claim-owned cost basis invariant or bounded by construction across the admitted envelopes. That row also settles what happens at the boundary: both terminal arms STAY required reds and are distinct. This row takes no position on whether a cost verdict qualifies a claim; it says only that the aggregate discards which arm fired. Filing the cost instability separately would be a second authority over one fact. RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable -- the dispositions are typed, located and counted at the fold, and the floor's own diagnostic text states plainly that the verdict is unknown rather than claiming a failure. What it does not do is carry that distinction past its own summary line. CEILING: structurally guaranteed. Whether a claim reached a verdict is decidable and already decided -- the floor holds the disposition -- so this is DESIGN section 5's wall after grounding, not a decidability limit. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, two conjuncts because either alone leaves the class where it is: (i) a required-gate verdict in which non-verdict dispositions are a THIRD outcome distinct from pass and fail -- DISTINCT IN DIAGNOSIS, NEVER IN WHETHER THE LINE STOPS. The third outcome must still block the gate: a run that did not finish looking has established nothing, and letting it through would be a widening arm of exactly the kind DESIGN section 5 forbids, trading a refusal that names no subject for no refusal at all. What must change is what the refusal SAYS -- an undecided row is reported as undecided and is discharged by making the claim reach a verdict, never by a rerun that happens to land under the ceiling. The defect is the conflation, not the stopping; AND (ii) a cost admission whose budget is a property of the CLAIM rather than of the run's host speed, sufficient that the same bytes yield the same disposition across runs -- without it, arm (i) merely relabels an intermittent outcome and the population that must be resolved keeps changing underneath its own roster. RECOGNITION RULE: ask what the gate reports when it could not measure, and compare it with what it reports when it measured and found the subject wrong. If those two are the same value, the gate's refusals do not name a subject, and the tell is behavioural: people begin rerunning it rather than reading it.)
- **an empty capture read as a clean result** (an instrument refuses on one stream while the reader keeps the other, so the capture is EMPTY and the empty capture is consumed as a finding of nothing. It is a specialization of `blank is not evidence` narrowed to a mechanism that recurs: the refusal is well-formed and honest, it simply travels on a channel nobody kept. SPECIMEN, 2026-09-02, reading a failing CI job during gunbc PR 9984: `gh run view --job <id> --log > f` writes ZERO BYTES and puts `run is still in progress; logs will be available when it is complete` on stderr, so the redirect captures nothing while the job is already recorded as completed failure. A grep for `panicked` or `FAILED` over that file then reports no failures for a job that failed. Two adjacent instances in the same call: `gh api .../actions/jobs/<id>/logs` writes zero bytes without --allow-escape-sequences, again refusing on stderr; and the same subcommand takes a JOB id rather than a run id, where the wrong id also yields nothing useful. THE ASYMMETRY IS WHAT MAKES IT RECUR: the failure direction is always benign. An empty capture never manufactures a false alarm, only a false all-clear, so nothing in the reader's experience trains them to check it. RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable at the reading site -- measuring the capture's size before consuming it converts the silent case into a loud one -- and silent wrongness wherever that check is absent, which is the default. CEILING: mechanically preventable for instruments this repository owns, since a reader that must consume a typed outcome cannot consume a byte count of zero as a verdict; it stays mitigatable for third-party command-line tools, whose stream discipline is outside the modeled guarantee and is observed rather than proven. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER: a repository instrument-reading surface whose result type distinguishes READ SUCCESSFULLY AND FOUND NOTHING from DID NOT READ, sufficient that a zero-byte capture cannot inhabit the first -- at which point the diligence rule below is subsumed for every call routed through it, and remains diligence for calls that are not. RECOGNITION RULE: before believing a quiet result, ask which stream the instrument refuses on and which stream you kept. If a refusal and a clean result produce the same bytes in the channel you read, you have no observation. The mechanical form, and it is a two-step rather than a byte-count rule, because inferring failure from size alone fabricates a refusal for the query that legitimately returned nothing: a zero-byte capture is NOT A VERDICT IN EITHER DIRECTION, so consult the instrument's typed status -- its exit code, and the stream its refusal travels on -- before consuming the emptiness. Where the status says the instrument ran and the capture is empty, that is FOUND NOTHING and is a real observation; where the status says it refused, or the status is unavailable, the capture is DID NOT READ and no conclusion may be drawn from it. The failure of the original habit is not that it read zero as one of those two, it is that it read zero WITHOUT ASKING which. FOURTH INSTANCE, 2026-09-02, gunbc PR 10078, AND IT DEFEATS BOTH RULES ABOVE AS WRITTEN: reading the newly promoted aggregate's receipt line via `gh api .../actions/jobs/<id>/logs` produced NINETY-NINE BYTES ON STDOUT -- the refusal string `the response contains terminal escape sequences; pass --allow-escape-sequences to output it anyway` written into the PAYLOAD channel rather than onto stderr. Both steps of the mechanical rule miss it: the capture is not zero-length, and the stream question -- which stream does the refusal travel on -- answers THE ONE YOU KEPT. A grep for the receipt line over that file finds nothing and exits non-zero, which is indistinguishable from THE RECEIPT LINE IS ABSENT -- and absent would have meant the freshly promoted lane was consuming nothing and the promotion was cosmetic. THE SHARPENING THIS INSTANCE ADDS is to the asymmetry claimed above: an empty capture never manufactures a false alarm is true of the CAPTURE and false of the CONSUMER, because a grep that searches for a symptom's ABSENCE converts the benign direction into a confident positive finding -- and a finding gets acted on where a green gets ignored, so the harm here is the greater one, not the lesser. The rule that actually caught it is a THIRD beside the two above and is independent of both: size the artifact against what the subject plausibly weighs before reading a grep over it as a result. Ninety-nine bytes is not a job log, whatever the exit code says. FIFTH INSTANCE, 2026-09-02, gunbc PR 10086, AND IT IS A THIRD SHAPE RATHER THAN A REPEAT: A COMPLETED JOB WHOSE LOG READS ZERO BYTES WITH NO REFUSAL ON EITHER STREAM. `gh run view --job 100322901366 --log` returned zero bytes and printed nothing on stderr -- not the documented in-progress refusal, no refusal at all -- while `gh api repos/gunb-ai/gunbc/actions/jobs/100322901366/logs --allow-escape-sequences` returned 442,248 bytes carrying the cause. A grep over the empty capture finds nothing and exits non-zero, which reads as THE JOB LOG CONTAINS NO FAILURE for the very job that failed. WHAT THIS INSTANCE BREAKS IS THE ROW ITSELF, and that is why it is filed rather than counted: the fallback advice accumulated above is CIRCULAR.
