And WAIVING the axis to land the artifact would admit it by disabling the property it exists to establish; where a NEIGHBOURING axis is already waived under a declared drop, that waiver was sound ONLY BECAUSE this axis still held, so waiving both leaves not a weakened guarantee but none. RECOGNITION RULE: for any gate pinned to a digest, compute two quantities and compare them -- the TIME TO PRODUCE the evidence, and the MEAN TIME BETWEEN EDITS to the surface the digest ranges over. If production time exceeds the edit interval, the gate is unreachable and every refusal it emits is a scheduling artefact rather than a finding about the subject. The tell is a refusal whose remedy is `re-run it and hope`, and a ledger that reads as a held wall while its green has never once been observed. THE ROOT IS THE SUBJECT SURFACE, NOT THE SCHEDULE: a digest that ranges over raw file bytes makes every edit inside the closure -- including edits that change no semantics, per DESIGN section 4c -- move the subject, so the lifetime is denominated in the WRONG UNIT. RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable, and the mitigation is a freeze nobody can grant. CEILING: structurally guaranteed -- a subject computed from what the routed entries semantically depend on cannot be moved by an edit those entries do not depend on. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, a capability and not an artifact: a receipt subject that ranges over the SEMANTIC DEPENDENCE of the routed entries -- the annotation-erased projection of exactly the declarations they reach -- rather than over the bytes of every file in their closure, so that the surface shrinks to what the evidence is actually about and the expected lifetime exceeds the production time by construction. NOT satisfied by a faster dispatch, a quieter window, or a wider waiver, each of which leaves the denominator unchanged.)
- **a next-rung trigger naming a capability that had ALREADY SHIPPED when the row was written, so the class is born retired and its stall is permanent and invisible** (INVALID STATE: a class below its ceiling names a restoration or next-rung trigger, as section 4b(2) obliges it to, and the named capability was ALREADY PRESENT IN THE TREE AT FILING TIME. The trigger is therefore not a transition: there is no future event at which it becomes true, because it was never false. The row reads as tracked debt with a named exit and is in fact untracked -- nobody will retire it, because retiring it requires observing a change that already happened before anyone was watching. HARM: this is worse than an untriggered stall, because an untriggered stall at least presents as unfinished. A row with a satisfied trigger presents as COMPLETE WORK AWAITING AN EVENT, so it is skipped by exactly the audits that exist to find stalls, and the class it describes stays at its found rung indefinitely while the roster reports it as governed. THE GAP THAT ADMITS IT IS IN THE OBLIGATION ITSELF: section 4b(2) requires a class below ceiling to NAME its next-rung trigger and says nothing about that trigger being UNSATISFIED AT FILING TIME. Naming is checked by review and by the reader; satisfiability is checked by nobody.RECEIPT, AND IT IS THE STRONGEST KIND -- FOUND BY THE AUTHOR OF THE ROW WHILE WITHDRAWING IT, NOT BY AN AUDIT: session vivid-ibex-751 withdrew a finding of their own whose trigger read that the listing states its own truncation and total, against a capability that had shipped in gunbc#9648 a WEEK EARLIER. The trigger was true on the day it was written. Credit for the specimen is theirs and it is cited here as their withdrawal rather than as a third-party report, because the withdrawal is what makes it evidence: an author checking their own trigger against the tree is the only place this is currently caught.RECOGNITION RULE, DECIDABLE AND CHEAP, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS A ROW AND NOT A NOTE: a trigger whose named capability PREDATES THE ROW'S OWN EARLIEST RECEIPT. Both operands are already carried -- the capability has a landing commit and the row has its receipts -- so the comparison needs no judgement and no new authority. A class whose detection needs judgement gets detected once.ITS EXACT DUAL IS ALREADY ROSTERED AS restoration_promise_names_a_route_that_does_not_exist, AND THE PAIR IS THE FINDING RATHER THAN EITHER HALF. That row is a trigger naming a run, lane, cadence or sweep the tree DOES NOT CONTAIN; this row is a trigger naming a capability the tree ALREADY CONTAINS. NOT-YET and ALREADY are TWO WAYS a named condition fails to be a TRANSITION, and both leave a class untracked while it reads as tracked. THIS IS DELIBERATELY NOT WRITTEN AS AN EXHAUSTIVE PAIR, and the reason is this row's own subject turned on itself: a closed count over a population nobody enumerated is the defect rostered as selection_view_read_as_population, and TWO IS THE NUMBER OF ARMS THE ROSTER HAPPENS TO HOLD TONIGHT, not a proof that a third is unwritable. At least one further arm is already conceivable -- a trigger whose satisfaction is UNDECIDABLE, which is neither not-yet nor already and cannot fire for a third reason -- and it is unrostered because nobody has hit it, which is exactly the evidence a closed claim would have suppressed. The axis is offered as a DISCOVERED PAIR to search along, never as a partition to check membership against -- the first as a promise that can never fire, the second as tracking that fired before anyone was watching.WHY TWO ROWS AND NOT ONE ROW WITH TWO ARMS, STATED EXPLICITLY BECAUSE A READER LANDING ON THE PAIR WILL ASK AND AN UNSTATED ANSWER READS AS AN OVERSIGHT. The test applied is whether the REPAIRS diverge, and they do, in both the detection and the remedy. DETECTION: the dual is decided against THE TREE'S CONTENTS -- does the named route exist -- and is at ceiling 3 for exactly that reason; this row is decided against THE ROW'S OWN RECEIPT DATES, which the tree does not need to be consulted for at all. REMEDY: the dual is repaired by BUILDING THE ROUTE the trigger names, or by rescoping the promise to one that exists -- work outside the row. This row is repaired by RE-TRIAGING THE CLASS ITSELF, because a trigger that was already true means the class's current rung was never re-measured after the capability landed, so the row may be at ceiling already, may have climbed silently, or may be genuinely stalled for a different reason nobody has looked for -- work inside the row. Different evidence, different actor, different artifact touched. They are cited to each other rather than merged.THREE NEARBY ROWS THAT ARE NOT THIS ONE, RECORDED BECAUSE THE THIRD WILL BE RE-LITIGATED. self_authorized_dissolution is about a trigger offered as PERMISSION to create debt rather than as a lifecycle fact -- an authority question, not a satisfiability one, and a trigger can be validly authorized and still satisfied at birth. a_written_row_is_not_a_firing_mechanism is about a row STANDING IN FOR a mechanism -- and a trigger satisfied at birth may sit under a perfectly real, perfectly firing mechanism, which is what makes it invisible. predicate_vacuously_true_on_an_empty_domain is the closest in flavour and still a different subject: its domain is empty at EVALUATION time and its harm is a verdict read as evidence, whereas this is satisfied at FILING time and its harm is a transition that can never occur.RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable, and the mitigation is that an author happens to check their own trigger against the tree while withdrawing something else. CEILING: structurally guaranteed -- a trigger carrying the capability it names, rather than prose describing it, admits a filing-time comparison against that capability's landing, so no Accepted program can file a row whose trigger is already discharged. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, a CAPABILITY and not an artifact, and stated so it cannot itself be satisfied at birth: the trigger field is typed as a reference to the capability it names rather than as prose, AND the roster refuses a row whose referenced capability predates the row's own earliest receipt. Neither half alone is sufficient -- the typed reference without the refusal is a shape that permits the state, and the refusal without the typed reference has no operand to compare.
- **a refusal summary whose every counter reads clean, because the counters aggregate over a disposition set that does not contain the one that fired** (INVALID STATE: a run prints a terminal verdict beside a fixed set of counters, and the disposition that actually caused the refusal is not among them. Every number reads zero and the verdict reads REFUSED. The reader is handed a summary that is TRUE IN EVERY FIELD and answers a question adjacent to the one they asked, and the cause is on a different line that the summary does not point at. HARM, AND IT IS WORSE THAN FAILING TO EXPLAIN: the shape FORECLOSES the question. A refusal with four zeros beside it reads as a clean run that refused for no stated reason, which is the exact presentation most likely to be dismissed as flakiness and re-run -- and re-running until it passes is the laundering step that converts a real defect into an unrankable intermittency. A summary that said nothing would send the reader to the log; a summary that says zero four times tells them there is nothing to find. EVIDENCE, TWO INDEPENDENT INSTANCES IN ONE NIGHT ON TWO PULL REQUESTS WITH TWO DIFFERENT CAUSES: `required-floor: verdict=FloorRefused unexpected_failures=0 verdict_incomplete=0 non_verdict_unenrolled=0 stale_non_verdict=0` printed once above a wet-lane receipt subject-mismatch and once above a completed-over-cost refusal. Neither cause has a counter in that line; both were named on the line immediately preceding it. THE COUNTER THAT MOVED IS NOT MISSING FROM THE ARTIFACT -- IT IS IN A DIFFERENT LINE, and that is sharper than saying the cause is printed above the verdict. On a third instance the POPULATION line one row up carries `completed_over_cost_requirement=1` beside `claims_failed=0`, while the verdict line prints its own four zeros. So the verdict line is not silent and not wrong: it is a COMPLETE AND CORRECT SUMMARY OF THE WRONG POPULATION. That makes the class a SUBSET-SUMMARY rather than an omission, and it predicts the rest: ANY disposition outside the aggregation set produces the same four zeros. Two such dispositions are already known -- interrupted-before-verdict at the cpu deadline, and completed-over-cost-requirement -- so the set is under-inclusive in at least two directions rather than one, and the next arm will present identically. A RECEIPT FROM OUTSIDE CI, WHICH SHOWS THE SHAPE IS NOT A PROPERTY OF THIS ARTIFACT: a census column keyed on the interrupted arm counted 16 crossings of the cost line across 60 runs and was published; re-read over THE SAME 60 RUNS with both arms it is 22. The omitted arm was 16 runs ON ITS OWN -- the same size as the one counted, not a tail -- so a summary keyed to a proper subset of the dispositions can be wrong by ITS OWN MAGNITUDE while reading as a confident number. A FOURTH INSTANCE WITH NO SUMMARISATION STEP TO BLAME, WHICH IS WHY IT IS THE SHARPEST: comparing a branch to main by roster cardinality, both sides read 86 while the identity sets were DISJOINT BY ONE EACH WAY -- main carried a row the branch did not and the branch carried a row main did not. Equal totals, two rows apart, and a count comparison reads that as CONVERGED. Had the count been trusted the conclusion would have been that there was nothing to merge, and main's row would have been dropped on the next push with no conflict and nothing to look at. 86 IS NOT A WRONG NUMBER: it is a true statement about cardinality answering a question about membership, and the two coincide often enough that the exception lands exactly where it costs. Here there is no counter, no verdict line and no aggregation -- the whole failure is the substitution of a count for an identity join -- which shows the class is not a property of summary lines at all. THE RULE THAT COVERS ALL FOUR: compare identities, never any count, and compare them in BOTH directions, since a one-sided comparison cannot see what the other side has. THAT RULE IS DERIVED, NOT TALLIED, AND THE DERIVATION IS ONE LINE: WHENEVER TWO LANES EACH APPEND EXACTLY ONE ROW BETWEEN MERGES, THE CARDINALITIES AGREE BY CONSTRUCTION WHILE THE MEMBERSHIPS CANNOT. Count equality is therefore not weakest at the margin -- it is at its most confidently wrong exactly where it is most often reached for, and it carries information ONLY in the case nobody checks, when the two sides happen to append different amounts. A reader who takes away a warning to be careful with counts has taken the weaker statement; the instrument is not to be used on this question at all. THE THREE OCCURRENCES SEEN WHILE WRITING THIS ROW -- one branch at 86, 93 and 94 against main, each with one identity on each side the other lacked, twice on the pull request carrying this very row -- ARE ILLUSTRATIONS AND ARE EXPLICITLY NOT EVIDENCE FOR THE DERIVATION. The derivation PREDICTS them, and a tally a hypothesis predicts cannot also confirm it; counting them as confirmations would be this row's own class committed one level up, in the row that exists to condemn it. They are recorded because they show what the state LOOKS like to someone in the middle of a merge, not because three is a number that establishes anything. RECOGNITION RULE: WHEN A VERDICT IS A REFUSAL AND EVERY COUNTER BESIDE IT IS ZERO, THE COUNTER SET IS INCOMPLETE -- read it as evidence about the summary's coverage, never as evidence about the run. A partition that cannot represent the outcome that occurred is not reporting a clean run; it is reporting that it has nothing to say. THE GENERALISATION, WHICH THREE LANES REACHED SEPARATELY THE SAME NIGHT BY THREE DIFFERENT ROUTES: a key that names ONE ARM of a multi-armed disposition returns a confident, plausible, WRONG NEGATIVE for every case in the other arm. A census column keyed on the interrupted arm, a filter keyed on the deadline that raised it, and a counter keyed on one refusal class each answered cleanly and each missed the sibling arm. THE TELL IS NOT THE VALUE'S MAGNITUDE -- IT IS THAT THE KEY NAMES ONE ARM OF SOMETHING THAT HAS TWO. Ask what the OTHER arm is called before trusting a zero. THE SAME BOUNDARY APPEARS ONE LAYER DOWN, AND IT IS WHY TIGHTENING A NUMBER IS NEVER THE REPAIR. Offered a mechanical discriminator between the two arms -- an interrupted claim's `eval_steps` being pinned to a multiple of the 4096-step budget poll interval -- and measured over `required_floor_claim_cost.tsv` for one run's 3588 rows, it holds at sensitivity 9/9 and specificity 3578/3579. THAT ONE FALSE POSITIVE IS THE WHOLE ARGUMENT. A rule wrong once in 3579 is a HEURISTIC, and DESIGN section 5 forbids dressing a heuristic as a wall, so it may serve as a FORENSIC TELL for reading runs that already happened -- labelled with its measured error rate -- and may never be a check anything gates on. Set the two side by side: the verdict line is a CORRECT SUMMARY OF THE WRONG POPULATION; the step-count rule is a CORRECT RULE OVER THE WRONG GUARANTEE. Both read confidently, both are very nearly right, and NEITHER CAN BE MADE SAFE BY IMPROVING THE NUMBER -- only by changing what is asked. The repair is the same in both places and it is structural: make the two populations DIFFERENT CONSTRUCTORS, so no consumer has to infer which one a row is and no summary can aggregate over a set that omits one. RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable, and the mitigation is that a reader happens to look one line up. CEILING: structurally impossible -- a summary derived from the SAME closed disposition coproduct the verdict is drawn from cannot omit an arm, because an unrepresented arm has no constructor. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, a capability and not an artifact: the summary line is projected from the terminal disposition's own exhaustive match rather than assembled from a hand-chosen list of counters, so adding a refusal arm without adding its counter fails to compile. NOT satisfied by adding the two missing counters, which repairs the instances and leaves every future arm exposed in exactly the same way.)
- **A type answers whether a mechanism COULD observe a subject, and an enforcement decision consumes that answer as though the mechanism DID observe it.** The type is not wrong; it answers a question the blocking decision is not asking, and the gap between the two is invisible at the call site because the same value satisfies both readings. MEASURED 2026-09-04 on the required floor. `v2.workflow.floor_terminal_ledger` `ClaimPreemptionReachability` is `CooperativelyPollable | OpaqueHostCallUnbounded`, and `v2.workflow.required_floor` treats the pollable arm as the case the two safety limits genuinely protect. The CPU deadline is polled in `v1_interpreter` `eval_expr` at `stride % 4096 == 0` -- a STEP stride, not a time stride. **273 OF 349 CLAIMS IN ONE FLOOR RUN NEVER REACH A SINGLE POLL**, because they take fewer than 4096 evaluator steps, so the stride condition is never true and the deadline cannot fire for them at all -- whichever arm they inhabit. `CooperativelyPollable` therefore means WOULD BE OBSERVED IF IT STRIDED, and it is consumed as WAS OBSERVED. **HARMLESS TODAY, AND THAT IS WHY IT IS FILED RATHER THAN FIXED IN PLACE.** The maximum cpu_ms across all 273 is under 100, against a 500ms ceiling: no crossing hides in that population now. A population that is cheap today is exactly the kind that stops being cheap with nobody re-checking the assumption that made it ignorable, and a row is the mechanism that forces the re-check where a note in a pull request is not. **THE SECOND CLAUSE IS UNESTABLISHED AND THE FIRST IS NOT ENOUGH.** For a completion past the limit to establish that the safety mechanism had a bounded opportunity to intervene, two things are needed: that the deadline observation DOMINATES continued execution, and that every execution segment BETWEEN observations is bounded. Exhaustiveness of the two arms gives domination. Nothing declares per-step cost bounded, so a bounded number of STEPS between observations is not a bounded number of MILLISECONDS. **AN ESTIMATOR THAT PRESUMES THE PROPOSITION IT ESTIMATES CANNOT BOUND IT, EVEN IN PRINCIPLE.** Estimating a claim's inter-poll segment as `cpu_ms / eval_steps * 4096` gives a maximum of 15.4ms across the 76 claims that actually stride -- but it ASSUMES UNIFORM PER-STEP COST, which is the exact proposition in question. Under non-uniform step cost a mean is not a bound of any kind, and a single expensive step is invisible to it and is the whole hazard. The figure earns only this: the bound is not obviously violated, and the trigger has a target. The 9947ms worst estimate in that same data is NOT an inter-poll segment: it belongs to a claim with fewer than 4096 steps, which has no inter-poll segment at all. A statistic computed over rows the quantity does not apply to is not a measurement of it. **A PARTIAL CLASSIFIER EXISTS TODAY, RETROACTIVELY, WITH NO NEW CODE AND NO NEW RUNS: EVER INTERRUPTED WITH `raised_by=cpu_deadline` IMPLIES PROVABLY `CooperativelyPollable`**, because an interrupt means a stride point fell inside the claim -- the poll fired, so it was reachable. **THE CONVERSE IS INVALID AND THE ASYMMETRY IS THE WHOLE POINT**: completing over the ceiling is consistent with EITHER arm, so this rule classifies one direction only and can never establish that a claim is opaque. Any reader can run it over a floor log they already have. APPLIED ACROSS THE SAVED LOGS OF TWO SESSIONS IT CLASSIFIES TWENTY IDENTITIES, AND THE TWENTIETH IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS: nineteen came from one lane's logs and contained no `v2.test.execution.*` member, which read as evidence that the execution family is uniformly opaque. It is not evidence -- it is a null over a denominator that STRUCTURALLY EXCLUDED THE REFUTING CASE, because `rust_emit_host_call_wrong_oracle_refuses_holds` is an execution-family interrupt that lived in a floor job that lane had not fetched. UNIONED, THE FAMILY-LEVEL CLAIM IS FALSE RATHER THAN WEAKLY SUPPORTED -- the family is MIXED, and family membership predicts nothing about any individual claim's arm. Recorded here because the under-powered form of that sentence is the one that would have been quoted onward without its caveat. **NEXT TRIGGER, NAMED AS THE CAPABILITY AND WITH WHAT IT MUST BE SUFFICIENT FOR:** either the deadline is observable for every claim REGARDLESS OF STEP COUNT, or the reachability vocabulary names the sub-stride case as its own arm -- sufficient for an enforcement decision being able to distinguish PROTECTED from UNOBSERVABLE without re-deriving step counts. A change that adds a third arm without the gate consuming it discharges nothing. 
- **A by-name evaluation TYPE-CHECKS AGAINST ANY CONTEXT AND ANY NAME, so the relationship between the evaluation context and the namespace it can reach is carried nowhere the compiler can see.** `run_in_context(ctx, "gunbc.x.y", false)` compiles whether or not `ctx` is scoped for `gunbc.*`. The binding is a STRING at compile time and a lookup at run time, and every static gate passes over the gap. MEASURED 2026-09-04 on gunbc#10319 before it was pushed. `floor_required_opaque_host_call_surface` read `gunbc.v1_interpreter_opaque_host_call.opaque_host_call_surface` through the `hermetic` frame, which is scoped for `v2.workflow.*`. `cargo check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo test -p v1-compiler --lib` were ALL GREEN on it. The floor would have been the first thing to evaluate it. **THE BLAST RADIUS WAS THE WHOLE FLOOR, AND THE FAIL-CLOSED ARM IS WHAT MADE IT SO.** The reader refuses rather than arming an empty surface, which is correct -- but an UNREACHABLE MODULE and an UNGROUNDED SURFACE collapse to the same refusal, and that refusal is the entire run rather than one column. A FAIL-CLOSED ARM AMPLIFIES A TARGETING ERROR; IT DOES NOT CONTAIN ONE. The review tell follows: a reviewer looking at a fail-closed arm reads it as safety and stops. THE RULE WAS ALREADY WRITTEN IN THE FILE BEING EDITED, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS A CLASS AND NOT AN ACCIDENT. `cli_run`'s comment on `REQUIRED_FLOOR_RUNTIME_AUTHORITY_MODULES` states it exactly: `a new by-name evaluation adds its module here or refuses at its own call site`. Four modules were enrolled, `gunbc.output_policy` among them, reached through `floor_authority_frame` rather than the hermetic context. The author had read that comment an hour earlier for an unrelated reason and it still did not fire. **A PROSE RULE BESIDE THE CONSTRUCT IT GOVERNS IS CONSUMED BY WHOEVER HAPPENS TO READ IT**, and the population of people who will edit a 40000-line module is not the population who have read its constants' comments. REPAIRED BY ENROLLING THE MODULE AND READING THROUGH ITS OWN AUTHORITY FRAME -- evaluated in its exact scope rather than privileged with a wider namespace than the claims it bounds, which is what the enrolment comment says the enrolment is FOR. Enrolling the module WITHOUT also fixing the frame would have been the same defect with a longer list. **FOUND AT AUTHORING TIME BY READING HOW A NEIGHBOURING `gunbc.*` MODULE IS REACHED, NOT BY ANY GATE.** That is the honest provenance and it is also the measure of the gap: the only thing standing between this and a corpus-wide refusal on an unrelated author's pull request was one person deciding to check a call they had already written and already compiled. CEILING: STRUCTURALLY IMPOSSIBLE, and the class is decidable so the ceiling is real rather than aspirational -- the set of modules an evaluation context can reach is known when the context is constructed, and the module a by-name call names is a literal at the call site. **NEXT TRIGGER, NAMED AS THE CAPABILITY AND WITH WHAT IT MUST BE SUFFICIENT FOR:** a by-name evaluation carries its reachable namespace in its TYPE, so a call naming a module the context cannot reach FAILS TO COMPILE -- sufficient for no by-name read being able to pass `cargo check` while being unresolvable at evaluation. A lint over call sites, or a runtime assertion at the call, is the artifact-grained version: both leave the invalid state writable and depend on the mechanism executing. 
- **a verification attributed to the revision it STARTED on rather than the one it FINISHED against** (a checkout is a fact about an INSTANT; a build, a regeneration or a test run consumes an INTERVAL. If the working tree changes anywhere inside that interval -- a branch switch, a merge, a colleague's push into a shared worktree, an editor save -- the artifact that gets exercised is the tree as of the READ, and the revision the author names is the tree as of the LAUNCH. The result is real and the subject is wrong. WHAT MAKES IT INVISIBLE IS THAT EVERY INTERMEDIATE SIGNAL IS CORRECT. The compile really compiled, the evaluation really returned, the exit code is really zero; nothing in the transcript is false, and nothing in it names a revision either. The only false proposition is the one the author supplies afterwards from memory, which is why it survives careful reading of the log -- the log does not contain the claim being checked. IT IS WORST WHERE THE TWO TREES ARE NEARLY IDENTICAL, because then the result reproduces and the misattribution is never caught, so the HABIT is ratified rather than the reasoning. The occasion it does not reproduce is the occasion where the tree difference mattered, i.e. exactly the case the verification was supposed to decide. RECOGNITION RULE: ASK WHICH REVISION WAS CHECKED OUT WHEN THE RUN FINISHED, NOT WHEN IT STARTED, and demand the answer as an immutable identifier rather than a branch name -- a branch name is itself a mutable binding, so 'I ran it on the control branch' is the same failure one level up. A local verification is bankable only as RESULT + EXACT IMMUTABLE SUBJECT REVISION + ACQUISITION TIME; a result missing any of the three names an unidentified subject. SPECIMEN, gunbc#10327. The positive-control witness for the preemption-reachability column was compiled and evaluated locally and reported as verified before push. The build had been launched from the control branch but FINISHED after a switch to `session/vivid-ibex-751-cost-receipts`, whose source-root is a strict SUPERSET of the shipped one -- three additional `recurring_failure_mode` rows and their roster imports. The witness file was untracked and byte-identical across both trees so the result is very probably reproducible, but 'very probably reproducible' is a PREDICTION about the shipped tree and had been written down as a RECEIPT for it. Corrected in the PR body by naming the head that actually ran and naming CI's floor run as the first execution against the shipped tree. IT IS NOT `window_rendered_subject_misattribution` AND MERGING THEM LOSES THE REMEDY. There, a correctly measured figure is attributed to the wrong subject because a rendering window cropped the subject; the defect is in the READ and a wider read fixes it. Here the measurement is attributed to the wrong revision of the RIGHT subject, the defect is in the WRITE, and no amount of re-reading the output recovers the revision -- the information was never captured. It is also distinct from `absent_artifact_may_be_measuring_instrument_vintage`: there the INSTRUMENT is stale against a current subject; here the instrument is current and the SUBJECT is not the one claimed. RUNG FOUND AT: MITIGATABLE. The harm is contained only by the author noticing and restating the revision, which is a discipline and not a mechanism, and the containing act happens after the false sentence has already been circulated. ATTAINABLE CEILING: STRUCTURALLY GUARANTEED, and not higher, because the invalid state remains describable -- a human can always type a revision into prose. Membership is nonetheless decidable by the runner itself: the tree identity is readable at the moment of the READ, so a verification receipt that CARRIES the revision it observed, rather than accepting one asserted alongside it, cannot name a tree it did not exercise. What no in-repository construction can prevent is an author transcribing a receipt into a sentence, which is the same residue DESIGN's name-the-instrument rule already addresses. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER: A VERIFICATION RECEIPT CARRIER WHOSE SUBJECT REVISION IS READ BY THE RUNNER AT COMPLETION AND WHICH REFUSES WHEN THE TREE MOVED MID-RUN -- sufficient for a local run that spans a branch switch to be a REFUSAL rather than a result with an author-supplied revision. A trigger naming only 'record the sha' is the artifact-grained version of this and is satisfied by recording the sha at launch, which is precisely the value that is wrong.)
- **a policy mechanism that ships in the tree it adjudicates, so the one change that supersedes it is adjudicated by the superseded version** (a merge driver, a hook, a lint config, a formatter, a codegen template, a CI entry point -- anything the toolchain reads from the WORKING TREE at the moment it acts. The version that acts is the version already installed, so on every change that leaves the mechanism alone its instruction is current, and on the single change that REPLACES the mechanism its instruction is guaranteed to be the retired one. **THIS IS AN ANTI-CORRELATION OF ONE, NOT A TIMING HAZARD, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE DISCRIMINATOR.** The mechanism is wrong exactly when it matters and only when it matters. There is no bad draw to be unlucky with and no window to shorten: the more significant the policy change, the more certainly the author is handed the old policy. A rate cannot be improved because the rate is one. IT IS DISTINCT FROM `verification_bound_to_the_revision_it_started_on` AND MERGING THEM LOSES BOTH. There the SUBJECT moves under a STABLE OBSERVER -- a run spans a tree change and the result binds to the wrong revision -- so it bites by timing and most runs escape it. HERE THE SUBJECT IS FINE AND THE OBSERVER IS STALE, BY CONSTRUCTION. Both halves are inverted, which is why a variant section on a row whose entire content is `results are bound to a revision` would leave this class findable only by someone who already knew it. IT IS NOT `stable_citation_mutable_referent`, THE NEAREST STRUCTURAL NEIGHBOUR. That row is about an IDENTIFIER addressing a CONTAINER that serves whatever currently occupies it; here nothing is cited at all, and the stale thing is an EXECUTABLE shipped in the tree it judges. It is not `authority_merges_unprotected_while_its_projection_is_guarded`, which is about which SIDE of a derivation a policy protects, nor `admission_predicate_evidenced_from_inside_its_own_subject`, which is about a surface that cannot represent its predicate being false -- here the surface is perfectly capable of saying the right thing and is simply one version behind. **WHAT MAKES IT LOAD-BEARING IS THAT NOTHING IN THE OUTPUT DISTINGUISHES THE RETIRED RECIPE FROM THE LIVE ONE.** Real mechanism, real refusal, real located instructions, every intermediate signal correct, one version stale. An author who follows it does substantial work down a route the merge is retiring and ends with a coherent, well-evidenced account of why -- which is the same reason it survives review, since the reviewer reads the same output. SPECIMEN, gunbc#10311 against gunbc#10302 at `d6fb9d61c8`. Integrating main produced a `GeneratedArtifactConcurrentDivergence` refusal on `docs/design-failure-modes.md` whose printed repair route was a four-step LOCAL whole-tree regeneration -- roughly twenty-five minutes. The merge being performed was precisely the one that DELEGATES regeneration of the two design-ledger projections to `heal-generated-artifacts`, and git had executed the branch's own pre-merge copy of `.githooks/generated-artifact-merge`. The live route is three steps and regenerates nothing locally. **MITIGATION, STATED AS AN EXECUTABLE PROCEDURE RATHER THAN AS VIGILANCE: when a merge changes a policy mechanism the toolchain itself executes, ABORT, INSTALL THE INCOMING VERSION FIRST AS ITS OWN COMMIT, THEN RE-RUN THE MERGE AND READ THE NEW DIAGNOSTIC.** In the specimen that is `bc402f4d`, and the re-run printed the delegated route. A row that says only `beware` leaves the next lane to rediscover the fix at a conflict, under time pressure, holding instructions that look authoritative. **THREE ARMS, REACHED INDEPENDENTLY BY THREE LANES INSIDE ONE HOUR, NONE ABLE TO FIND THE CLASS IN THE ROSTER -- WHICH IS ITSELF THE EVIDENCE THAT IT IS A DISTINCT IDENTITY.** They differ in WHO is misled and in which direction, and a row carrying only the first teaches a reader to distrust the instruction when the actual defect is the VERSION. ARM ONE, THE AUTHOR FOLLOWING THE RETIRED ROUTE (gunbc#10311, the specimen above): cost is one wasted regeneration. Expensive and self-limiting. ARM TWO, THE MIRROR, AND IT IS WORSE (snappy-koi-879, gunbc#10271, hit within minutes and without knowledge of the first): the author was about to report a LANDED CAPABILITY AS NOT HAVING TAKEN EFFECT -- telling a manager that the emitted diagnostic contradicted the claim that delegation had merged, from a completely correct reading of a stale instrument. The forward arm costs a regeneration; THE MIRROR ARM RETRACTS A SHIPPED CAPABILITY ON EVIDENCE THAT LOOKS IMPECCABLE, and a false negative about a capability propagates: it gets relayed, it stops other lanes adopting the new route, and correcting it requires someone to disbelieve a direct instrument reading. In their words, the delivery and the invocation are THE SAME OPERATION, so it is not a race and cannot be retried into correctness. ARM THREE, THE ENFORCEMENT INSTRUMENT RATHER THAN THE AUTHOR (neat-swift-219, a merge-readiness gate probing candidate heads with `git merge-tree --write-tree` from a worktree several commits behind main): the gate deciding whether anything merges was adjudicating heads against a policy the repository had already retired. **ITS VERDICT FIELD WAS IDENTICAL UNDER BOTH VERSIONS -- REFUSED -- AND ONLY THE INSTRUCTIONS DIFFERED, SO THE ARM THAT DECIDES WAS UNCHANGED WHILE THE ARM THAT EXPLAINS WAS WRONG.** Nothing in the output could have revealed it and a verdict-only reader would never have looked. This arm generalises furthest: any long-lived checker holding a cached copy of a versioned policy inherits it, and the more automated the checker the less likely anyone re-reads its prose. **THE REMEDY IS snappy-koi-879's AND IT HAS A RECEIPT FROM SOMEONE WHO IS NOT ITS AUTHOR:** extract the mechanism from the incoming side and point at it with a one-shot config override -- `git -c merge.generated-artifact.driver="bash <extracted> %O %A %B %P" merge ...` -- touching no tracked file. neat-swift-219 executed it independently and their gate immediately printed the delegated route in place of the retired one; it now re-extracts on every probe rather than caching. THE GENERAL FORM, IN THEIR WORDS: WHENEVER THE MERGE CARRIES THE TOOL, THE TOOL MUST BE TAKEN FROM THE INCOMING SIDE BEFORE IT JUDGES, NEVER FROM THE WORKTREE. **WHY THE OVERRIDE BEATS THE OBVIOUS FIX, AND THIS IS THE SENTENCE THAT SAVES THE NEXT LANE AN HOUR.** The instinctive repair is to sync the worktree to main so the tool is current -- and doing so CONFLICTED on the very roster carrier the tool adjudicates, which two other lanes were appending to at that moment. So the obvious fix for a stale policy tool drags the fixer into the exact contention the tool exists to arbitrate. The override is not merely more elegant: it is the only version that does not put the checker into the queue it is checking. The install-first commit in the specimen works for an author who is merging anyway; the override is what a CHECKER must use, because a checker has no business acquiring a merge. CROSS-REFERENCE WITHOUT MERGER: this shares a SIGNATURE with the upload-side defect where the worse the workflow breakage the less repair material escaped. Both degrade in the wrong direction -- reliability falling as stakes rise -- and the mechanisms are unrelated. The shared signature is the thing to pattern-match on; a common row over it would name a shape, not a class. RUNG FOUND AT: MITIGATABLE, and only by an author who happens to notice that the diff touches the mechanism. Nothing refuses, nothing warns, and the instruction is delivered with full authority. ATTAINABLE CEILING: STRUCTURALLY GUARANTEED. Membership is decidable at the moment of the merge -- the merge itself knows whether the incoming side changes the mechanism's own path -- so a mechanism can refuse rather than instruct when it is about to be replaced. It does not reach impossibility while the mechanism is a tree-resident file that the toolchain resolves by path, because the invalid state remains perfectly writable; making it impossible would require the toolchain to resolve the mechanism from the MERGED authority rather than from the worktree, which is not ours to change. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, NAMED AS THE CAPABILITY: A TREE-RESIDENT POLICY MECHANISM THAT DETECTS ITS OWN PATH AMONG THE INCOMING CHANGES AND REFUSES WITH A LOCATED DIAGNOSTIC NAMING THE SUPERSEDING VERSION, INSTEAD OF EMITTING ITS OWN INSTRUCTIONS -- sufficient for an author integrating a policy change to be STOPPED rather than misdirected, and sufficient for the retired recipe never to be printed as if it were live. `Document the install-first procedure` is the artifact-grained version and discharges nothing: it is satisfied by prose that the misdirected author has no reason to consult, because from inside the failure nothing looks wrong.)
- **denominator moved between the measurement and the comparison** (an identity-grain join is only as stable as the POPULATION it joins against, and `refs/remotes/origin/main` is not stable: it is one ref shared by every worktree of a clone, and any concurrent session's fetch fast-forwards it under a lane that is mid-merge. So a check of the shape `merge, then join the result against origin/main` reads the ref TWICE AT TWO TIMES and reports the interval's inbound commits as rows the merge lost. Specimen (2026-09-03, gunbc#10197): merging `origin/main` at `b05994a8e39` into the rung-drop split, then joining `gunbc.recurring_failure_mode` rows by identity, reported three of main's rows silently dropped by a clean auto-merge with no conflict -- which would have been a severe finding about the single-line-row carrier the PR exists to repair, and it read as a confirmation of that PR's own thesis, which is what made it credible. It was false. `git reflog show origin/main` showed the ref had fast-forwarded 78 -> 79 -> 81 rows in the four minutes after the merge, twice by other sessions; the three rows had ARRIVED, not vanished. `git merge-tree --write-tree` against the CURRENT ref reproduced the complete 81 and a re-run of the identical merge in a scratch worktree produced 81 as well, which is what falsified it. **THE TELL IS THAT THE ORACLE AND THE SUBJECT WERE PRODUCED AT DIFFERENT TIMES AND THE ORACLE IS THE ONE THAT MOVED**, and it is invisible in the check's own output, because a join against a moving ref prints exactly what a join against a stable ref would print for a real drop. Distinct from `stale_base_branch_reads_as_pending_work`, which is about a BRANCH whose content is superseded: here nothing is stale and nothing is superseded -- the two reads are both current, of different instants. **RECOGNITION RULE: for any check whose expected value is derived from a remote-tracking ref, a shared mutable file, a live API or wall-clock-dependent state, ask whether the subject and the oracle were read at the SAME INSTANT; if not, the delta is not attributable to the subject.** THE REPAIR IS A PIN AND NOT A RETRY: resolve the ref to a SHA once, record it, and make every downstream derivation, merge and oracle read that SHA -- a re-run merely samples the moving ref again and can agree with itself while both reads are wrong. The re-run is a diagnostic, not the fix. The generalization worth carrying past git: the direction of a suspicious delta is evidence, and `corruption shortens` reasoning is the same instrument -- but it decides nothing when the denominator itself may have grown, so pin first and read the direction second.)
- **a search that never reached its subject returns exactly what a real absence returns** (an instrument that answers `zero occurrences` over a region it never actually read is not reporting a measurement, it is reporting its own miss in the vocabulary of a finding. The two states are indistinguishable in the output -- both are the empty result -- so the reader supplies the interpretation, and the interpretation that fits the question being asked is `absent`. THREE SPECIMENS IN ONE NIGHT, TWO SESSIONS, THREE DIFFERENT WAYS OF MISSING THE SUBJECT (2026-09-04, gunbc#10197). WRONG PATH: joining main's 142 `gunbc#10254` admission rows against the tree to decide whether they were consumed, the witness module's file was derived from its module path as `dag/test/claim/spark/spark_oobe_screen_observation_witness.dag`; the real file is `..._witness_test.dag`. The read returned empty and the join reported 128 of 142 rows NOT bound to their target -- the answer that would have had 128 consumed rows CARRIED, and a carried consumed row is a stale admission, which refuses every unrelated pull request in the repository. WRONG WINDOW: a reviewer checking the same claim bounded the import read to lines 13-30 and got zero for the real spelling, because the import block runs well past line 30. WRONG DIRECTORY: an earlier check of the same night grepped `dag/product/...` for a symbol that lives under `dag/gunbc/product/...` and got nothing, which read as `the symbol does not exist` and nearly stood as a finding. **THE TELL IS THAT A ZERO WAS ACCEPTED WITHOUT ANYTHING ESTABLISHING THAT THE INSTRUMENT RESOLVED ITS SUBJECT** -- no denominator, no positive control, no assertion that the file read was non-empty or that the window contained what it was supposed to contain. The second specimen is the one that shows the shape cleanly: the reviewer ran a FAKE control spelling alongside the real one, got zero for both, and only that pairing revealed the window was wrong rather than the symbol absent. The control cost one extra line and was the entire difference between a measurement and a miss. **RECOGNITION RULE: whenever an empty result is about to be read as evidence of absence, ask what the instrument would have returned had it never reached its subject; if that is the same value, the result carries no information and the absence is unestablished.** THE REPAIR IS THAT THE INSTRUMENT MUST NOT BE ABLE TO RETURN A VALUE IT CANNOT SHOW IT RESOLVED. A positive control that must hit, or a denominator it must report -- a path read asserts the source is non-empty before its emptiness is allowed to mean anything, a windowed read asserts the window contains a known landmark, a directory search asserts the directory exists. A retry does not repair it: the same wrong path, window or directory returns the same empty result and agrees with itself. TWO MECHANISMS WHERE THE INSTRUMENT NEVER EXECUTED OVER ITS SUBJECT AT ALL, probed 2026-09-04 by sessions jolly-deer-769 and royal-carp-732 on a gunbc BUILT FROM THE TREE. The three specimens above are failures of AIM -- the instrument ran and missed -- so the recognition rule they support asks whether it reached its subject. THESE ASK A QUESTION IT DOES NOT: DID IT EXECUTE. FIRST, THE SOURCE ROOT THAT ABORTS BEFORE ANYTHING COMPILES. Three separable arms, distinguished by guard and by ARGUMENT POSITION, each reproduced one variable at a time: a PRIMARY root outside the workspace is a clean typed root-admission refusal and no panic; a root inside the workspace that is not a directory panics in `anchor_source_root`; the SAME out-of-tree path passed as a SECONDARY root panics in `repo_relative_path_normalized`. Containment and existence are different guards, and the identical path is a refusal in one position and a panic in another. NAME THE GUARD, NEVER QUOTE THE MESSAGE: the first write of this receipt quoted `source root does not exist`, which is what the PATH-INSTALLED binary printed; the tree-built binary says something else at the same guard, and a quoted sentence no build emits is the stale-citation class filed one row over. ALL THREE ARMS ARE LOUD -- stderr, non-zero exit. THE SILENCE WAS MANUFACTURED BY THE READER'S FILTER, and that is the sharper statement of this whole class. Measured twice from opposite sides: a grep keyed on the SUBJECT module's own name discards the abort, because the failure message is about the root and never about the subject, so it cannot match; and a count of lines matching `error:` returns zero, because the abort says `panicked at`. AN ALLOWLIST FILTER -- KEYED ON THE SUBJECT OR ON THE DIAGNOSTIC VOCABULARY -- CANNOT DISTINGUISH THE SUBJECT IS CLEAN FROM THE RUN DIED BEFORE REACHING IT. SECOND, THE MODULE THE RUN KNOWS AND NEVER COMPILES, and the run prints its own two denominators in one line: `resolved N sources (primary-root population root=dag), M indexed modules in the name census only` -- two populations, both true of the same run, whose sizes are READ FROM THAT LINE rather than stated here, because the gap moves with every merge. THE DISCRIMINATING PAIR, one file and one defect moved between them: a probe carrying a return-type mismatch under the PRIMARY root is compiled and the error reported; the SAME file with the SAME defect under a SECONDARY root joins the name census, is never compiled, produces NO diagnostic, and leaves the blocking error count unchanged. The module is fully KNOWN to the run and never looked at. Path mismatch is NOT the mechanism and was measured not to be: membership keys on the DECLARED module path with the directory only as the value, so a file declaring a namespace its directory does not name is resolvable by name AND is still compiled and diagnosed under a primary root. What survives is the shape rather than the cause -- a directory and a namespace are two populations that read as one, and an instrument standing in either has no way to report that its complement was never in scope. AND THE CONTROL DOES NOT DISCRIMINATE UNLESS ITS FAILURE IS VISIBLE IN THE CHANNEL BEING READ -- retracted and re-measured by royal-carp-732 against their own earlier account, which is why it is stated here as the correction rather than as the finding. A must-fail control planted as `Int + String` raises at EVALUATION time and can never appear in `gunbc compile` output; the same intent written as a return-type mismatch is static and does appear. The first control was a genuine defect, genuinely present, in a genuinely compiled module, and COULD NOT HAVE FIRED however well the instrument worked -- it looked rigorous and was inert, which is worse than no control, because it is cited as proof the instrument was live. Choosing a control is not choosing something that must fail; it is choosing something whose failure THAT instrument must report. A runtime defect cannot control a static analysis, and a static defect cannot control a runtime probe. THE COST CALIBRATION, which says what the cheap check buys and where it stops: a non-zero exit status catches all three abort arms. It does NOT catch the never-compiled module, where the process exits zero having compiled thousands of modules and simply not the one under test -- AN EXIT CODE IS NOT AN EXECUTION RECEIPT WHEN THE THING THAT DID NOT EXECUTE IS ONE MODULE OUT OF THOUSANDS. Only a must-fail control separates those, and only when control and instrument share a channel. THE CORPUS SIZE IS NOT TRANSCRIBED HERE AND THE INSTRUMENT IS NAMED INSTEAD: `gunbc compile --source-root dag --source-root src/v2` reports both populations on the summary line quoted above, under the policy `gunbc.compile_clean_diagnostic_policy`, and `claim_batch` reports the same denominator as `edge_index_construction.source_files` in its cost-partition receipt. Re-derive it from either producer; the claim rests on the order of magnitude, not on a value. **THE ITERATING FORM, WHERE REPETITION MANUFACTURES THE CONFIDENCE INSTEAD OF EARNING IT (2026-09-05, clever-dove-410).** The specimens above are single reads. Put the same miss inside a loop and the harm inverts the reader's strongest heuristic: a harness sweeping a population one shard at a time, whose per-shard failure is INVARIANT, dies identically on every iteration and reports a growing tally of shards that found nothing. That output is byte-for-byte what a genuinely clean population produces, and it gets MORE convincing the longer it runs. MEASURED: a chunked census over 1199 modules reported 1199 consecutive rounds of zero progress and a final `PASS=0 FAIL=0 failed_probes=0`. Every round had died in under three seconds on one invariant refusal -- `MemoryCgroupBindRefused: the cgroup2 tree is not writable by this process` -- before reaching any subject, and the loop's own requeue logic then walked the whole queue one dropped head at a time. Nothing in the summary distinguished that from a corpus with no defects in it. **THE GUARD IS LIVENESS ACROSS ITERATIONS, NOT A CONTROL WITHIN ONE**, and it is what separates this from its parent form: a positive control proves an instrument CAN report, once; it says nothing about a loop whose every later iteration is dead. The harness must assert its own progress -- refuse after N consecutive iterations that complete no member of the population, and print the raw log of the failing iteration rather than its own tally. Rearmed with that guard, the identical failure was caught on the second occurrence in three seconds. A COROLLARY ON WHERE THE REFUSAL CAME FROM, since it is the honest kind: the tool refused to run UNBOUND when the memory bind it was asked for could not be created, which is correct fail-closed behaviour and not a defect. The manufactured silence was entirely the harness's, which is the general shape -- a loud, typed, correct refusal on every iteration, rendered as a clean sweep by the thing counting iterations. Distinct from `denominator_moved_between_measurement_and_comparison`, where the instrument reached a real subject that had changed underneath it; here the subject never moved and was never reached. Both are cases of a number that describes the measuring rather than the measured, which is why neither is caught by re-running.)
- **a carrier that every lane is INSTRUCTED to append to, serialized so that independent appends CAN LAND IN THE SAME MERGE REGION AND NOTHING DECIDES WHETHER THEY DO, makes collision a standing property of the carrier rather than of any disagreement** (INVALID STATE: two lanes add logically disjoint content -- different rows, different subjects, no shared meaning -- and the textual merge refuses, because the serialization put their edits on the same line or at the same end of the same list. **THE CONDITIONAL IS STATED AS A CAPABILITY AND NOT AS A CERTAINTY, BECAUSE THE TWO ARE DIFFERENT CLAIMS AND AN EARLIER REVISION CONFLATED THEM.** WHERE two appends actually occupy one region their collision is NECESSARY, not merely likely; what varies is WHETHER they land there, and nothing in the carrier or the process decides that. So the invalid state is the CAPABILITY -- a serialization under which two disjoint appends may share a region -- and the frequency is a separate, measured claim about how often that capability is exercised. Stating the antecedent as 'occupy the same region' and the consequent as 'default' asserted certainty and likelihood of the same fact at once, which is why it read as internally inconsistent beside the counterexample below.  Nothing is contested. The authors do not disagree about anything. The conflict is manufactured entirely by the layout of the bytes. **A CLEAN MERGE OF THIS CARRIER IS NOT EVIDENCE AGAINST THE CLASS** -- stated first because the reader who most needs it is the one who stops reading early, and the next person to see a roster merge cleanly will otherwise cite it as a refutation. HARM: it is paid by every lane, on every append, forever, and it is paid in the most expensive currency available -- a human or agent hand-resolving a merge whose correct answer is mechanically determined. Worse, hand-resolution of a mechanical union is where REAL content gets dropped, because the resolver is pattern-matching rather than reading.TWO CARRIERS, ONE MECHANISM, AND THEY ARE FILED AS ONE ROW BECAUSE ONE CAPABILITY RETIRES BOTH. `gunbc.rung_drop` rows serialize as multi-kilobyte SINGLE-LINE blobs, so two lanes editing disjoint FIELDS OF ONE ROW conflict on that line -- three lanes hit it in one night. `gunbc.recurring_failure_mode.roster` is an append-only list with a PARALLEL import block, so two lanes appending different rows conflict twice, once per block. Different shapes -- one row too wide, one list too append-hot -- and the same defect: THE UNIT OF MERGE IS COARSER THAN THE UNIT OF EDIT. Specimens unify on mechanism and never on symptom, and the test that these are one class is that a serialization in which independent appends do not share a merge region retires both at once.RECEIPT: THREE BRANCHES OF ONE SESSION CONFLICTED ON THE ROSTER SIMULTANEOUSLY, WITHIN EIGHT MINUTES, ON THE SAME TWO BLOCKS. Main had appended two rows; each branch had appended its own. The resolution was a union in identical order in both blocks and was mechanical in all three -- no judgement was exercised, and none was available to exercise. That is the diagnostic signature: if every resolution of a conflict class is the same mechanical operation, the conflict carried no information and the merge was asking a question with a computable answer.THE RESOLUTION HAS ITS OWN TRAP, AND A SET-GRAIN CHECK WALKS INTO IT. Roster ORDER is load-bearing in the generated projection, so the union must agree as an ORDERED SEQUENCE and not merely as a set -- a reordering changes the emitted bytes while every membership test stays green. The verification that does work is diffing the import block against the roster list as sequences; the verification that looks equivalent and is not is comparing their lengths or their sets.DISTINCT FROM THE GENERATED-PROJECTION CONFLICT THAT ACCOMPANIES IT, AND THE BOUNDARY DECIDES THE REMEDY. When both sides change a generated artifact, NEITHER side's bytes are the projection of the merged authorities, so the correct answer is a regeneration and a hand-resolution is definitionally wrong. Here both sides' bytes are AUTHORED and both are correct; the union IS the answer and the only defect is that a machine is not computing it. One class must refuse resolution, the other must not require it.RECOGNITION RULE: FOR EVERY CARRIER THE PROCESS INSTRUCTS LANES TO APPEND TO, ASK WHETHER TWO INDEPENDENT APPENDS SHARE A MERGE REGION. If they do, the conflict rate is a function of LANE CONCURRENCY and not of disagreement, so it rises with adoption -- the carrier gets worse exactly as the practice it supports succeeds. The tell is a conflict whose every historical resolution was the same mechanical union, and the sharper tell is a carrier whose instructions tell every lane to write to one place while its serialization assumes one writer.SPECIMEN REQUIRING NO SECOND AUTHOR AND NO DISAGREEMENT (2026-09-04, recurring_failure_mode roster). One author held three appends to this roster's single region across two of their OWN open heads: #10417 adding one row, and #10436 adding two after a class was split in review. The two appends sharing #10436's head do not collide with each other; the collision is between the two heads. Nobody disagreed about anything and there was no second party to coordinate with -- every participant was the same author -- and the enumeration collided regardless. WHAT THAT SPECIMEN DOES AND DOES NOT ESTABLISH, stated because the tempting stronger claim is false. It does NOT show a nonzero conflict floor at concurrency one: the causal variable in the recognition rule above is INDEPENDENT APPENDS, which is a count of HEADS diverging from one base, and git neither knows nor cares that one person authored both -- so this specimen is concurrency two under the only reading the mechanism supports, and an author count of one is not a concurrency of one. What it DOES establish is that the collision needs no disagreement and no second party, so coordination has nothing here to arbitrate. That is what distinguishes a serialization defect from contention, and it is the reading this row should be cited for.**A COUNTEREXAMPLE ARRIVED FROM THIS ROW'S OWN LANDING AND IT NARROWS THE CLAIM, SO IT IS RECORDED HERE RATHER THAN LEFT FOR A READER TO DISCOVER.** An earlier revision of this row said the conflict was a CERTAINTY. It is not. Within the hour of this row merging, a sibling branch of the same session merged main carrying two fresh roster appends and the roster MERGED CLEANLY -- only the generated projection refused. Git's three-way merge resolves two appends whenever they are separated by enough unchanged context; it refuses only when both land at the same insertion point, which is what happened to three branches earlier the same night and is what a strictly append-at-the-end discipline produces. **SO THE HONEST CLAIM IS ABOUT THE DEFAULT AND NOT ABOUT NECESSITY: the collision is a function of whether two appends choose the same insertion point, which nothing coordinates and nothing forbids** -- which is worse than a certainty for diagnosis, because an intermittent mechanical conflict is one people learn to resolve by reflex instead of ranking for repair. The capability in the trigger is unchanged by this: a serialization where independent appends do not share a merge region removes the possibility rather than lowering its rate. **AND THE NARROWING APPLIES TO ONE CARRIER AND NOT THE OTHER, WHICH IS A DISTINCTION WORTH CARRYING RATHER THAN A QUALIFICATION.** The counterexample is about INSERTION POINTS in a list, so it narrows the roster carrier, where two appends may or may not land together. It does NOT narrow the rung_drop case, because there the colliding edits are disjoint FIELDS OF ONE ROW and that row is a single line -- rows still reach 63,174 characters on one line after the per-file split -- so there is no second insertion point for two edits to differ on. **A SHARED LINE ADMITS NO SEPARATION, SO WITHIN A ROW THE COLLISION REMAINS A CERTAINTY WHILE ACROSS A LIST IT IS ONLY THE DEFAULT.** Note also that the per-file split has already landed, so two lanes declaring DIFFERENT drops now write different files and no longer collide at all; what survives is two lanes editing one drop. That is the same capability from the other side: the split separated rows and left fields sharing a region. **AND THAT MAKES rung_drop A WORKED EXAMPLE, IN ONE CARRIER'S OWN HISTORY, OF A PARTIAL FIX SATISFYING THE VISIBLE HALF OF A CAPABILITY WHILE THE OTHER HALF STAYS DEAD** -- which is the §4b(3) shape this trigger was written to resist, demonstrated by the very carrier the trigger is about. The split was real work and it removed a real collision; it is also the artifact that would have been cited to retire this trigger, and every field-level collision would have survived it. The receipt is that this row's own author read the repairing commit as evidence of a live defect and had to be asked before checking -- so the half that got fixed became the visible one and the half that remained became invisible, to the person writing the row about it. **A TRIGGER THAT CAN POINT AT ITS OWN CARRIER'S HISTORY AS THE FAILURE MODE IT PREVENTS IS HARDER TO RETIRE BY AN ARTIFACT THAN ONE THAT ONLY ASSERTS THE RISK.** RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable, and the mitigation is that a resolver reads both sides and unions them by hand. CEILING: structurally impossible -- a serialization in which an independent append occupies its own merge region admits no textual collision between disjoint appends, so the invalid state has no constructor rather than being caught by a check. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, a CAPABILITY and not either carrier's cleanup: independent appends to a shared carrier do not share a merge region, AND the carrier's derived order is computed rather than authored, so that membership and order cannot disagree. Splitting rung_drop into one row per file, or reformatting the roster, satisfies neither half and leaves every other append-hot carrier in the tree exposed in the same way.
- A located refusal renders a CORRECT span in units the reader cannot know, so the location is authoritative, specific, and points at nothing. DESIGN section 5 requires every failure to be a typed, LOCATED diagnostic; this row is about the location surviving the trip to the reader, which the typed-and-located requirement does not by itself secure. RECEIPT, 2026-09-04, gunbc#10350, and it is recorded with both readers because the two failures are the point. The module index refused with `dag/gunbc/type_reference_decl_file_occurrence_census.dag:15713-15714: expected expression, found Colon`. That file is 526 lines in origin/main, in the PR head, and in the merge result. THE FIRST READER -- this lane's manager -- did the correct diligence and was defeated by it. They checked the cited file in all three trees, confirmed no line 15713 exists in any of them, and additionally confirmed the file is not a generated artifact, so they were not reading a pre-regeneration copy. They then declined to guess and named two candidate causes: a wrong PATH attribution, or a line index global across the concatenated corpus. Both were wrong, and neither could have been eliminated from the log. THE ACTUAL ANSWER IS A THIRD THING NEITHER CANDIDATE COVERS: 15713 is a BYTE OFFSET into that file, and the file name is exactly right. `head -c 15760 <file> | tail -c 220` lands on the opening quote of the unterminated literal. The span was never wrong -- only its rendering, which presents a byte offset in the `path:N-M` shape every reader parses as `path:line-line`. WHY THE UNITS ARE UNRECOVERABLE FROM THE ARTIFACT, which is what makes this structural rather than a typo. `path:N-M` is well-formed under both readings, and for any file long enough the byte reading and the line reading are BOTH plausible; the two only visibly diverge when the offset exceeds the line count, which is precisely the case that reads as a corrupt or mislocated diagnostic rather than as a unit mismatch. So a short file hides the defect entirely and a long file misdirects silently. The reader has no way to tell which they are holding. WHAT IT COST, stated because the harm is reader time and would otherwise be invisible: three whole-tree checks by the manager, two candidate mechanisms authored and both false, and an explicit instruction to another session not to chase the line number. The underlying defect -- raw double quotes inside a .dag String literal -- was a one-character-class fix once the file was believed. THIS IS THE FABRICATED-PLAUSIBLE-OUTPUT SHAPE APPLIED TO A DIAGNOSTIC'S OWN SPAN. DESIGN section 5 forbids a fabricated plausible answer on the success path; a span rendered in undeclared units is the same failure on the REFUSAL path, and it is worse than an unlocated refusal rather than better. An unlocated refusal makes the reader search and they know they are searching. A confidently mislocated one makes them verify the location, find it absent, and doubt the path or the tool -- so the strongest-looking part of the diagnostic is the part that misleads. RECOGNITION RULE: when a diagnostic's cited position does not exist in the cited file, check the UNITS before doubting the PATH. Compare the cited number against the file's byte length as well as its line count -- if it falls inside the byte length and outside the line count, it is an offset, and the path is probably correct. The tell that generalises past this instance: a position rendered without a unit is a position that cannot be checked, and `path:N-M` declares no unit. WHAT IS NOT CLAIMED. One receipt is not a rate, and this row does not claim the corpus renders spans in the wrong units generally -- several diagnostic surfaces in this tree do render real line and column numbers, and the note in gunbc.namespace_cut_replacement_plan recording that a pinned compiler prints `msg (file:start-end)` while a current one prints `error[file:line:col]` shows the two renderings coexisting across instruments. The claim is narrower and sufficient: at least one refusal path renders byte offsets in a shape that reads as lines, and nothing in the artifact discloses which. CEILING AND WHY THIS ROW IS FILED RATHER THAN REPAIRED HERE. The class is decidable and therefore has a wall available: a span carrying its unit in its TYPE -- a byte offset and a line-column position as distinct constructors rather than two readings of one integer pair -- makes the ambiguity unwritable, which is DESIGN section 4b rung 4 and section 5's construction-over-validation. That is a change to the diagnostic span carrier and its every renderer, not a fix to the file that surfaced it, so it is filed at the grain it must be repaired at. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, naming the capability rather than an artifact: a span type whose constructors distinguish byte offsets from line-column positions, with the renderers deriving their format from the constructor instead of choosing one. A SIBLING INSTANCE FROM THE SAME NIGHT, ESTABLISHED INDEPENDENTLY BY ANOTHER LANE, which is what lifts this from an anecdote to a class. jolly-ferret-412 (gunbc#10352) found the floor rendering `cost=500ms EXACT` from an integer-truncated NANOSECOND measurement: the comparison and the refusal were both correct, and the rendering was the defect. Two diagnostics in one night where the VALUE was right, the RENDERING was wrong, and the rendering recruited readers into filing against a correct component -- there against the comparator, here against the path. The two arrived from unrelated subjects and neither reader was looking for this shape. THE GENERAL RULE THE PAIR SUPPORTS, and it is the operative half of this row: WHEN A LOCATED DIAGNOSTIC POINTS SOMEWHERE IMPOSSIBLE, DISTRUST THE UNITS OF THE LOCATION BEFORE DISTRUSTING THE LOCATION. The impossible-looking component is usually the correct one being rendered in a unit nobody declared. STANDING BESIDE THIS AND NOT DUPLICATING IT: DESIGN section 3's rule is cite the SYMBOL, not the position, because a position decays silently when anything above it moves. This row is the complementary failure -- the position was not stale, it was in undeclared units -- and both point the same way. A position is the weakest form of citation available, and where one is unavoidable it must at minimum say what it is measuring. 
- Two predicates in one ordered dispatch chain both answer TRUE for the same item, and the wrong answer is suppressed only because the right arm is tested FIRST. Nothing in either predicate excludes the other's subject, so the chain is correct by ACCIDENT OF ORDER rather than by construction. The invalid state -- an item satisfying two mutually exclusive classifications -- remains fully writable, and the guard against it is a line number.RECEIPT, 2026-09-04, gunbc#10350, in v1.compiler.emit_core_support. is_resource_def_item claims any item with no transport, no children, no body and AT LEAST ONE PROPERTY. That is the same property-COUNTING shape v1.compiler.parse already had to repair: `type X sole_constructor { .. }` carries exactly one property, minted by parsed_sole_constructor_properties as a TYPE MODIFIER, so counting properties swallowed 202 sole-constructor types across 100 modules into the resource bucket. The parse side was repaired by asking WHICH property rather than HOW MANY; the emit side still counts.IT IS DORMANT AND THAT IS EXACTLY WHY IT IS DANGEROUS. In all three emitters -- v1.compiler.emit_python, v1.compiler.emit_go, v1.compiler.emit_rust -- is_type_decl_item is tested BEFORE is_resource_def_item, so a sole-constructor type is claimed by the type arm and never reaches the resource arm. The defect therefore produces no wrong output today, files no diagnostic, and appears in no census. It is not prevented; it is UNREACHED.THE DISTINGUISHING FACT, and it is the whole reason this row exists: the mask is ARM ORDER and nothing else. Reordering two predicate arms is the archetypal harmless-looking edit -- a reviewer reads it as style, a formatter or a refactor could motivate it, and no test names the ordering as load-bearing. That single edit would arm a misclassification over 202 items across 100 modules, silently, with the emitters producing resource output for ordinary types. A correctness property whose only guard is the sequence of else-if arms is one careless tidy-up away from firing.THIS IS WHY THE ACCOMPANYING REPAIR DID NOT REORDER. The census refusal that surfaced this class -- CensusUnavailable { cause: DeclarationDomainDisagrees } on `resource Network` and `resource AuthContext` -- could have been made to disappear by moving the resource arm ahead of the type arms. That would have ARMED this defect while greening the instrument, which is the fail-open trade in its purest form: a passing signal bought by widening a latent wrong answer. The repair instead excluded resource items from the type predicates by asking v1.compiler.parse parsed_item_carries_resource_entries, the single authority that already decides what a resource item is, so the two readers agree by DERIVATION rather than by sequence.WHAT IS NOT CLAIMED, because the harm here is potential and stating it as realized would be the same inflation this ledger exists to catch. No wrong output has been observed from is_resource_def_item. The 202-item figure is the count v1.compiler.parse's own note records for the population that WOULD be claimed, measured when the parse-side instance was live, not a count of items currently miscompiled. The claim is precisely: the invalid state is writable, it is currently unreached, and what keeps it unreached is not a property of either predicate.RECOGNITION RULE: when two arms of an ordered dispatch can both answer TRUE for one input, the chain does not classify -- it PRIORITIZES, and the priority is invisible at both definition sites. Ask of any else-if chain whether reordering it would change an answer; if yes, the arms are not a partition and the ordering is load-bearing logic living in syntax. The tell that generalises: a predicate whose correctness argument mentions where it appears rather than what it tests.CEILING AND NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER. The class is decidable and its ceiling is STRUCTURALLY IMPOSSIBLE, DESIGN section 4b rung 4: an item's kind is a closed variant carried positively on the item, so dispatch is a total match over that kind and two arms CANNOT both claim one item -- the overlapping state has no constructor. Today the class sits at MITIGATABLE: the wrong answer is reachable in principle and is contained only by the order of an if-chain, which is not a mechanism that executes a check. The NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, naming the capability rather than an artifact, is: A POSITIVE ITEM KIND CARRIED ON THE EMIT SIDE, from which every emitter's item dispatch is derived as an exhaustive match, so that classification cannot be order-sensitive and a kind belonging to no arm fails to compile. The trigger is NOT the repair that accompanies this row -- that repair removes one overlapping PAIR and leaves the chain a priority list. v1.compiler.parse's note reaches the same place from the other side, calling a positive item kind 'the third receipt' after two shape-predicate defects it had to fix; this is the fourth, and it is on the reader that note deliberately did not touch.CLIMBED TO STRUCTURALLY IMPOSSIBLE, 2026-09-04, and the trigger this row named is what did it -- a POSITIVE ITEM KIND CARRIED ON THE EMIT SIDE. v1.std.core Node carries module_item_kind: ParsedModuleItemKind, stamped by the eight item constructors in v1.compiler.parse from the ItemForm body_kind they were already dispatching on and propagated by every construction that rebuilds a node. All three emitters' item dispatch -- v1.compiler.emit_rust emit_typed_item, emit_go emit_go_typed_item, emit_python emit_py_typed_item -- is now a MATCH over that coproduct rather than an ordered if-chain, so arm order carries no information: a variant selects exactly one arm, two arms cannot both claim one item, and the overlapping state has no constructor. The specific predicate this row was filed against, is_resource_def_item, is deleted along with the four other kind-deciding predicates. THE EXCLUSION THIS ROW WAS FILED BESIDE IS ALSO GONE, and that is the part worth stating plainly: gunbc#10350's repair derived the resource exclusion from v1.compiler.parse parsed_item_carries_resource_entries so the fact had one definition, which was correct and was still a VALIDATOR -- it grows one conjunct per collision discovered and cannot become a positive authority. Both the exclusion and parsed_item_carries_resource_entries are deleted; nothing derives an item's kind any more, because the item carries it. EXECUTED EVIDENCE FOR THE RUNG, because the row's wording is not the measurement. THE DISCRIMINATING RED IS AUTHORABLE AS FIXTURE SOURCE even though no accepted program contains it, which is the case DESIGN section 4b names when it says a compiler's regression probes are invalid programs. A module whose match over the imported ParsedModuleItemKind omits one variant was compiled under the live source roots and REFUSED: `non-exhaustive match: missing variant(s) NotAModuleItem`, at resolve, before any emit ran; the seven-arm control compiled and returned PASS. So a kind belonging to no arm does not compile, which is rung 4 rather than rung 3. That pair is enrolled as v1.tests.claim.item_kind_dispatch_totality_test (in the src/v1 Rust test tree, whose fixture declares its own seven-variant kind because an invalid module cannot live inside a source root that gets scanned); the probe against the REAL type is recorded there as a recipe. THE SECOND PAIR, ENROLLED AND EXECUTED, is v1.tests.claim.item_kind_dissolves_shape_predicates_control_test: a node satisfying EVERY conjunct of the old shape test while carrying ModuleItemResource. It PASSES against the kind-gated predicate and was confirmed RED by running the revert arm -- restoring the shape-only body of is_leaf_type_item in the .dag and re-running the same claim, which reported FAIL -- so the row discriminates rather than merely passing. Per DESIGN section 4b(4) both it and its positive control stay enrolled now that the machinery they falsified is deleted; the generated Rust row a_resource_item_is_not_read_as_a_type_item likewise survives the climb, restamped with the kind each fixture's constructor would have given it. WHAT DID NOT CLIMB, so this row is not read as more than it is: the kind decides WHICH construct is being emitted and says nothing about whether the optional fields that construct reads are Present. Node.body and Node.type_annotation stay `Node?`, so each dispatch arm still matches and refuses rather than unwrapping. That residue is [[kind_carried_but_its_fields_stay_optional]], whose trigger is a per-kind item carrier making each kind's payload non-optional by construction.
- **a refusal is only a refusal if it SURVIVES ITS CALLER -- a producer can be correct at its inability boundary while the composed path still fabricates an answer** (INVALID STATE: a producer returns its typed empty or refusal at an explicit inability boundary, and an intermediate consumer reads that outcome as permission to substitute a default, a nickname, a shallower identity, or an empty-success. The composed path widens even though the producing site is correct in isolation. HARM: the fabricated value reaches a judgment site that believes it is comparing real data, so a mismatch that should have been loud is silently admitted -- section 5's forbidden state, reached by a route in which no single frame did anything obviously wrong.)WHY SITE-LOCAL REVIEW IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO IT, WHICH IS THE WHOLE REASON IT IS A CLASS. Inspection at the producer sees the intended refusal. A unit test at the producer sees the intended refusal. A reviewer reading the diff that ADDS the refusal sees a correct refusal being added. Only following the outcome through EVERY consumer reveals that a later frame absorbs it and recreates an answer -- and nothing in the producer's diff points at the frame that will do so. The defect is not in any line you would think to look at.MEASURED INSTANCE, gunbc#10146: carried_structural_type_name was repaired to return empty at its depth-16 ceiling -- correct, and correct in isolation. Its PARENT FRAME converted that empty back to `here`, so declared_alias_target_matches_produced continued to receive a fabricated shallow identity and alias admission continued to succeed. The first repair was therefore indistinguishable from no repair at the only site that decides, and it was caught on a full-path reread rather than at the edit. THE FIRST FIX WAS ITSELF AN INSTANCE OF THE CLASS IT WAS FIXING.DISTINGUISHING WITNESS, and note that it must assert at the JUDGMENT SITE and not at the producer: construct a chain that reaches the inability boundary, then assert AT THE FINAL JUDGMENT SITE that no fallback identity or value is admitted and the typed refusal or loud mismatch survives unchanged. A witness that asserts at the producer passes against both the broken and the repaired composition and therefore discriminates nothing.REPAIR RULE: A REFUSAL IS ABSORBING ACROSS THE FULL CALLER CHAIN. Consumers may propagate a typed cause or enrich it; none may translate it into a default, a nickname, a shallower identity, an empty-success, or an acceptance. The rule is stated over the chain rather than over any frame, because every frame in the observed instance was locally defensible.BOUNDARY AGAINST THE ABSORBING-FALLBACK ROW, WHICH IS ADJACENT AND NOT THE SAME. There, a FAILURE ARM widens instead of refusing -- the producer is the defect, and the repair is to make that arm refuse. Here the producer ALREADY REFUSES CORRECTLY and a CONSUMER undoes it. Different site, different repair, and no single capability retires both: making failure arms refuse does nothing about a caller that re-absorbs a correct refusal.RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable, and the mitigation was a human performing a full-path reread after a review flagged the site. CEILING: structurally guaranteed -- a typed refusal whose absorption by a consumer is not expressible cannot appear in an Accepted program, and the decidable fact is whether any frame maps a refusal outcome onto a non-refusal value. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, stated as a CAPABILITY and deliberately not as an artifact: the compiler can decide, for a producer's refusal outcome, whether every consumer on every path either propagates it or refuses -- and refuses the program when one converts it to a value. Adding the depth-ceiling witness, or repairing this one parent frame, satisfies none of that and leaves every other producer-consumer pair in the tree exposed identically.
- **the producer's countable artifact is not necessarily the consumer's subject** (INVALID STATE: a claim about a downstream consumer's behaviour, coverage or resource use is derived from an UPSTREAM CARRIER because that carrier is easy to inspect or count, while no receipt binds the carrier to the exact subject or event the consumer actually processed -- and the carrier-to-consumer mapping may transform, suppress, coalesce, repeat or fan out. HARM: every measurement of the carrier can be perfectly pinned and perfectly correct, and the conclusion about the consumer still false, because the two name different objects.
