The error survives arbitrary care applied to the wrong side of the boundary.)THIS IS AN IDENTITY SUBSTITUTION ACROSS A CONSUMPTION BOUNDARY, AND NOT A PROXY METRIC BEING IMPRECISE. The distinction is load-bearing: an imprecise proxy is improved by measuring it better, which is exactly the move that entrenches this defect. Measuring the carrier harder produces a more confident statement about an object nobody asked about.MEASURED INSTANCE ONE -- branch tree substituted for the CI subject. A required workflow runs on `pull_request`, so its subject is the COMPOSED tree T = merge(M, H), never the branch head H. A projection census run on H reported 96/96/96 and was reported as a live safety gap in what CI would accept. Re-measured on T: merge-tree CLEAN, every row present, the relation covering the case exactly. The H-side measurement was TRUE OF H and was never a statement about the consumer. The consumer transforms the carrier before judging it.MEASURED INSTANCE TWO -- head SHAs substituted for paid validation epochs. Merge latency was modelled as raw head count times one CI cycle. The mapping is not a bijection in either direction: a head superseded before its run starts consumes ZERO started executions (the workflow groups by PR with cancel-in-progress false, so several pushes collapse into one pending member), while retries and separate events can make one head consume more than one. Measured across live PRs, started-runs / distinct-heads ranged 0.29 to 0.88 and was never 1. The resource is consumed by STARTED epochs; heads are merely what is easy to count.DISTINGUISHING WITNESS, and it must be taken on the CONSUMER'S side: name the exact subject the consumer processed -- the composed tree's oid, the started run's id -- and assert the claim against THAT, not against the artifact the producer emitted. A witness that measures the carrier passes identically whether or not the substitution occurred, and therefore discriminates nothing.RECOGNITION RULE, phrased so it fires before the measurement is taken: ask WHAT OBJECT THE CONSUMER ACTUALLY READS, and whether any receipt binds your measured artifact to it. If the consumer transforms, filters, batches or supersedes its input, the carrier is a different object and no amount of rigour on the carrier repairs the inference. The tell is a sentence of the form 'I measured X, therefore the consumer of X will Y' with no step establishing that the consumer's subject IS X.BOUNDARY AGAINST instrument_output_read_as_subject_content, WHICH IS ADJACENT AND NOT THE SAME: there a SET is derived from a report whose COMPLETENESS is a property of the reporting tool, and the repair is to know the tool's coverage. Here the carrier may be reported completely and correctly, and the defect is that the consumer's subject is a TRANSFORMATION of it. Knowing your instrument's coverage perfectly does not tell you that CI checks out a merge commit.RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable, and the mitigation was re-measuring on the consumer's subject after a peer challenged the inference -- twice, both times catching a claim already stated as a finding. CEILING: mechanically preventable -- whether a claimed subject is the one a named consumer processes is decidable wherever the consumer's subject is itself an identified artifact (a run id, a tree oid). NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, stated as a CAPABILITY and not as an artifact: a receipt that binds each measurement to the identified subject the consumer processed, so a claim measured on the carrier cannot be stated about the consumer without the join failing loudly. Re-measuring either instance by hand satisfies none of that and leaves every other producer-consumer pair unbound.
- **a claim file builds its discriminating arms and enrols only its accepting control** (the fixtures that would go red are constructed, named and committed, but no assertion consumes them; the one enrolled assertion is the arm whose expected answer is `accepted`, so the file cannot fail on the behaviour it was built to police). IT READS AS COVERAGE FROM EVERY ANGLE THAT DOES NOT COUNT ASSERTIONS. The file is named for the property, its receipts are named for the property, the discriminating inputs are present and correct, and a reader tracing the subject finds everything they expect — because everything they expect IS there except the consumption. This is DESIGN section 4b's decoration reached by a different road than a permanently-green check: the check is not weak, it is ABSENT, while the surrounding material asserts it exists. THE CHEAP DETECTOR IS A RATIO, NOT A READING: count the constructed fixtures or receipts against the enrolled assertions. `n` receipts and one `test fn` is the signature, and it is decidable without understanding the subject at all. Reading the file for meaning is what FAILS here, because the meaning is entirely correct — the author of the specimen understood the class precisely and wrote the right probes. SPECIMEN, and the reason this row does not name a repair: `v2.test.manual.nominal_distinctness_cross_call` constructs three compile receipts — records cross-call, desugared-brands cross-call, and an accepting control — and enrols exactly one `test fn`, `nominal_distinct_control_compiles_ok`. The two discriminating arms have receipts and no assertion. THE ARMS CANNOT BE ENROLLED TODAY AND THAT IS THE ACTUAL FINDING. `v2.compiler.00_compile compile_ingest_staging` composes lex, parse, normalize, resolve and translate and NO INFER STAGE, even though `src/v2/compiler/04_infer.dag` exists on disk and is simply not composed into that pipeline. So those receipts cannot form a nominal-mismatch judgment under any keying of any identity: enrolling them as expecting-red would enrol two assertions that are red today and red for as long as staging cannot type. The file is not negligent; it is BLOCKED, and its silence is the only part that is wrong. Rung found at 1. CEILING for the enrolment itself is 3 once the capability exists. NEXT TRIGGER, capability and not artifact: a type-judging stage reachable from `compile_ingest_staging`, sufficient for those receipts to yield `Rejected` on a cross-call nominal mismatch — NOT merely the existence of `src/v2/compiler/04_infer.dag`, which already exists and satisfies nothing. Until then the honest state is a declared 4b(2) cannot-climb-until row rather than an unenrolled file that reads as coverage. SUBJECT BOUNDARY, recorded because these two were conflated once already during this row's own discovery: this class is about v2 RECORD nominal distinctness through the staging pipeline — `type IntentId { value: String }`, which is what a brand looks like AFTER desugaring. It is NOT the `where brand("...")` refinement walled in gunbc#10337, which is a v1 `04_infer` concern. Same word, two representations, two pipelines; a reader who merges them will conclude the wall already covers this and it does not. 
- **a fixture is reshaped until it matches the reader's grain, and the accommodation is written up as fidelity to the producer** (INVALID STATE: a check is red because the reader under test cannot see the fixture's shape. The author changes the FIXTURE, records why in a careful note asserting that the real producer emits the accommodated shape, and the check goes green -- so the reader's grain defect is now the check's specification and no future edit to the reader can make the check disagree. HARM: it is worse than an absent check, because the note is persuasive and reads as diligence. The one written here even names the empty-fixture failure it was avoiding. MEASURED SPECIMEN (gunbc#10156, adjudicated by execution 2026-09-03). `v2.std.fn_index` `call_reachable_decls` reads a declaration's callees from `decl.output` through `atom_identities_in_node`, which returns the node's own atom identity and those of its IMMEDIATE children. `v2.test.claim.enforcement.determinism_transitive_witness` had first been authored with the callee atom one level deeper, nothing was reachable, and both of its discrimination rows agreed for that shared wrong cause. The repair flattened the fixture and recorded the reason: THE FN-ARROW SKELETON IS FLAT, AND THE FIXTURE MUST BE TOO, because the Rust marshal hoists callee atoms onto the body root. That premise is false. `marshal_generic` emits EVERY node as a Conj record carrying its callee atom as a positional child, so a callee sits one level under the root only when the body IS that single call; a `let`-block body carries its callees two or more levels down. Measured over the live corpus by `v2.lens.fn_index_depth_agreement`: of 1777 fn-arrow declarations, 877 had callees the depth-one reader could not see at all, and the depth-one reader returned 890 of 8908 subtree atom identities. Every consumer of the cross-declaration walk -- `v2.lens.determinism`, `v2.lens.effect_reach`, `v2.lens.live_read_classification` -- was reaching only the declarations whose whole body is one call, and all their fixtures were flat, so all of them were green. **THE TELL IS SPECIFIC AND MECHANICAL: A NOTE THAT JUSTIFIES THE FIXTURE'S SHAPE BY ASSERTING WHAT THE REAL PRODUCER EMITS, WITH NOTHING EXECUTED AGAINST THE REAL PRODUCER.** The fixture is where the producer is absent by construction, so a claim about the producer made inside a fixture file is unfalsifiable in the place it is written; it has to be decided against the live population or it is a story. RUNG FOUND AT: outside the ladder -- the check executed, was gated on, and discriminated nothing about the grain it appeared to fix. CEILING: 2, mechanically preventable, and the reason it is not 3 is that a fixture is authored data: no construction forbids authoring a shape narrower than the population, because a narrower shape is exactly what a MINIMAL fixture legitimately is. NEXT TRIGGER, NAMED AS THE CAPABILITY: **a shape-conformance judgment that decides, by execution against the live producer, whether a fixture's node shapes are inhabited by the population the check claims to range over, and refuses a fixture whose shapes are disjoint from it** -- with a discriminating RED (a fixture whose body shape no live declaration exhibits, refused), an accepted positive control (a fixture shape the live corpus inhabits), and a control proving the judgment reads the producer rather than a second fixture. Until it exists, the standing mitigation is the one applied here: when a fixture is reshaped to make a reader green, the reshape must land beside a row measured on the live population, and the fixture keeps a row at the UNACCOMMODATED shape.)
- **a head reaches main while that head's own verification run is still in flight, so what lands carries no terminal verdict about itself.** INVALID STATE, AT THE GRAIN THAT WAS ACTUALLY OBSERVED: at the instant of the landing there exists no concluded run for the landed ref. WHAT THIS ROW DOES NOT CLAIM, because the evidence does not reach it: WHICH admission path permitted the landing -- an absent constraint, a constraint that treats an unreported required context as not-failing, or a bypass -- is a separate question, and the actor and the timestamps do not settle it. The class is the uncertified landing itself, which is observable from the record alone. THIS ROW IS RANKED FIRST AND IT SUBSUMES THE DETECTION-TIMING CLASSES. If a head can reach main before its checks conclude, then no improvement to WHAT a check detects, HOW FAST it detects it, or WHICH lane it is routed to changes the outcome: the detector was correct and it was consulted after the fact. Any repair argued from detection quality is arguing below this row. HARM: the break lands on main, where every other lane inherits it. The 2026-09-03 outage ran roughly eighty minutes -- main red from 19:49 UTC until #10278 (1af8892e143) deleted four lines -- and during it any lane resolving `gunbc.recurring_failure_mode` could not regenerate its projection, so the blast radius was every concurrent lane touching a generated artifact, not the author's own branch. **SPECIMEN, MEASURED (gunbc#10236, cfe19ea7f48, 2026-09-03).** The pull request's only CI run on its merged head was created at 19:49:40Z; the pull request merged at 19:49:43Z, THREE SECONDS later; that run concluded FAILURE at 20:30:34Z. The interval between the run beginning and the merge is shorter than any job in it takes to check out a tree, so no reading of the record can make the merge a decision taken on evidence. **THE MERGE WAS PERFORMED BY A PERSON, AND THAT IS RECORDED AS AN OBSERVATION ABOUT THE ACTOR, NOT AS A MECHANISM.** `gh api` reports `merged_by: briansrls` on the merged pull request, so the landing was a human action rather than an automated queue advancing a ref. WHY THE ACTOR IS WORTH RECORDING AT ALL: it fixes WHO the repair has to reach -- a repair aimed at an automated arm changes nothing for a landing no automated arm performed, while a repair that puts the run before the landing reaches both. IT DOES NOT FOLLOW, AND THIS ROW DOES NOT SAY, THAT NOTHING STOOD BETWEEN THE ACTOR AND MAIN: that would be a claim about the admission path, and the next receipt keeps this filing's measurement apart from the repository's. DISTINCT FROM `required_evidence_absent_reads_as_evidence_of_pass`, and the two are easily fused because they share a specimen. That row's subject is an ADMISSION ARM whose quantifier folds over the receipts that have arrived, so the empty enumeration admits -- a defect readable in the arm. This row's subject is the LANDING: a ref reached main with no terminal verdict of its own, whatever arm did or did not consider it. One names a wrong answer; the other names the state that answer produced, which is also reachable by paths that arm never touches. **WHAT IS UNMEASURED HERE, AND WHAT IS MEASURED ELSEWHERE, KEPT APART ON PURPOSE.** THIS filing could not read the protection configuration -- branch protection is unreadable from a session token, `gh api` returning 403 -- so nothing below is my own measurement of the ruleset. WHAT THE REPOSITORY DOES RECORD, cited rather than re-derived: `gunbc.merge_lifecycle` holds that `gunbc.repo_ruleset` `desired_ruleset_rules` read ruleset 16178731 back with NO divergence -- active, default-branch-scoped, one required context -- and that the merge was admitted anyway, because the platform decides required contexts on what has REPORTED at the merge instant and a context that has not reported is `expected` rather than `failing`. So on that authority the answer to `was there a constraint` is YES AND IT ADMITTED, which is exactly why this row is written about the UNCERTIFIED LANDING and not about a missing gate. A reader with a token that can read protection can convert that citation into a first-hand measurement; until then this row asserts the landing and nothing about the ruleset, since treating it as ruleset evidence would be the fabrication DESIGN section 5 forbids. **THE POPULATION, WHICH IS WHAT SEPARATES A RATE FROM AN ANECDOTE, AND IT IS CITED RATHER THAN RE-COUNTED.** `gunbc.merge_lifecycle` records, beside the ruleset reading above: across the FORTY MOST RECENTLY MERGED pull requests at that module's measurement, SIX had no `witnesses` run that completed successfully on their own head before `merged_at`. That denominator is a closed, independently discovered population rather than a number read off the current tree, and it is the reason this row is filed as a class at all -- one specimen reads as a bad night, while roughly one merge in seven landing uncertified is a standing property of the landing path. It also answers the question the trigger below is waiting on: whether the construction that deletes this state is worth its cost is a decision about a rate, not about an incident. NOT RE-DERIVED HERE, DELIBERATELY: naming the module that measured it is the citation, and re-counting would mint a second authority for one fact. A later reader wanting a current figure re-runs that module's instrument rather than trusting this sentence. RECOGNITION RULE: for a landed head, read two timestamps and one actor -- the terminal conclusion time of the run on THAT head, and the merge time. If the merge precedes the conclusion, the landing was uncertified regardless of what the run later said, and the actor tells you which of the two repairs applies. The record afterwards looks ordinary in both cases, because the run does eventually conclude and the conclusion is attached to a head that is already in history. RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable, and only by human habit -- the failure is contained after the fact by a repair pull request, and nothing structural stood between the decision and main. **CEILING, AND THE ROW ANSWERS THE QUESTION IT WAS ASKED RATHER THAN ASSERTING A NUMBER: THE CEILING SPLITS BY PATH, AND THE CLASS TAKES THE MINIMUM.** DESIGN section 4b puts external reality OFF the ladder deliberately -- observed, refused or mitigated at a declared boundary, and explicitly not a fifth rung -- so `a merge queue would close this` is only a ceiling if the thing that would refuse is a path this repository CONSTRUCTS. It is not one path here, it is two. PATH ONE, OURS AND ON THE LADDER: a landing taken through an admission decision this repository models. That decision exists -- `gunbc.merge_lifecycle` `merge_enabled` consulting `gunbc.merge_admission` `policy_admits`, with the absent-receipt case now an ARM of the decision rather than a fold seed -- so the invalid state is authorable, the refusal is authorable, and the RED is enrollable against it. CEILING ON THIS PATH: 3, structurally guaranteed, because no `Accepted` landing through our own path carries a head with no terminal verdict. It is not 4 while the same model can still represent a policy that admits one. PATH TWO, NOT OURS AND OFF THE LADDER: a person pressing merge in the hosting platform. Nothing this repository constructs stands on that path, and the platform behaviour that would close it is observed, not authored -- `gunbc.repo_ruleset` `desired_ruleset_rules` reads ruleset 16178731 back with NO divergence while the merge still lands, because the platform decides required contexts on what has REPORTED at the merge instant, and a context that has not reported is `expected` rather than `failing`. The honest form on this path is therefore a BOUNDARY OBLIGATION and not a rung: declare the desired ruleset state, converge it, and OBSERVE every landing -- comparing the landed head against the terminal verdicts that existed at its merge instant -- refusing loudly when there were none. That observation is ours and can climb; the enforcement is not ours and cannot be claimed. Today even the observation is partial, because branch protection is unreadable from a session token, so the boundary is currently held by timestamps alone. **SO THE ROW'S CEILING IS THE BOUNDARY OBLIGATION, WHICH IS THE MINIMUM ACROSS ITS IN-SCOPE PATHS**, per 4b(1): a class's rung is the minimum over the paths in scope, and this class's specimen is on the path we do not own. Claiming structural guarantee for the whole class on the strength of path one would be exactly the inflation 4b(1) names -- citing the strongest path while the one the incident happened on stays silent. What a merge queue changes is not that we PROVE anything; it is that path two stops existing, and the class collapses onto path one. That is a real repair and it is why the trigger still names it -- but until it is configured, this row is a declared boundary, and it says so. RECORDED BECAUSE A READER WILL NOTICE IT: the sibling row `required_evidence_absent_reads_as_evidence_of_pass` states CEILING: structurally guaranteed on the same merge-queue construction. This row does not amend that one -- its subject is an admission arm, which is nearer to path one -- but the split above is the narrower reading, and a reader reconciling the two should reconcile them in that direction: the queue is a construction we can ADOPT, not one we can PROVE from here. NEXT TRIGGER, NAMED AS THE CAPABILITY AND IN TWO PARTS BECAUSE THE CEILING HAS TWO PATHS: `gunbc.guarantee_stall` `merge_admission_terminal_verdict_stall` -- (a) a landing path in which the verdict on the ref that lands is a PRECONDITION of the landing, sufficient to refuse a merge whose head carries no terminal conclusion, human actor included, which is what removes path two rather than measuring it; and (b) until that exists, a landing OBSERVATION over merged heads, sufficient to refuse when the landed head carried no terminal verdict at its merge instant, which is the declared boundary discharging its own obligation. The stall was ruled open by the operator on 2026-09-03 -- "Leave the hole open for now - i know merging is not sound" -- so (a) is held by ruling and not by omission, and (b) is what this row asks for meanwhile. A dashboard, a reminder, or a review convention does not discharge it: each leaves the same person the same three seconds.
- **a branch appends a declaration to an append-only carrier that its OWN MERGE BASE already carries, and the three-way text merge reports nothing, because both sides are INSERTIONS AT DIFFERENT OFFSETS and text merge is offset-based rather than identity-based.** INVALID STATE: one module declares one name twice, each copy byte-identical to the other, arriving through a merge that never saw a conflict. **SPECIMEN, MEASURED (gunbc, 2026-09-03).** `absence_classifier_default_bucket` and `green_reported_over_a_population_the_instrument_does_not_own` were first added to `gunbc.recurring_failure_mode` by gunbc#10166 (2bba578d56e) at 07:29Z. gunbc#10236 (cfe19ea7f48) added BOTH a second time at 19:49Z; its entire diff on that file is `+4` lines and nothing else. `git merge-base --is-ancestor 2bba578d56e cfe19ea7f48` holds, so the branch re-added two declarations that ALREADY EXISTED IN ITS OWN BASE. Counting additions of those two declaration lines per commit touching the file scores #10236 at 2 and #9981, #10269, #10252 and #10248 at 0 each, which is what separates the cause from the traffic around it. **THE ROW EXISTS TO NAME THE BELIEF IT FALSIFIES, WHICH IS WRITTEN DOWN IN THE CARRIER'S OWN HEADER.** `gunbc.recurring_failure_mode` says of its split into rows: "two lanes touch two declarations and never collide, while two lanes editing the SAME class still conflict -- which is correct, because that is real disagreement about one fact". THAT HOLDS FOR EDITS AND FAILS FOR APPENDS. An edit to an existing region is anchored to context both sides share, so a text merge sees the overlap; an append is anchored to nothing, so two appends of the SAME identity at two offsets are, to the merge, two unrelated additions. And an append is the only operation an append-only roster performs -- so the carrier's contention model was reasoned entirely about the case it does not have. HARM, AND WHY IT ARRIVES LATE AND WIDE: a duplicate declaration is refused at RESOLVE, loudly and correctly -- `duplicate declaration <name> in module gunbc.recurring_failure_mode`, located and naming the module -- but the refusal fires when someone resolves the authority, which is after landing. On 2026-09-03 that was main, for roughly eighty minutes, and it stopped every concurrent lane that needed to regenerate a generated artifact, not the author who wrote it. **WHY THE PROJECTION-FIDELITY GATE WAS GREEN THROUGHOUT, which is the part a reader will otherwise assume was drift.** `gunbc.design_ledgers` projects `docs/design-failure-modes.md` by mapping over the ROSTER, and the roster names each identity exactly once, so a duplicated DECLARATION renders nowhere. The projection was byte-correct, the drift gate had nothing to report, and deleting the duplicates changed no projection bytes. A check whose subject is the projection cannot see a defect in the declaration set the projection selects FROM. DISTINCT FROM `premise_that_a_shared_subject_means_disagreement`, WHICH QUOTES THE SAME HEADER SENTENCE AND IS A DIFFERENT FINDING. That row's invalid state is a PREMISE that classified a recurring cost -- author time spent hand-negotiating byte order inside one row -- as intended, so nobody measured it. This row's invalid state is a MERGE RESULT: the same sentence also asserts that lanes touching two declarations "never collide", and the failure here is that two lanes appending the SAME declaration collide semantically while not colliding textually, which lands a module that cannot resolve. One sentence, one axis missed on each side: that row loses a cost, this row loses a refusal. RECOGNITION RULE: for any carrier whose normal operation is APPEND, ask what the merge is comparing. If it compares LINE REGIONS, then identity collisions between two appends are invisible by construction, and the absence of a conflict is evidence about offsets and about nothing else. The tell in the record is a diff that is purely additive, mergeable without incident, and semantically a duplicate of content already above it. DISTINCT FROM `authority_merges_unprotected_while_its_projection_is_guarded`: that row is about which merge DRIVER a path resolves to. This row holds even where the driver is correct, because a driver that merges text is still merging text -- the defect is the grain of the comparison, not its configuration. DISTINCT FROM `append_only_carrier_whose_serialization_shares_a_merge_region`, WHICH LANDED ON MAIN WHILE THIS ROW WAS IN REVIEW AND IS THIS ROW'S EXACT DUAL. That class is a CONFLICT THAT CARRIES NO INFORMATION: two lanes append logically disjoint rows, the serialization puts them in one merge region, and the textual merge refuses a union whose answer is mechanically determined. This class is A COLLISION THAT PRODUCES NO CONFLICT: two lanes append the SAME identity, the serialization puts them at different offsets, and the textual merge accepts a result that cannot resolve. Same carrier, same merge, opposite errors -- one refuses where it should union, the other unions where it should refuse -- and neither repair is the other's. A serialization in which independent appends do not share a merge region retires that row and leaves this one exactly where it is, because the defect here is not WHERE the comparison happens but WHAT it compares: offsets rather than identities. This row was originally filed as `append_only_carrier_re_adds_what_its_own_base_already_carries` and renamed on the merge that brought the two together, because two rows opening with the same four words is the fusion DESIGN section 3 warns about. RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable. The compiler refuses the resulting state totally and with a located diagnostic, so the ordinary floor held and no wrong answer was produced; what was unheld is everything before the refusal. CEILING: structurally guaranteed. The merge of an append-only carrier is decidable at IDENTITY grain -- the declaration sets of base, ours and theirs are enumerable, and a result whose declaration set repeats an identity can be refused as a merge result rather than discovered as a compile failure later. It is not rung 4 while the carrier's contents are reached through a general-purpose text merge that can construct any byte sequence. NEXT TRIGGER, NAMED AS THE CAPABILITY AND NOT AS A DIAGNOSTIC THAT WOULD CONTRIBUTE TO ONE: a merge over append-only carriers that combines DECLARATION SETS rather than line regions, sufficient to REFUSE a merge result in which one identity is declared twice, with a discriminating RED (base carrying an identity, one side re-adding it at a different offset, refused) and an accepted positive control (two sides appending two distinct identities, merged additively). A parse-time or resolve-time duplicate diagnostic is a better message about the same landed state and does not discharge this trigger, because it still fires after the merge that should not have produced it.
- **a head merges carrying a verdict that was reached against a base main has since moved away from, so the evidence certifies a combination that no longer exists.** INVALID STATE: the verdict is real, terminal and green; the tree it was computed over is not the tree that results from the merge, and nothing at the merge instant represents the age of the verdict or the distance between those two trees. **SCOPED FIRST, BECAUSE MIS-SCOPING THIS IS WHAT SENT THE FIRST TWO READINGS OF THIS OUTAGE WRONG: this class is ANCILLARY TO THE 2026-09-03 OUTAGE AND IS EXPLICITLY NOT ITS CAUSE.** gunbc#9981 (8b2323f4145) merged at 20:29Z with its last verdict FIFTY-TWO MINUTES stale. It added `exact_duplicate_match_arm_not_refused`, a NEW identity colliding with nothing, onto a tree that had already been broken for forty minutes -- main went red at 19:49Z. So #9981 neither created the duplicate declarations nor could its own contents have collided with anything; it merged INTO a break rather than causing one. The cause is filed as `duplicate_declaration_arrives_through_a_clean_merge`, and the landing that carried it as `head_landed_by_hand_before_its_own_verification_reported`. IT IS FILED ANYWAY BECAUSE THE CLASS IS REAL AND ITS SPECIMEN IS CLEAN: the same measurement that clears #9981 of causing the outage exhibits, in one record, a merge admitted on evidence about a base fifty-two minutes gone. Declining to file it because this instance was harmless would leave the class known only inside a narrative that concluded it was not to blame. HARM: a merge is a JOIN of two trees, and a verdict on either side alone is evidence about a part. Where a verdict is stale, the result of the merge has been tested by nobody -- and, as here, main may already carry a refusal the incoming head has never met, so a lane can merge cleanly into a tree that cannot resolve and read its own green as evidence that it can. RECOGNITION RULE: at any landing, compare the time of the head's last terminal verdict with the time of the merge, and the base that verdict was computed over with the base actually merged into. Staleness is a fact about a PAIR, so it is not visible in either record alone -- the run page shows a green run and the merge page shows a merge, and only the join of the two shows that they are about different trees. DISTINCT FROM `head_landed_by_hand_before_its_own_verification_reported`, which is the case where there is NO terminal verdict on the landing head at all: there the evidence is absent, here it exists and is about the wrong subject, and the second cannot be repaired by waiting longer for the first. DISTINCT ALSO FROM `stale_base_branch_reads_as_pending_work`, which is an ANALYST reading a stale base as unfinished work; this row is a LANDING taken on a stale verdict. RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable, by habit and by whoever notices main is red. CEILING: structurally guaranteed, by the same construction that closes the row above it -- a merge queue verifies the QUEUED REF, which is the join rather than either side, so a verdict about a superseded base cannot be the evidence a landing rests on. That single construction discharges both rows, which is a reason to price it once rather than twice. NEXT TRIGGER, NAMED AS THE CAPABILITY: `gunbc.guarantee_stall` `merge_admission_terminal_verdict_stall` -- a landing path whose precondition is a terminal verdict over the MERGED tree, sufficient to refuse a landing whose evidence was computed over a base the target has moved off. A staleness threshold expressed in minutes does not discharge it: it makes the same wrong subject acceptable below some age, which is the same class with a parameter.
- **container status read as a claim about its members** (an aggregate field on a CONTAINER is read as though it asserted something about the container's MEMBERS. The field is not stale, not censored and not lying: it is answering a question about the container, faithfully, and the reader asked a question about a member. Every conclusion drawn is then true of the wrapper and false of the contents. **THE BOUNDARY AGAINST `instrument_output_read_as_subject_content`, which is the nearest row and has a different repair.** There the completeness of a SET is a property of the REPORTING TOOL rather than of the subject, and the repair is to bind the report to a subject-side enumeration. Here nothing is incomplete -- the container reports its own state correctly and totally -- and the repair is to READ THE MEMBER COLLECTION INSTEAD, which is a different call, not a wider one. Widening the container query returns more containers, all equally silent about members. **SPECIMEN WITH A RECEIPT, measured 2026-09-04 on GitHub Actions run 33853722488 (gunbc#10382), and it is a specimen precisely because THREE sessions read one run within an hour and all three concluded wrongly.** A cancel was REQUESTED from a session at 08:47Z -- a client action, not a run event, and the only non-run timestamp in this paragraph. Read per job via `/actions/runs/33853722488/jobs`: five jobs cancelled between 08:51:23Z and 08:52:16Z, one skipped, and `witnesses` never scheduled. The run's own status stayed `queued` for the next twenty-one minutes and its final conclusion is `cancelled`, while the job that actually decided its completion has conclusion `failure` -- it acquired a runner at 09:13:18Z, ran for FOUR SECONDS, and failed at 09:13:22Z. So the container says cancelled and a member says failure; the container says queued and five of seven members are already terminal. Both container answers are correct about the container. **THE THREE WRONG READINGS, kept because the pattern is in how they failed rather than in that they failed.** (1) `the cancel is queued behind the thing it is cancelling` -- inferred from the run status, when the cancel had already succeeded on five members including three that had acquired named runners. (2) `the aggregate follows its laggard, so a mostly-cancelled run READS as live` -- right about the mechanism, wrong about the harm: the run was not misreporting, it was genuinely unfinished. (3) `a run status is not monotone` -- an in_progress -> queued transition read from the run LISTING, never reproduced against the run resource, and withdrawn. Each reading was a different wrong theory about ONE MISSING JOB QUERY. **THE COST IS REAL AND IS NOT A REPORTING ARTIFACT, which is the distinction the first two readings fused.** `.github/workflows/witnesses.yml` sets `cancel-in-progress: false` and keys the concurrency group per pull request, so the live-head run for the same PR could not register a single check while this one stood. TWO INTERVALS ARE MEASURABLE HERE AND THEY ARE DIFFERENT SUBJECTS, which is worth stating because they coincide in this specimen and diverge in the general case: DEAD TIME UNDER HOLD, last member ends to next member starts, 08:52:16Z -> 09:13:18Z = 1262s, during which ZERO members ran; and GROUP HOLD, last member ends to container settles, 08:52:16Z -> 09:13:22Z = 1266s, during which the group was UNAVAILABLE TO SUCCESSORS. They differ by four seconds here and by the laggard's whole runtime in general. Dead time prices WASTED CAPACITY; group hold prices BLOCKED SUCCESSORS, and a starvation question is always the second one. The laggard's own history is the sharpest datum: `witnesses` sat queued 2,601 seconds from run creation, acquired srv3-01, ran four seconds and failed -- forty-three minutes of group occupancy purchasing four seconds of work and no verdict. THE QUANTITY NOT TO MEASURE, because it is the one a reader reaches for first: lag between the last member transition and the container's resolution is ZERO BY CONSTRUCTION, since the laggard's completion IS the container's completion. It will always return ~0 and always tell you nothing. **SPECIMEN TWO, measured by warm-seal-35 and re-measured here at 09:27Z on a four-PR table, and it is what turns this from a restatement into a generalisation: THE CONTAINER IS A PULL REQUEST, NOT A WORKFLOW RUN.** All four rows read live and open unless marked: gunbc#10365 OPEN base=session/eager-otter-105 check_runs=0 CLEAN; gunbc#10387 OPEN base=main check_runs=0 BLOCKED (this row's own PR); gunbc#10300 OPEN base=main check_runs=6 BLOCKED; gunbc#10333 MERGED base=main check_runs=14 UNKNOWN. CLEAN is a CORRECT AND TOTAL statement about the container -- nothing is currently BLOCKING the merge, and with no members registered nothing blocks. The reader wants `did the checks pass`, a fact about MEMBERS. A session came within one query of merging an entirely unevaluated head on #10365, stopped only by counting check-runs beside a control. **THE TABLE SAYS THE SILENCE RUNS BOTH WAYS, AND THE SECOND DIRECTION IS THE ONE THAT HIDES.** Hold the members at ZERO and vary the base: #10365 and #10387 have identical empty member sets, are both open, and read CLEAN and BLOCKED respectively -- the verdict flips on the base and its protection while the members stay fixed, so it is not merely silent about members, it varies with something else entirely. Now hold the base at main and vary the members: #10387 has ZERO check-runs and #10300 has SIX, and BOTH READ BLOCKED -- the verdict is constant across a state where no member exists and a state where six exist and are pending. Neither direction carries information about members. The CLEAN case is the dangerous one because it invites a merge; the BLOCKED case is the quiet one, because two materially different states are reported in the same word and nobody investigates a BLOCKED. **AND THE CONTROL IN THIS SPECIMEN DECAYED WHILE IT WAS BEING WRITTEN, WHICH IS THE ROW'S OWN CLASS ONE LEVEL UP.** #10333 was first cited as the check_runs=14 / UNKNOWN contrast; it had already MERGED, so its UNKNOWN is driven by LIFECYCLE, a third variable, and the verdict half of that pair isolated nothing. Its nonzero check_runs still serves as a control for the QUERY, which is all it is now used for. A specimen whose control silently changes subject is the same defect the row files. **SPECIMEN THREE, also warm-seal-35, same container type but a DIFFERENT FIELD, which shows the class is not a property of one endpoint.** A falling series of `actions/runs?status=in_progress` was published as evidence that the CI pool was losing capacity. A peer counted `runner_name` over in-progress JOBS instead: 38 concurrent across srv1-srv4, all four serving. `in_progress` counts RUNS, and a run holds that status until its LAST member resolves -- so a saturated pool doing SLOWER work produces exactly the same falling series as a shrinking one, and the container-level series fits both stories while discriminating neither. **SPECIMEN FOUR, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT SHOWS THE RULE IS NOT ENOUGH: the two authors of this row committed the class again, in one exchange, hours after filing it.** A run was cancelled; the cancel was claimed complete from the POST return. The second reader corrected that from the RUN field -- status queued, conclusion null -- and concluded the cancel had not taken. The truth was one level below both: at job grain, SIX OF SEVEN JOBS WERE TERMINAL in each of two runs, and the only survivor was the aggregating job, unscheduled. The cancel had taken everywhere it could. Three stops, three grains, and the correct one was reached last. **WHY THE RULE FAILED TO FIRE ON ITS OWN AUTHORS, in the second author's own account: the grain rule had been written down that morning as `wait on the JOB set, never on the run reaching a status` -- AND FILED UNDER A VERB INSTEAD OF UNDER A SUBJECT.** It was then applied only to WAITING. It is not about waiting. It governs every question asked of a container -- cancelled, started, finished, held, superseded -- because in each one the answer is a fact about MEMBERS, and the container field does not own it. A rule indexed by the verb of the question it was discovered under will not be retrieved for the next verb, and the next verb is where it is needed. **THE SUFFICIENT CLAIM, STATED WITHOUT AN AGGREGATION LAW NOBODY MEASURED: A CONTAINER FIELD DOES NOT OWN PREDICATES OVER ITS MEMBER POPULATION.** That forbids every coercion this row is about while leaving the container's computation rule OPAQUE, which is the stronger position: it cannot be falsified by an upstream change to a scheduler nobody here controls. An earlier draft of this row asserted that the container reports its LEAST-ADVANCED member -- an aggregation law licensed by nothing that was measured. Three samples were all consistent with it and none was a differential that varied ONE member's state while fixing the rest, which is the only shape that could have refused it. That is this roster's `population_selector_that_cannot_admit_a_counterexample` committed inside the row filing its neighbour, and it is withdrawn here rather than shipped. **WHAT IS VERIFIABLE IS OURS, NOT UPSTREAM'S**: in this repository's emitted workflow the aggregating job declares `needs: [required-witnesses-build, required-witnesses-floor]`, so it is last by OUR topology, and a run cannot be terminal before its last job is. That is a fact about bytes this repository generates. The operative consequence survives on insufficiency alone: for this workflow the run-level field is never the right read. Three sightings in nine hours are one object, not a flaky scheduler -- a run queued twenty-one minutes with six jobs terminal, and two more with the identical split. Observed from the healthy direction on the same day: a PR sat eighty-plus minutes with six jobs green and the aggregator queued while the fleet ran twenty-eight concurrent jobs across four servers. For this workflow the run-level field is NEVER the right read. **THE MOST DANGEROUS PART IS THAT THE OPERATIVE ADVICE SURVIVED THE WRONG JUSTIFICATION.** `the group is still held` was true under both readings -- a cancel that succeeds completely still does not release the slot while one member stays unscheduled -- so the practical guidance never changed and nothing in the outcome flagged that its reasoning was false. Right answer, wrong instrument, no signal. That is the state in which a container-level read survives indefinitely: it is not producing visibly wrong advice, it is producing correct advice for a reason that will fail silently the first time the two diverge. **RECOGNITION RULE: name the subject of the field before drawing the conclusion.** If the sentence you are about to write says `the run`, `the batch`, `the suite`, `the deployment` or `the transaction`, and the fact you need is about a job, an item, a test, an instance or a statement, you are one query short. THE MEASURED CORPUS IS THREE INSTANCES IN ONE SYSTEM, NOT FIVE DOMAINS: batch/item, suite/test, deployment/instance and transaction/statement are ILLUSTRATIONS OF THE SHAPE and carry no receipt here. They are named so a reader recognises the shape elsewhere, not as evidence it has been observed there -- an unmeasured analogy sitting beside real receipts borrows their credibility, and the next reader cannot tell which is which. The tell is that the conclusion is about a plural and the evidence is about a singular. The check is cheap and it is the whole repair: fetch the member collection and count it. **AND A COROLLARY THAT COST MORE THAN THE ORIGINAL ERROR: a count printed beneath the member table it summarises is not evidence, it is a second claim.** In this specimen the seven-row job table was pasted correctly and the sentence under it said `six of seven cancelled` when the table shows five; it was then relayed into a downstream worker's brief and only caught when that worker added the rows up. A member listing does not immunise the summary standing next to it -- the arithmetic is the check, and nobody ran it for two hops. **RUNG: 1 (mitigatable).** The class is nameable and, once found, held open by the habit of fetching the member collection. **CEILING: 2 (mechanically preventable) for our own consumers, not higher:** where the container is an EXTERNAL system's API the grain is outside the modeled guarantee, so no constructor of ours can make the confusion unwritable; what is reachable is a typed accessor per external container that has no member-count-shaped field at all, forcing the member query. **NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, a capability and not an artifact: a modeled external-container binding whose read surface makes THE MEMBER QUERY THE ONLY PATH TO A MEMBER FACT.** Omitting aggregates that merely LOOK numeric is not sufficient and specimen two is why: `BLOCKED` is not member-count-shaped in any way, and it is read as a member fact regardless. The trigger is satisfied only when no container-level field can be reached where a member fact is what the consumer needs -- so a consumer cannot spell the aggregate-about-members sentence in the first place. 
- **an actuator's verdict channel says repair is required while its requested effect channel performs no repair.** INVALID STATE: an invocation accepts an effect-shaped option, runs the producer, and correctly refuses on drift, yet the option does not select a worktree-writing arm and the named worktree stays unchanged. Verdict and effect are independently readable facts with no typed outcome binding them: the verdict says generated bytes must be installed while read-back of the requested destination says the invocation installed nothing. This is specification-without-execution inverted at the effect boundary: execution happened and found the defect, but the actuator did not perform the effect its invocation appeared to request. MEASURED SPECIMEN (2026-09-04), WITH THE RELAYED EXIT RECEIPT RETRACTED RATHER THAN PRESERVED: `claim_executor --required-regen --write` prints `required-regen: FAIL generated surface drift: compiler_tests.rs, std_measure.rs`, returns exit 1, and leaves `git status` clean. The earlier exit-0 observation was the status of a trailing pipeline command, not the actuator; nothing here claims process success. The effect disagreement DOES reproduce: `--write` is consumed only by the unrelated partition-crate emitter, while required regeneration always writes below `target/stage0-regen-candidate/src/` and never installs those bytes into the worktree. THE NEAR MISS IS A DISTINCT RECOGNITION FACT: `cat target/stage0-regen-candidate/std_measure.rs` answers `No such file or directory`; only `target/stage0-regen-candidate/src/std_measure.rs` exists. A reader checking the destination named by the candidate directory but missing its extra `src/` level obtains a second plausible observation that the actuator produced nothing. Recognition rule: for every effectful actuator, perturb a subject into refusal and require one typed terminal outcome to agree with an independent read-back of the declared effect destination; neither log text, exit status, nor a clean source tree may silently outrank the others. HARM IS MEASURED, WHILE CAUSATION IS STATED AS AN INFERENCE: gunbc#10273 genuinely ran regeneration twice, then shipped a stale mirror; main remained red for roughly two hours and every pull request in that interval inherited a failure indistinguishable from its own. The verdict/effect split is the most likely mechanism by which those runs were mistaken for completed repairs, not a proven fact about the author's mental path: branch history proves the stale candidate deletion and later unregenerated authority edit, while gunbc#10467 independently records that the surviving route produces candidate bytes that still require installation. RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable. The process outcome and diagnostic are loud and correct, but repair depends on a person discovering and copying candidate bytes despite having invoked `--write`; the invalid interface state remains writable. CEILING: structurally impossible, because verdict and applied effect are decidable products of one modeled operation and need not be independent channels: a closed terminal result can carry Refused with no committed effect, CandidateProduced with explicit unapplied destinations, or Applied with an independently read-back value, with no constructor for an effect request silently ignored by this mode. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER: an effect-actuation capability whose closed result couples requested mode, verdict, declared destination, application status, and independent read-back, sufficient for required regeneration either to install and verify every named mirror or to return typed CandidateProduced locations that cannot be consumed as Applied. 
- **a fail-closed gate predicates evaluation on base-revision health and therefore refuses the change that restores that health.** INVALID STATE: the gate correctly declines to compare an unreadable base, but has no representable arm for `the base defect is exactly the subject this change removes`. Its safety property becomes self-sealing: every ordinary change is rightly refused while the repair is refused for the same reason, so no gate-admitted transition can restore the precondition the gate requires. MEASURED SPECIMEN (2026-09-04): gunbc#10390 left an unattached `//` block at the tail of `dag/test/claim/emit_copy_qualification_witness_test.dag`; the base revision then failed parsing with 16 diagnostics. On gunbc#10428, which moved that block onto a declaration and made the file parse again, the namespace-wave-admission phase reported `NotEvaluated -- dag/test/claim/emit_copy_qualification_witness_test.dag does not parse at the base revision (16 diagnostic(s)), so its base-side declarations cannot be read`. The refusal was correct, and #10428 could repair main only because it landed despite that refusal. Its next run was green. DISTINGUISHING FACTS: nothing widened, defaulted, or fabricated, so this is NOT `absorbing_fallback`; the gate preserved ignorance as NotEvaluated exactly as fail-closed requires. The defect is transition reachability, not verdict truth. Recognition rule: for every gate that requires a healthy base, construct a base violating that precondition and a head differing only by removal of the violation; if the same refusal covers both that repair and an unrelated change, the gate has no repair discriminator and its invariant is self-sealing. HARM: a below-floor parse defect on main made its own direct repair unmergeable by the required admission mechanism, and every other pull request inherited the refusal until an operator used a route outside that mechanism. A gate intended to bound change thereby extended the outage and required an unmodeled exception at the moment its guarantee mattered most. RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable by operator inspection and exceptional landing, with the invalid transition still writable and no mechanism distinguishing it. CEILING: mechanically preventable, not structural today: admitting a purported repair from the head alone is the obvious fail-open trap, because an ordinary change can conceal, relocate, or replace the unreadable base subject while claiming repair. A safe arm must establish both a bounded defect identity on the base and monotone removal of only that defect without pretending to compare base semantics the parser could not produce. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER: a revision-scoped recovery-parse and change-classification capability sufficient to locate a bounded base defect in raw source, prove that the head removes that same defect, and prove that no outside-scope semantic change rides with it; the gate may then admit only that typed Repair transition while continuing to refuse every unclassified NotEvaluated change. 
- a live authority name carries a superseded claim. A rename or authority replacement sweeps prose for the NAME, every occurrence is updated, every updated occurrence resolves -- and the PROPOSITION each name was embedded in is never re-read. The citation is now current and the sentence around it is false. **THIS IS NOT A STALE CITATION AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE WHOLE CLASS.** A stale name is visibly stale: it fails to resolve, or it names something a reader can see is gone. A REFRESHED name is worse, because the refresh is itself an attestation -- it tells the reader that someone recently looked at this sentence and found it correct. The sweep supplies that signal without having supplied the reading. Neighbouring classes differ at the referent, not here: `positional_citation` and `stable_citation_mutable_referent` are about the pointer decaying underneath a claim that stayed true, and this is the exact inverse -- the pointer is repaired and the claim is what died. **RECEIPT (eager-pike-541, 2026-09-04, gunbc#10358).** The PR renamed `gunbc.model.choice` to `gunbc.model.ollama_choice` and updated the citation in `docs/plans/spark-fleet-hand-edit-gap-analysis.md` section 7 accordingly. The updated paragraph continued to state that serving configuration identity derives from the runner's argv -- a mechanism `ObservedOllamaLaunchConfiguration` had ALREADY REPLACED before the rename was proposed. The rename was correct, the new name was correct, and the sentence carrying it had been false since a prior change. Found by external review as REQUEST_CHANGES (`review 59866`), not by any mechanism in the tree. **WHY NO EXISTING WALL SEES IT, stated as a boundary rather than a gap to be closed by the neighbour.** gunbc's namespace-wave admission (`gunbc.namespace_wave_admission`) enumerates AUTHORED NAME OCCURRENCES resolved per module and adjudicates every changed binding -- on this same PR it correctly demanded 255 `TargetChanged` rows. It does not inspect prose, and it is not supposed to: DESIGN section 4c makes annotations invisible to every semantic pass, and a `.md` plan is further out still. So a fully green namespace wave is positive evidence about the executable binding surface and NO evidence about whether the prose describing that authority remains true. The two are complementary walls, not a strong and a weak version of one wall. **RECEIPT (warm-ant-80, 2026-09-05, source 06a30d90376d5fb1fef91d6963604e3b053b9df1).** The annotation on `gunbc.instruments.opaque_realization_census.read_emitted_tree` says `shell.Find.FilesByNameSorted` reports sort's success even when find fails, and says a partial walk cannot be detected until that transport changes. The named operation already captures find's exit status, sorts only after find succeeds, and returns the captured failure; its adjacent `find_pipe_shell_emit_dissolution_trigger` describes that repair. The pointer still resolves, but the proposition it accompanies has been superseded. While investigating an artifact-digest difference, a manager relied on the annotation and directed this lane to file the repaired transport defect as newly live. Reading the operation stopped that false filing; no new transport defect was established. The source comparison and the withdrawn instruction are the evidence here, not an execution of a broken listing. **SECOND RECEIPT (warm-ant-80, 2026-09-05, same source revision).** The header of `tools.emission_entry_instrument` says a scoped compile fits on the runner that killed a whole-corpus compile, citing `gunbc.whole_corpus_compile_admission`. That authority's scope note explicitly withdraws the fit claim, citing clever-tern-899's 2026-08-28 scoped control produced by `gunbc compile` with entry `src/v2/std/node.dag`: the observed demand exceeded that runner's reported budget. A manager relied on the surviving header to redirect this lane to a route as though its fit were established, then withdrew the instruction after reading the cited authority. The grain claim survives: one entry's closure differs from the whole-corpus population. The runner-specific fit claim does not. The instrument restated a proposition rather than deriving it, so the authority's withdrawal did not reach its restatement. **THE SAME FAILURE WITHOUT A NAME-REFRESH HUNK.** A repair or a withdrawn measurement can invalidate a sibling's prose while leaving every cited name unchanged. The repair's own dissolution trigger does not enumerate or adjudicate those descriptions. Here the surviving citations lent authority to claims that caused wrong instructions. This extends the specimen boundary beyond rename sweeps: a resolving pointer is no attestation that its surrounding proposition was rechecked after the referenced behavior changed. **RUNG: MITIGATABLE.** External review caught this instance; nothing in the tree can. The ceiling is not structural impossibility: the truth of an arbitrary proposition about a mechanism is not decidable from the Node tree, so the honest ceiling is MECHANICALLY PREVENTABLE. **NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER: a claim census** -- an instrument that takes the prose citations a rename or authority replacement TOUCHED and surfaces, for adjudication, the proposition each one sits inside, refusing the change until each is dispositioned. That is a real instrument and it does not exist; naming it here is the trigger, not a claim of coverage. **RECOGNITION RULE: any diff that updates a name inside prose it did not otherwise read.** The tell is a citation-only hunk -- one identifier changed, the surrounding sentence untouched. The obligation is that a sweep which refreshes a name has adopted the claim: re-read the proposition, and either confirm it against the current mechanism or mark it superseded. Updating the name alone is not the cheap half of the job, it is the half that manufactures the false attestation.
- **a witness that fails to COMPILE is ABSENT rather than red, and running its SUBJECT proves nothing about it** (a claim file is committed, reviewed and merged while it cannot compile, so none of its assertions ever execute and the guarantee it was written to carry does not exist; the class it polices reports as walled while nothing walls it, which is worse than an absent witness because the file is cited as coverage by its own author and by its reviewers). WHY IT IS A CLASS AND NOT A SLIP: every ordinary signal is compatible with the dead file and NONE OF THEM IS MALFUNCTIONING. It emits no advisories, because a file that does not compile contributes none. It fails no assertions, because none run. It is ABSENT from the floor disposition rather than failing in it, so a disposition read shows nothing to investigate. `cargo fmt` is green because the file is well-formed text. The push is green. And a reviewer can APPROVE on a wholly correct reading of the source, because READING DOES NOT COMPILE. THE CORE IS A TWO-CLAIM COLLAPSE: `the compiler behaves correctly` and `the witness that enrols that behaviour as executing evidence works` are DIFFERENT CLAIMS, and running the subject as a direct fixture establishes only the first. The mechanism of the misread is that holding the stronger claim's output makes the weaker one feel already answered — the author has the passing fixtures in hand, which is exactly why the file meant to carry them never gets checked. RECOGNITION RULE: ask what would have caught it, and IF THE ONLY ANSWER IS A RUN AIMED AT A DIFFERENT QUESTION, the class has no dedicated detector. The specimen surfaced from a whole-tree advisory census re-derivation chasing an unrelated hypothesis about a denominator, as blocking error number one, after the arms were green, the PR was open and an APPROVE was already recorded. THE ONE INSTRUMENT THAT SEES IT is a whole-tree compile INCLUDING the test roots; compiling the subject, or entry-scoping to the witness itself, does not. Rung found at 1, mitigatable, held only by an unrelated run happening to range over the file, which is not a mechanism. CEILING 3, structurally guaranteed: the population is decidable and closed — every file under the claim roots — so a required step that compiles them and refuses a non-compiling claim file makes the state unwritable in an `Accepted` tree. NEXT TRIGGER, capability and not artifact: the required floor must COMPILE every claim-root file and refuse a claim file that does not compile, rather than only executing the claim files that compile — AN EXECUTION ROSTER KEYED TO COMPILING FILES CANNOT SEE A FILE THAT FELL OUT OF IT, the same shape as a check whose population IS its own roster. Specimen gunbc#10337: `brand_nominal_identity_witness_test` named a host builtin in its import list; four direct fixtures had already proven the compiler refuses, and the witness enrolling them had never run. 
- **a step's INSTRUCTION and the TREE it operates on are resolved from different revisions** (INVALID STATE: the command a step runs -- which entry point, which function, which path -- is taken from revision A, while the working tree that command is pointed at is checked out at revision B, and nothing in the step binds the two or reports which is which. HARM: the step's refusal is REAL, typed and located, and it names a subject that never carried the instruction. Section 5's located diagnostic is intact and points at the wrong tree, so the repair it invites -- edit the module the error names -- is correct-looking and cannot work, because that module is not what disagreed. THIS IS NOT A STALE-BASE PROBLEM AND CALLING IT ONE SENDS THE FIX TO THE WRONG PLACE. Age of the base is neither necessary nor sufficient: a branch whose instruction and tree both come from the OLD revision is perfectly consistent and refuses nothing, and a branch that integrates the new revision is consistent again. What produces the failure is the SPLIT, and a step can hold it open at any base age. The reason it presents as staleness is that the split is invisible while the two revisions happen to agree, which is most of the time. **SPECIMEN, gunbc#10479, run 33926616205 job 101196425514 (2026-09-04).** The heal-generated-artifacts job refused with NoSuchFunction { name: heal_repair_declaration } while evaluating dag/gunbc/instruments/generated_artifact_gate.dag. The function and the invocation of it landed together in ONE commit, 865fddf03d (#10373) -- .dag definition and workflow argv in the same diff -- so the pair is atomic everywhere and no asymmetric merge resolution is involved. The split is the job's own construction: for a pull_request event GitHub supplies the WORKFLOW FILE from the merge ref, and this job declares ref: github.head_ref because it PUSHES the regeneration back to the branch and therefore cannot run detached on refs/pull/N/merge. Its checkout line reads git checkout --progress --force -B <branch> refs/remotes/origin/<branch>, not a merge ref. Measured at three revisions: branch head a86d7ed225 had definition 0 and invocation 0, a consistent OLD pair; origin/main had 1 and 1, a consistent NEW pair; what EXECUTED was the invocation from the merge ref against the definition from the branch head. git merge-base --is-ancestor 865fddf03d a86d7ed225 is false. Every other required job runs the merge ref and stayed green, which is why exactly one job reddened and why integrating main cleared it with nothing resolved by hand. **AND THE FIRST ACCOUNT OF THIS SPECIMEN WAS WRONG IN THE INSTRUCTIVE DIRECTION, which is why the population statement is written twice here.** It was first filed as any branch based before 865fddf03d will red this way -- a rule keyed on the BASE. A reviewer then established that both halves landed atomically and concluded the opposite, that a consistent old pair cannot refuse and so the cause must be an asymmetric merge resolution that took one side. Both readings check one ref and infer the other. The measurement that decides it is the checkout line in the job log, and neither account had read it. The population is: the branch HEAD predates the commit that added the invocation, AND the entry point is reached by a step that checks out the head rather than the merge ref -- narrower than base age in one direction, and broader than a bad merge resolution in the other. **SECOND SPECIMEN, gunbc#10490, AND IT IS A MATCHED PAIR RATHER THAN A SECOND SIGHTING.** Reached independently by another lane on a case neither party to the first diagnosis chose, and diagnosed correctly there before this row existed -- which is the evidence the roster is for: the class is recognisable in the wild, not only in retrospect. Re-measured here rather than accepted as reported. RED ARM, head c30c049f68: the definition is absent (git merge-base --is-ancestor 865fddf03d c30c049f68 is false), and on run 33930364431 heal-generated-artifacts failed while required-witnesses-build, required-witnesses-floor and the aggregator all succeeded -- one job red, and it is the one job whose ref differs from the workflow file it is running. GREEN ARM, head 5888fb0106 after integrating main: the definition is present. The property flips with the remedy, which makes the pair discriminating rather than illustrative, and per section 4b(4) it stays enrolled as the arm that would catch a regression in the eventual fix. ONE HONEST GAP IN THAT PAIR: at the time of writing, #10490 green run 33932538855 had not concluded, so the executed green arm is gunbc#10479 at 245416762b, where heal passed in 18m56s after exactly this remedy. Naming #10490 as an executed positive control before its run reports would be the unbacked-execution-claim failure inside the row that files a class about misattributed evidence. **SCOPE, STATED BECAUSE THE CLASS IS WRITTEN WIDER THAN ITS EVIDENCE.** The general shape is a step whose checkout ref differs from the ref that supplied the workflow file, and it is written at that grain deliberately: the next instance will be whichever step next needs to write back to the branch. But BOTH executed specimens are heal-generated-artifacts, which is the only such step in the required workflow today, so the evidence covers one job and the generalisation is reasoning. A reader deciding whether their own red is this class should check their step's ref: declaration, not assume the shape has been observed anywhere else. **DISTINCT FROM ITS THREE NEAREST NEIGHBOURS, and the repair law is what separates them.** upstream_carrier_substituted_for_the_consumer_selected_subject is about deriving a CLAIM about a consumer from a carrier the consumer transforms; there the defect is in a measurement and the repair is to measure the composed subject. Here nothing is measured -- the EXECUTOR pairs the two revisions itself, and the repair is in the step. verification_bound_to_the_revision_it_started_on is one tree MOVING across an interval; here the two revisions are fixed for the whole run and differ by construction, so waiting or re-running changes nothing. receipt_names_a_property_not_the_tree_it_holds_of is an expired GREEN that produces no signal at all; this class produces a loud, correctly typed RED whose subject attribution is false, which is a different failure to catch and a different one to trust. **RUNG: 1 (mitigatable).** The line stops, the diagnostic is typed and located, and the harm is bounded to a misattributed red plus the investigation it misdirects -- there is no silent wrongness here. **CEILING: 3 (structurally guaranteed), and not 4, for a stated reason:** which ref supplies a workflow file is GitHub's decision and sits outside the modeled guarantee, so no constructor of ours can make the two-ref pairing unwritable. What IS reachable is that a step cannot emit an unattributed refusal: the invocation is resolved against the tree the step checked out, and an entry that tree does not define refuses by naming BOTH revisions and classifying the cause as an instruction/subject split rather than as a defect in the module it names. **NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, a capability and not an artifact: a step whose entry point is resolved from the revision it checked out, with the refusal carrying both revision identities.** What does NOT satisfy it: making this one job check out the merge ref, which it cannot do and still push; renaming or restoring the function; or adding a note telling readers to merge main, which is guidance rather than a mechanism. **RECOGNITION RULE: when a job refuses naming an entry point, function or path that is absent from the branch, ask which revision supplied the COMMAND and which supplied the TREE, and demand both as immutable shas.** The tell is that the refusal's own subject has no history on the branch at all -- git log -S on the branch is empty -- while git show <default-branch>:<workflow file> contains the invocation. Two refs, one of them never named in the error. 
- **a required check reaches a terminal refusal on the head and the same head is then landed by hand, so the check detects the invalid state without being a precondition of landing.** INVALID STATE: a committed emitted mirror differs from what its authority emits; the required comparator enumerates that mirror, observes the differing bytes, and reports failure before the merge, yet the merge completes. This is an admission failure after detection, not an inert or mis-scoped detector. HARM: hand-maintained projections reach main in either direction -- bytes the authority emits can disappear and bytes it does not emit can appear -- while every later branch inherits a red required lane. A separate actuator may repeatedly report green over its own population, making the repository look converged without touching the drifted mirrors. MEASURED SPECIMEN (gunbc#10273, merged as 5a12090e7a, 2026-09-04). The pull request hand-maintained two files in `v1_compiler.emitted_population`: it removed the 47-line rustc-judged `nested_refinement_cast_fixture_closure_discrimination` pair from `compiler_tests.rs` while emission still produced it, and added 39 lines to `std_measure.rs` that emission did not produce. The required build run 33908874519 executed `claim_executor --required-ci --required-lane build`, reported `planned=156 executed=156 adjudicated=156`, and at 19:17:52Z printed `required-ci: regen FAIL generated surface drift: compiler_tests.rs, std_measure.rs`; its aggregate required `witnesses` check concluded FAILURE at 19:30:00Z. The human merge occurred at 19:59:28Z. Thus a terminal refusal existed for twenty-nine minutes before landing. A second pull-request run was created at 19:59:46Z, after the merge, and is not the evidence this receipt rests on. THE SPECIMEN IS AN INSTANCE OF A MEASURED RATE, NOT AN OUTLIER. Reproduced on 2026-09-04 over the 25 most recently merged pull requests, all merged that same day: for each pull request, join its merged head SHA and `mergedAt` to that commit's check runs, retain `name == witnesses` with `completed_at < mergedAt`, and take the latest by completion time. The closed tally was 13 success, 9 failure, 3 undetermined, total 25. Thus 9 of 25 landed after a terminal red on that exact head, and 12 of 25 landed without an established green from this instrument. THE THREE ARE `undetermined`, NOT VIOLATIONS: no check named `witnesses` completed before their merge, but check names date a receipt and this census did not establish what an earlier roster called the executing check. The population is ONE DAY of merges and supports no extrapolation beyond those 25. The query was controlled first on gunbc#10273 and returned its known `failure` at 19:30:00Z; this matters because an earlier instrument passed an unsupported second `--arg` to `gh api --jq`, suppressed the resulting errors, and rendered every failure as an empty result. FOLLOW-UP RECEIPT, INDEPENDENTLY MEASURED 2026-09-05T03:58:38Z (gunbc#10500). This is the `undetermined` bucket, not another terminal refusal crossed and not an addition to the earlier day's census. `gh pr view 10500 --repo gunb-ai/gunbc --json mergedAt,headRefOid,state` identifies the merged head as `5c55457118814759467fe21c35419746ee920ba7` and the merge instant as 2026-09-05T03:49:17Z. Querying `repos/gunb-ai/gunbc/commits/5c55457118814759467fe21c35419746ee920ba7/check-runs?per_page=100&filter=all` returned `total_count=3`, checked nonzero and equal to the enumerated population before counting conclusions: `heal-generated-artifacts` (101243152637) started at 03:46:49Z, `required-witnesses-build` (101243152749) at 03:47:26Z, and `required-witnesses-floor` (101243152786) at 03:47:37Z, all on 2026-09-05 and all before the merge. At this later observation every `completed_at` and `conclusion` was null and every status was `in_progress`; no aggregate check-run named `witnesses` existed. The exact-head actions-runs query returned the `witnesses` pull_request workflow run 33942442212, created at 03:39:06Z and still in_progress. Thus the observed checks had begun before landing and remained nonterminal after it; no terminal verdict was available at the merge instant. A subsequent reading found `heal-generated-artifacts` completed successfully at 2026-09-05T04:04:43Z, strictly after `mergedAt`; the build and floor checks still had null completion fields. For those unfinished checks this receipt retains the dated null-completion observation rather than inventing a later verdict; when completion is reported, compare `completed_at` to `mergedAt`, not the current status label. Use the full head SHA and require a successful API response with a nonempty, fully enumerated population: an error or an empty result is not evidence of no refusal. Independently reading #10469's `mergedAt` gives 2026-09-05T02:18:23Z, placing this landing 1 hour 30 minutes 54 seconds after the row landed. The program that filed the row then populated its predicted undetermined bucket with its own PR while the row was live on main. Documentation had not made a terminal verdict a precondition of that landing path. This establishes neither a terminal-refusal violation of this row's primary subject nor that the merge was wrong, and attributes no actor or admission mechanism. DISTINGUISHING FACTS. `compiler_tests.rs` and `std_measure.rs` are members of `v1_compiler.emitted_population`, whose required regen phase compared all 156 planned mirrors and named both paths. They are not members of the distinct `gunbc.generated_artifact` `generated_artifact_registry`: the heal run on the same head reported `HealNoChange`, while the required generated-artifact phase reported `rostered=38 adjudicated=38 matches=38 drifted=0`. Heal runs `tools.generated_artifact_gate` `main_wet` and stages that 38-artifact registry; it does not run or install the 156-mirror required-regen candidate. Its green was true of its own population and could not converge either file. THE CONTENT WITNESS IS NOT THE MIRROR GATE. `nested_refinement_cast_fixture_closure_discrimination` is `#[ignore]`, and cargo test is on no CI step, so no executing merge-path gate covered what the deleted test asserts. The required regen phase nevertheless covered the containing mirror byte-for-byte and went red. Saying the test did not execute therefore explains why its semantics were not independently exercised; it does not explain or weaken the detected mirror drift. DISTINCT FROM `head_landed_by_hand_before_its_own_verification_reported`: there the landing precedes the head's terminal verdict, so improving detector speed cannot help and the record cannot say whether a reported refusal would have blocked. Here the required verdict was terminal and red twenty-nine minutes before the landing. Waiting longer was impossible as a repair because the answer had already arrived. Distinct from `green_reported_over_a_population_the_instrument_does_not_own`: that row concerns a green being read beyond its denominator. Heal exhibits that neighbouring shape here, but the landing defect is established by the red comparator that did own both files. RECOGNITION RULE: join the required check's subject SHA, conclusion time and conclusion to the landing's subject SHA and merge time. If the same head has a terminal required refusal before `merged_at`, yet lands, the detector is neither absent, inert nor late; the landing path did not make its refusal a precondition. Then enumerate every actuator separately before asking why a later green did not repair the state -- shared words such as generated and regen do not make two populations one. RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable for admission. The comparator mechanically exposed the mirror drift at rung 2, but this row's subject is the consumer of that refusal: admission did not prevent the landing, and repair depended on a later author noticing the red residual and installing a full candidate. A correct detector whose refusal has no accountable consumer is observation, not rung 2 prevention on the landing path. This subsumes the otherwise-separate class `gate_refusal_has_no_accountable_consumer`: the unconsumed refusal is the mechanism established here, so a second row would give one class two authorities. CEILING: outside the modeled guarantee on the human hosting-platform path, and structurally guaranteed on a landing path this repository constructs; the class takes the weaker boundary honestly. The repository can model a landing whose accepted constructor requires a terminal success for the exact ref, but a person pressing the hosting platform's merge control is an external boundary until that path is removed or made to consume the modeled decision. Calling the existing required context a structural guarantee would contradict the specimen: its refusal was present and the external action still occurred. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, the same capability already named by `gunbc.guarantee_stall` `merge_admission_terminal_verdict_stall`: a landing path in which terminal SUCCESS for the exact ref that lands is a precondition, sufficient to refuse both absent evidence and an observed FAILURE, human actor included; until that exists, a landing observation that joins every merged head to the terminal verdict present at its merge instant and reports this class explicitly. Adding another comparator, widening heal, or speeding the existing run does not discharge it: the owned comparator already returned the right answer in time.)
- **corroboration without an independent derivation axis** (a second reader reaches the same conclusion as the first, and the agreement is counted as confirmation -- but both readings ran the same query over the same lexical projection of the same subject, so the second signature carries no information the first did not. TWO SIGNATURES, ONE EVIDENCE LINEAGE. What makes it dangerous is that it looks exactly like independent replication from the outside, and it is REPORTED as replication: the second reader honestly says they checked. Specimen, measured 2026-09-04 on gunbc: two sessions independently concluded that HTTP listing pagination was modelled in this tree. Both read `extdeps.github.github` `default_per_page` and the `per_page` fields that consume it, and both substituted PAGE SIZE for CONTINUATION CLOSURE. The tree in fact carried no page parameter, no cursor advance, no Link-header handling and no page fold anywhere, so every listing read in the repository was capped at its first page -- and `extdeps.github.github` declared a `Pagination` type with `per_page`, `cursor` and `has_next` fields that NOTHING consumed, which is a shape with no execution behind it. **THE THREE CLAIMS THE SUBJECT ACTUALLY CARRIED, and conflating them is the mechanism rather than an incidental error: page SIZE (how many items one request asks for), cursor SHAPE (how the next request is named), and continuation CLOSURE (whether the sequence of requests was consumed until the authority said there was no next).** Only the first appears lexically in a request declaration. The third appears nowhere unless a fold exists, so a lexical reading can only ever return evidence about the first, and both readings returned it. **Recognition rule: when a second reading agrees, ask which DERIVATION AXIS it changed.** If both readings are the same query over the same projection, the agreement is one observation reported twice. A reading corroborates only when it moves an axis -- operation-graph reachability rather than token presence, an EXECUTING transition rather than a declaration, or a controlled multi-page mutation rather than a read of the shape that would serve one.
